Hence $\mathbb E_y\mathrm{Up}\le\mathrm{Bnd}(g):=\frac12(C_2N^kJ_{2k}(g)+N^{-k}M)$.  Integrability in $y$ is Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:gibbs-pow-integrable} (for $A^{4k}$) and Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:thermal-factor-moment} (for $B^{4k}$).
Finally $\mathbb E_gJ_{2k}\le K'/N^{2k}$ by the uniform form of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:overlap-moments-all} (with $K'=K_0(\beta_0,2k)$), so $\mathbb E_g\mathrm{Bnd}\le\frac12(C_2K'N^{-k}+MN^{-k})$.  Formalized in \texttt{SpinGlasses/SKGaussianCorollaries.lean}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The Gaussian fluctuation principle with a multiplier]
\label{thm:sg-tal:mul-exp}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:cor-kernel, lem:sg-tal:double-dominated}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_gaussian_fluctuation_mul_exp}
  \leanok
\talsource{the common core of Eqs.~(1.206) and~(1.207); the book proves them in parallel}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, $k\ge1$, $C_j\ge0$ ($j\le8k$), $c\ge0$.  There is $K>0$, depending only on these data, such that for every $\beta$ with $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, every $h$, every fixed point $q$ at $\beta$, every $\varepsilon$ with $\varepsilon^2=1$, every exponentially tame $U$ with $|U^{(j)}(x)|\le C_je^{c|x|}$ ($j\le8k$) and every $N\ge1$,
\[
\mathbb E\Bigl(\Bigl(\bigl\langle U(\dot S)\bigr\rangle-\mathbb E_\xi U(\xi\sqrt{1-q})\Bigr)\exp\Bigl(\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle\Bigr)\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{N^k}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:double-dominated} with $F=(AB)^{2k}\ge0$ (an even power) and the kernel of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cor-kernel}; $K=\frac12(C_2K'+M)+1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Corollary~1.7.13: Eqs.~(1.206) and~(1.207)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cor-1713}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:mul-exp, lem:sg-tal:exptame-instances, lem:sg-tal:exp-gauss-averages, lem:sg-tal:ising-split}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_gaussian_fluctuation_exp,
        SpinGlasses.sk_gaussian_fluctuation_id_mul_exp}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.7.13, Eqs.~(1.206) and (1.207)}
Let $0\le\beta_0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $k\ge1$.  There is $K>0$, depending only on $\beta_0$ and $k$, such that for every $\beta$ with $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, every $h$, every fixed point $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, every $\varepsilon=\pm1$ and every $N\ge1$, writing $\langle\cdot\rangle$ for the SK Gibbs measure and $\dot S=N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\dot\sigma_i$,
\begin{align*}
&\mathbb E\Bigl(\Bigl\langle e^{\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\sigma_i}\Bigr\rangle-e^{\beta^2(1-q)/2}\,e^{\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle}\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{N^k},\tag{1.206}\\
&\mathbb E\Bigl(\Bigl\langle\dot S\,e^{\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\sigma_i}\Bigr\rangle-\varepsilon\beta(1-q)\,e^{\beta^2(1-q)/2}\,e^{\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle}\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{N^k}.\tag{1.207}
\end{align*}
(The two inequalities have different constants $K$; the statement is for each.)  In particular $K$ does not depend on $\beta$, $h$ or $q$, which is the book's ``$K$ stays bounded with $\beta\le\beta_0$''.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:mul-exp} with $U(x)=e^{\varepsilon\beta x}$, resp.\ $U(x)=xe^{\varepsilon\beta x}$, using the bounds of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:exptame-instances} with $B=\beta_0$ (so $C_j=\beta_0^j$, $c=\beta_0$, resp.\ $C_j=2(j\beta_0^{j-1}+\beta_0^j)$, $c=\beta_0+1$), which hold for all $|\beta|\le\beta_0$ since $|\varepsilon\beta|=|\beta|$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:exp-gauss-averages}, $\mathbb E_\xi U(\xi\sqrt{1-q})$ equals $e^{\beta^2(1-q)/2}$, resp.\ $\varepsilon\beta(1-q)e^{\beta^2(1-q)/2}$.
By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:ising-split} the Gibbs average $\langle e^{\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum y_i\sigma_i}\rangle$ equals $\langle e^{\varepsilon\beta\dot S}\rangle\cdot B$ (resp.\ with the extra factor $\dot S$), so the difference in the statement is $\bigl(\langle U(\dot S)\rangle-\mathbb E_\xi U(\xi\sqrt{1-q})\bigr)B$, which is the quantity of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:mul-exp}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Deviations from the book in Corollary~1.7.13]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-1713}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{No Cauchy--Schwarz; Theorem~1.7.11 is not quoted as a black box.}  The book applies (1.197) with $2k$ replaced by $4k$ to get $\mathbb EA^{4k}\le K/N^{2k}$, bounds $\mathbb EB^{4k}$ by (A.6), and concludes with
  $\mathbb E(AB)^{2k}\le(\mathbb EA^{4k}\,\mathbb EB^{4k})^{1/2}$.  The formalization instead multiplies out \emph{pointwise}: $(AB)^{2k}\le\frac12(N^kA^{4k}+N^{-k}B^{4k})$, integrates first in $y$ and then in $g$, and so needs the
  \emph{disorder-by-disorder} bound $\mathbb E_yA^{4k}\le C_2J_{2k}(g)$ that is an intermediate step in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:general-fluct}, rather than its final statement.  The reason is that Cauchy--Schwarz for the product of two functions of $(g,y)$ would
  require carrying integrability of $A^{4k}$ and $B^{4k}$ on the product space; the pointwise inequality isolates the two factors, and each is integrated against the measure that suits it.  The resulting rate $N^{-k}$ is the same.
\item \emph{Uniformity in $\beta\le\beta_0$.}  The book adds ``$K$ stays bounded with $\beta\le\beta_0$ \dots all the estimates are uniform over that range'' without proof.  Here the uniformity is built into the statements: the constants come from three places, each made explicit.  (i) The Taylor step (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:gibbs-taylor}) depends on the observable $U$ only through bounds $|U^{(j)}|\le C_je^{c|x|}$ on its derivatives, which for $e^{\varepsilon\beta x}$ and $xe^{\varepsilon\beta x}$ are monotone in $|\beta|$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:exptame-instances}).  (ii) The overlap moments (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:overlap-moments-all}) are bounded with $\beta_0$ in place of $\beta$.  (iii) $\mathbb E_yB^{4k}\le e^{8k^2\beta^2}\le e^{8k^2\beta_0^2}$.  The fixed point $q$ is a hypothesis, not a constant, and enters only through $q\in[0,1]$.  The constant is even uniform in $h$ for (1.206)--(1.207); for Corollary~1.7.15 it depends on $h$ through $\cosh h$.  This uniformity is what the application to the $(N-1)$-spin system at $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{1-1/N}$ needs, since $\beta_-$ moves with $N$.
\item \emph{The multiplier form.}  Both (1.206) and (1.207) are instances of one theorem for an arbitrary tame $U$; the book proves them in parallel.
\item \emph{Integrability is proved.}  Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:gibbs-pow-integrable} and the integrability in Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:thermal-factor-moment} make explicit the integrability on which the book's expectations silently rely.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\subsection{Lemma~1.7.14 and Corollary~1.7.15: the cavity field}
\label{sec:sg-tal:fluct-cavity-field}

\emph{Content.}  The exponentials of Corollary~1.7.13 are the building blocks of the quantities that appear when the last spin of the $N$-spin system is removed (Lemma~1.7.4): there the Gibbs average of $\sigma_N$ is a ratio
$\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E\rangle/\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E\rangle$ with $\mathcal E=\exp(\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\sigma_i+\varepsilon h)$ and $\mathrm{Av}$ the average over $\varepsilon=\pm1$.
Since $\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E=\cosh(X+h)$ and $\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E=\sinh(X+h)$ for $X=\beta N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i\sigma_i$, we state everything in terms of $\sinh$ and $\cosh$ of the exponent.
\emph{Uses:} Corollary~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1713}.  \emph{Status:} fully proved.

\begin{lemma}[Elementary hyperbolic estimates]
\label{lem:sg-tal:hyperbolic}
  \lean{Real.cosh_add_eq_exp,
        Real.sinh_add_eq_exp,
        Real.exp_le_two_cosh,
        Real.exp_neg_le_two_cosh,
        Real.abs_exp_mul_add_exp_neg_mul_div_two_le,
        Real.abs_exp_mul_sub_exp_neg_mul_div_two_le,
        integral_sinh_add,
        integral_cosh_add,
        integral_mul_sinh_add,
        integral_mul_cosh_add,
        one_le_integral_cosh,
        abs_integral_sinh_le_integral_cosh,
        SpinGlasses.abs_sinh_cosh_diff_le,
        SpinGlasses.abs_id_mul_exp_cosh_sub_le,
        SpinGlasses.dotProduct_ising_neg,
        SpinGlasses.abs_beta_one_sub_q_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{the identities ``$\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E=\mathrm{ch}$, $\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E=\mathrm{sh}$'' used in the proof of Corollary~1.7.15}
For reals $u,h,D_1,D_2$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\cosh(u+h)=\frac12(e^he^u+e^{-h}e^{-u})$, $\sinh(u+h)=\frac12(e^he^u-e^{-h}e^{-u})$; for a probability measure on a finite set, $\int\cosh(u+h)=\frac12\bigl(e^h\!\int e^u+e^{-h}\!\int e^{-u}\bigr)$ and similarly for $\sinh$, and with any weight $\Psi$: $\int\Psi\cosh(u+h)=\frac12\bigl(e^h\!\int\Psi e^u+e^{-h}\!\int\Psi e^{-u}\bigr)$ (used with $\Psi=\dot S$ for Eq.~(1.210); the unweighted forms are the case $\Psi=1$);
\item $1\le\int\cosh(u+h)$ and $|\int\sinh(u+h)|\le\int\cosh(u+h)$;
\item $e^{\pm h}\le2\cosh h$ and $\bigl|\tfrac12(e^hD_1\pm e^{-h}D_2)\bigr|\le\cosh h\,(|D_1|+|D_2|)$;
\item if $A'-A=\frac12(e^hD_1-e^{-h}D_2)$ and $B'-B=\frac12(e^hD_1+e^{-h}D_2)$ then $|A'-A|+|B'-B|\le2\cosh h\,(|D_1|+|D_2|)$;
\item if $4\beta^2<1$ and $q\in[0,1]$ then $|\beta(1-q)|\le1$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(1) is $\cosh x=\frac12(e^x+e^{-x})$ and linearity of the integral.  (2) is $\cosh\ge1$ and $|\sinh x|\le\cosh x$, integrated.  (3) The first part is $\cosh h=\frac12(e^h+e^{-h})$; the second is the triangle inequality and $e^{\pm h}\le2\cosh h$.
(4) Apply (3) with both signs and add.  (5) $4\beta^2<1$ gives $|\beta|<\frac12\le1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma~1.7.14: a ratio comparison]
\label{lem:sg-tal:ratio}
  \lean{abs_div_sub_div_le,
        abs_div_sub_div_le_of_denom_ge}
  \leanok
\talsource{Lemma~1.7.14}
(i) If $|A'|\le B'$, $B'>0$ and $B\ge1$ then $\bigl|\frac{A'}{B'}-\frac AB\bigr|\le|A-A'|+|B-B'|$.
(ii) If $B',B>0$ and $|A/B|\le C$ then $\bigl|\frac{A'}{B'}-\frac AB\bigr|\le\frac1{B'}\bigl(|A'-A|+C\,|B'-B|\bigr)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(i) is the book's proof: $\frac{A'}{B'}-\frac AB=\frac{A'}{B'}\cdot\frac{B-B'}{B}+\frac1B(A'-A)$, then $|A'|/B'\le1$ and $1/B\le1$.  (The book omits that $B'>0$ is needed; with $|A'|\le B'$ it is automatic that $B'\ge0$, and we assume $B'>0$ to divide.)
(ii) is not in the book: $\frac{A'}{B'}-\frac AB=\frac{A'-A}{B'}-\frac AB\cdot\frac{B'-B}{B'}$.  It is the form that is needed when the numerator $A'$ is \emph{not} dominated by the denominator $B'$ (as for Eq.~(1.210) below) and the target quotient $A/B$ is bounded instead.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Corollary~1.7.15, Eq.~(1.209): the cavity field]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:cor-1713, lem:sg-tal:ratio, lem:sg-tal:hyperbolic, lem:sg-tal:cor-kernel}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_field_tanh_approx}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.7.15, Eq.~(1.209)}
Let $0\le\beta_0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$, $h\in\mathbb R$ and $k\ge1$.  There is $K>0$, depending only on $\beta_0,h,k$, such that for every $\beta$ with $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ and all $N\ge1$,
\[
\mathbb E\Bigl(\frac{\langle\sinh(X+h)\rangle}{\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle}-\tanh\Bigl(\frac\beta{\sqrt N}\sum_{i\le N}y_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle+h\Bigr)\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{N^k},\qquad X=\frac\beta{\sqrt N}\sum_{i\le N}y_i\sigma_i .
\]
In the book's notation the left quotient is $\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E\rangle/\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E\rangle$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Fix $g$ and $y$ and put $w=\frac\beta{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle$, $E=e^{\beta^2(1-q)/2}\ge1$, $A'=\langle\sinh(X+h)\rangle$, $B'=\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle$, $A=\sinh(w+h)E$, $B=\cosh(w+h)E$, and for $\varepsilon=\pm1$
\[
D_\varepsilon=\bigl\langle e^{\varepsilon X}\bigr\rangle-E\,e^{\varepsilon w}
\]
(the quantity of Eq.~(1.206); $D_1,D_{-1}$ are $D_1,D_2$ below).  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:hyperbolic}(1), $A'-A=\frac12(e^hD_1-e^{-h}D_{-1})$ and $B'-B=\frac12(e^hD_1+e^{-h}D_{-1})$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:ratio}(i) applies: $|A'|\le B'$, $B'\ge1>0$ and $B=\cosh(w+h)E\ge1\cdot1$, \emph{because $E\ge1$}.  Since $A/B=\tanh(w+h)$,
\[
\bigl|\tfrac{A'}{B'}-\tanh(w+h)\bigr|\le|A'-A|+|B'-B|\le2\cosh h\,(|D_1|+|D_{-1}|).
\]
Raise to the power $2k$ and use $(x+y)^{2k}\le2^{2k-1}(x^{2k}+y^{2k})$: the left side of the theorem is bounded by $(2\cosh h)^{2k}2^{2k-1}(D_1^{2k}+D_{-1}^{2k})$.  Each $D_{\pm1}^{2k}$ is dominated by the kernel $\mathrm{Up}_{\pm1}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cor-kernel} with $\varepsilon=\pm1$ and $U=e^{\pm\beta x}$
(by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:ising-split}, $D_\varepsilon=(\langle e^{\varepsilon\beta\dot S}\rangle-E)B_\varepsilon$), so the claim follows by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:double-dominated} and the bounds on $\mathbb E_g\mathrm{Bnd}_{\pm1}$.  The constants of the two kernels (families $e^{\pm\beta x}$, $|\beta|\le\beta_0$) are those of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cor-kernel} for $C_j=\beta_0^j$, $c=\beta_0$, so $K=(2\cosh h)^{2k}2^{2k-1}(A_++A_-)+1$, with $A_\pm=\frac12(C_2K'+M)$ the two kernel constants, does not depend on $\beta$ or $q$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Corollary~1.7.15, Eq.~(1.210): the reaction term]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:cor-1713, lem:sg-tal:ratio, lem:sg-tal:hyperbolic, lem:sg-tal:cor-kernel, thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_field_reaction_approx}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.7.15, Eq.~(1.210), in the form displayed just before the last identity of its proof}
With $\dot S=\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\dot\sigma_i$ and the other notation and quantifiers as in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh} ($K$ depends only on $\beta_0,h,k$; $|\beta|\le\beta_0$ and $q$ a fixed point at $\beta$ are arbitrary),
\[
\mathbb E\Bigl(\frac{\langle\dot S\cosh(X+h)\rangle}{\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle}-\beta(1-q)\,\frac{\langle\sinh(X+h)\rangle}{\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle}\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{N^k}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Let $A''=\langle\dot S\cosh(X+h)\rangle$, $A_{\mathrm{tgt}}=\beta(1-q)E\sinh(w+h)$ and $D_\varepsilon'=\langle\dot Se^{\varepsilon X}\rangle-\varepsilon\beta(1-q)E\,e^{\varepsilon w}$ (the quantity of Eq.~(1.207)).  As before,
$A''-A_{\mathrm{tgt}}=\frac12(e^hD_1'+e^{-h}D_{-1}')$ and $B'-B=\frac12(e^hD_1+e^{-h}D_{-1})$.  Now $|A''|\le B'$ is \emph{false} in general, so Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:ratio}(i) is not available;
we use (ii) with the \emph{target} quotient $A_{\mathrm{tgt}}/B=\beta(1-q)\tanh(w+h)$, whose absolute value is at most $C=1$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:hyperbolic}(5):
\[
\Bigl|\frac{A''}{B'}-\beta(1-q)\tanh(w+h)\Bigr|\le\frac1{B'}\bigl(|A''-A_{\mathrm{tgt}}|+|B'-B|\bigr)\le\cosh h\,(|D_1'|+|D_{-1}'|+|D_1|+|D_{-1}|),
\]
using $B'\ge1$.  The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh} gives $|\frac{A'}{B'}-\tanh(w+h)|\le2\cosh h(|D_1|+|D_{-1}|)$, and $|\beta(1-q)|\le1$, so
\[
\Bigl|\frac{A''}{B'}-\beta(1-q)\frac{A'}{B'}\Bigr|\le3\cosh h\,(|D_1'|+|D_{-1}'|+|D_1|+|D_{-1}|).
\]
Raising to the power $2k$ with $(x_1+x_2+x_3+x_4)^{2k}\le4^{2k-1}\sum x_i^{2k}$ and using the kernels of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cor-kernel} for $U=e^{\pm\beta x}$ (the unprimed $D$'s) and $U=xe^{\pm\beta x}$ (the primed ones) concludes as in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh}, with the constants of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:exptame-instances} for $B=\beta_0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gibbs covariance of a linear statistic]
\label{lem:sg-tal:centered-linear}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_dotProduct_centered_mul, SpinGlasses.reaction_form_alg}
  \leanok
Let $\mu$ be a finite measure on a finite set, $\xi_i$ real functions, $w$ a real function, $c\in\mathbb R$ and $y\in\mathbb R^N$. Then
$\int\bigl(\sum_ic\,(\xi_i-\langle\xi_i\rangle)y_i\bigr)\,w\,d\mu=c\sum_iy_i\bigl(\langle\xi_iw\rangle-\langle\xi_i\rangle\langle w\rangle\bigr)$, where $\langle f\rangle=\int f\,d\mu$.  Moreover, for $D\neq0$, $c\sum_iy_i\frac{S_i}{D}-b\frac{s}{D}-c\sum_iy_im_i=\frac{c\sum_iy_i(S_i-m_iD)}{D}-b\frac sD$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Expand the dot product, use linearity of the integral over the finite sum, and $\int(\xi_iw-\langle\xi_i\rangle w)\,d\mu=\langle\xi_iw\rangle-\langle\xi_i\rangle\langle w\rangle$.  The second identity is the termwise identity $y_i(S_i/D)-y_im_i=y_i(S_i-m_iD)/D$ summed over $i$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Corollary~1.7.15, Eq.~(1.210) in the book form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction-book}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction, lem:sg-tal:centered-linear}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_field_reaction_approx_book}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.7.15, Eq.~(1.210), last displayed form}
With the notation of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction} (so $K$ depends only on $\beta_0,h,k$),
\[
\mathbb E\Bigl(\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\frac{\langle\sigma_i\cosh(X+h)\rangle}{\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle}-\beta(1-q)\frac{\langle\sinh(X+h)\rangle}{\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle}-\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{N^k}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Take $\xi_i=\sigma_i$, $w=\cosh(X+h)$, $c=N^{-1/2}$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:centered-linear}: the numerator $\langle\dot S\cosh(X+h)\rangle$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction} equals $N^{-1/2}\sum_iy_i(\langle\sigma_iw\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle\langle w\rangle)$.  Dividing by $\langle w\rangle\ge1$ gives the integrand of the present statement, pointwise, so the bound is that of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Convexity of even powers]
\label{lem:sg-tal:convex-power}
  \lean{add_pow_two_mul_le,
        add_pow_four_two_mul_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{used silently throughout Section~1.7 (``by convexity'')}
For $k\ge1$ and reals $x,y,x_1,\dots,x_4$:
$(x+y)^{2k}\le2^{2k-1}(x^{2k}+y^{2k})$ and $(x_1+x_2+x_3+x_4)^{2k}\le4^{2k-1}\sum_{j\le4}x_j^{2k}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Reduce to $|x|+|y|$ with the triangle inequality and apply convexity of $t\mapsto t^{2k}$ (Jensen with two points); the second inequality is the first applied twice, $2^{2k-1}\cdot2^{2k-1}=4^{2k-1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Deviations from the book in Corollary~1.7.15]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-1715}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{$\sinh/\cosh$ instead of $\mathrm{Av}$.}  The book writes the two numerators and the denominator as $\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E\rangle$, $\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E\rangle$ with $\mathcal E=\exp(\varepsilon\beta N^{-1/2}\sum y_i\sigma_i+\varepsilon h)$.
  We state the theorems for the explicit functions $\sinh(X+h)$ and $\cosh(X+h)$ (which is what $\mathrm{Av}$ produces) to avoid introducing an average over $\varepsilon$ as a new object; the identification is Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:hyperbolic}(1).  It will be used in Lemma~1.7.4, where $\sigma_N$ appears through $\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E\rangle_-$.
\item \emph{Eq.~(1.210) is stated before its last rewriting.}  The book's (1.210) reads $\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\frac{\langle\sigma_i\mathrm{Av}\mathcal E\rangle}{\langle\mathrm{Av}\mathcal E\rangle}-\beta(1-q)\frac{\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E\rangle}{\langle\mathrm{Av}\mathcal E\rangle}-\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle$,
  and its proof ends by the linear identity $\frac{\langle\dot S\,\mathrm{Av}\mathcal E\rangle}{\langle\mathrm{Av}\mathcal E\rangle}=\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\frac{\langle\sigma_i\mathrm{Av}\mathcal E\rangle}{\langle\mathrm{Av}\mathcal E\rangle}-\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_iy_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle$.
  Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction} is the statement before that identity; the identity itself (linearity of the Gibbs average, $\langle\dot\sigma_i\cdot\rangle=\langle\sigma_i\cdot\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle\langle\cdot\rangle$) is not yet formalized and will be done where it is used.
\item \emph{Which ratio lemma.}  For (1.209) the book's Lemma~1.7.14 applies verbatim, and the requirement $B\ge1$ is met without the rescaling by $e^{\beta^2(1-q)/2}$ one might expect to need, because the comparison quotient has $B=\cosh(w+h)E\ge1$ (as $E\ge1$);
  the lower bound is only needed for the \emph{target} denominator.  For (1.210) the numerator is not dominated by the denominator, and the second form (ii) of the lemma is used, with the boundedness $|\beta(1-q)\tanh|\le1$ of the target.
\item \emph{Constants and uniformity.}  As in Corollary~1.7.13, $K$ is uniform in $|\beta|\le\beta_0$ and in the fixed point $q$; it depends on $h$ through the factor $\cosh h$ coming from Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:hyperbolic} (Remark~\ref{rem:sg-tal:dev-1713}).  Both statements are for a general number $N$ of spins and a general inverse temperature $\beta$ and are applied in Lemma~1.7.4 to the $(N-1)$-spin system at $\beta_-$, whose energy prefactor $\beta_-/\sqrt{N-1}$ equals $\beta/\sqrt N$.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\subsection{Lemma~1.7.4, Eq.~(1.185): the last spin is a $\tanh$ of the cavity field}
\label{sec:sg-tal:fluct-last-spin}

\emph{Content.}  Let the full system have $N+1$ spins and coupling $\beta$.  Summing out the last spin (Proposition~1.6.1) writes $\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle=\langle\sinh L\rangle_-/\langle\cosh L\rangle_-$, where $\langle\cdot\rangle_-$ is the Gibbs measure of the first $N$ spins with the \emph{old} couplings at $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$ and $L(\rho)=\beta(N+1)^{-1/2}\sum_{i\le N}g_{i,N+1}\rho_i+h$ is linear in the new couplings $y_i=g_{i,N+1}$.
Because $(\sqrt N)^{-1}\beta_-=(\sqrt{N+1})^{-1}\beta$, this is exactly the quotient $\langle\sinh(X+h)\rangle/\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle$ of Corollary~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh} for the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-$, and the disorder of the $(N+1)$-spin system is the product of the old disorder and $y$ (Proposition~\ref{lem:sg-tal:disorder-split}).
We index by $N+1$ rather than $N$ (the book's $N$ and $N-1$) to avoid subtraction of natural numbers.
\emph{Uses:} Corollary~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh}, the cavity formula of Proposition~1.6.1.  \emph{Status:} fully proved.

\begin{lemma}[The $\beta_-$ coefficient]
\label{lem:sg-tal:beta-minus}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_beta_minus_coefficient,
        SpinGlasses.dotProduct_sk_beta_minus,
        SpinGlasses.one_sub_inv_succ_eq_div}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.4 (the identity $\beta_-/\sqrt{N-1}=\beta/\sqrt N$)}
For $N\ge1$, $\beta\in\mathbb R$ and $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$: $N^{-1/2}\beta_-=(N+1)^{-1/2}\beta$; hence for all $y,a\in\mathbb R^N$, $\sum_iN^{-1/2}\beta_-a_iy_i=(N+1)^{-1/2}\beta\sum_iy_ia_i$.  Also $1-\frac1{N+1}=\frac N{N+1}$, so $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{1-1/(N+1)}$ is the parameter of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift} for $N+1$ spins.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
$\sqrt{N/(N+1)}=\sqrt N/\sqrt{N+1}$, then cancel.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Cavity splitting of the SK energy]
\label{lem:sg-tal:energy-cavity}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:beta-minus}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.skGibbsEnergy_succ_cavity}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.6, Eq.~(1.145) with the normalization of Section~1.7}
For $N\ge1$, $\beta,h\in\mathbb R$, $g:\mathrm{Pairs}(N+1)\to\mathbb R$ and $\sigma\in\{\pm1\}^{N+1}$, the energy of the $(N+1)$-spin system equals
$H_N^{(\beta_-,h)}(g|_{\mathrm{old}};\sigma|_{\le N})+\sigma_{N+1}\bigl(\sum_i N^{-1/2}\beta_-\sigma_iy_i+h\bigr)$ with $y_i=g_{i,N+1}$ and $g|_{\mathrm{old}}$ the couplings of the pairs inside $\{1,\dots,N\}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
This is the algebraic splitting \texttt{sk\_cavity\_energy\_snoc} with coefficient $c=\beta/\sqrt{N+1}$, together with Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:beta-minus} to identify $c$ with the coefficient of the $N$-spin energy at $\beta_-$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Splitting of the SK disorder]
\label{lem:sg-tal:disorder-split}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.skCavityDisorderEquiv,
        SpinGlasses.measurePreserving_skCavityDisorderEquiv,
        SpinGlasses.measurePreserving_sk_cavity_disorder,
        SpinGlasses.integral_comp_sk_cavity_disorder}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.6, Eq.~(1.146) (independence of the old and the new couplings)}
The map $g\mapsto(g|_{\mathrm{old}},y)$ with $y_i=g_{i,N+1}$ is a measurable equivalence from $\mathbb R^{\mathrm{Pairs}(N+1)}$ to $\mathbb R^{\mathrm{Pairs}(N)}\times\mathbb R^N$ carrying the product of standard Gaussians to the product of the two product Gaussians.  Consequently, for \emph{every} function $F$ of $(g_{\mathrm{old}},y)$ with values in a Banach space, $\int F(g|_{\mathrm{old}},y(g))\,d\gamma^{\mathrm{Pairs}(N+1)}=\int F\,d(\gamma^{\mathrm{Pairs}(N)}\otimes\gamma^N)$, with no measurability or integrability hypothesis.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The map is the composition of the reindexing along $\mathrm{Pairs}(N+1)\simeq\mathrm{Pairs}(N)\sqcup\{1,\dots,N\}$ and the splitting of a product over a disjoint union.  The statement about integrals is change of variables along a measure-preserving measurable equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Cavity formula for the last spin]
\label{lem:sg-tal:last-spin-ratio}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:energy-cavity}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_last_spin_eq_sinh_div_cosh}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.4 (the formula $\langle\sigma_N\rangle=\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\varepsilon\mathcal E\rangle_-/\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E\rangle_-$)}
For $N\ge1$ and every disorder $g$ of the $(N+1)$-spin system, $\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle=\langle\sinh(X+h)\rangle_-/\langle\cosh(X+h)\rangle_-$ with $X=\sum_iN^{-1/2}\beta_-y_i\rho_i$, $y_i=g_{i,N+1}$, and $\langle\cdot\rangle_-$ the Gibbs measure of the $N$-spin energy of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:energy-cavity}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Rewrite the energy by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:energy-cavity} and apply the finite cavity identity \texttt{integral\_count\_tilted\_fin\_snoc\_cavity\_units} to $f(\sigma)=\sigma_{N+1}$: the numerator is $(e^L-e^{-L})/2=\sinh L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.7.4, Eq.~(1.185)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-a}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:last-spin-ratio, lem:sg-tal:disorder-split, thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh, lem:sg-tal:beta-minus}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_last_spin_tanh_approx_pow,
        SpinGlasses.sk_last_spin_tanh_approx}
  \leanok
\talsource{Lemma~1.7.4, Eq.~(1.185) (Eq.~(1.212) in its proof), for the system of $N+1$ spins; the $L^{2k}$ form is the one Theorem~1.7.7 needs}
Let $\beta_0\ge0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$, $h\in\mathbb R$ and $k\ge1$.  There is $K>0$ such that for all $|\beta|\le\beta_0$ and $N\ge1$, with $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$,
\[
\mathbb E\Bigl(\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle-\tanh\Bigl(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{N+1}}\sum_{i\le N}g_{i,N+1}\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-+h\Bigr)\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{(N+1)^k},
\]
where $\langle\cdot\rangle$ is the Gibbs measure of the $(N+1)$-spin system with disorder $g$ and $\langle\cdot\rangle_-$ that of the first $N$ spins with the old couplings at $\beta_-$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:energy-cavity}).  $K$ depends only on $(\beta_0,h,k)$.  The case $k=1$ is stated separately (Eq.~(1.185)).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Pick a fixed point $q_-$ of the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-$ (it exists, Eq.~(1.74); the statement does not involve it) and note $|\beta_-|\le|\beta|\le\beta_0$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:last-spin-ratio} and Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:beta-minus} the integrand is $F(g|_{\mathrm{old}},y)$ for the $2k$-th power difference $F$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh} for the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-$.  By Proposition~\ref{lem:sg-tal:disorder-split} the left side is $\int F\,d(\gamma^{\mathrm{Pairs}(N)}\otimes\gamma^N)$, which, $F$ being nonnegative, is at most the iterated integral, hence at most $K_{1.7.15}/N^k\le2^kK_{1.7.15}/(N+1)^k$, because $N+1\le2N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Differences from the book in Lemma~1.7.4]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-174}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Indexing.}  The book states (1.185) for $N$ spins and applies (1.209) to the $(N-1)$-spin system; we write $N+1$ and $N$.  Constants are the same up to the factor $2^k$ from $1/N^k\le2^k/(N+1)^k$.  The $L^{2k}$ form is stated because Theorem~1.7.7 is an $L^{2k}$ statement.
\item \emph{No fixed point in the statement.}  The book's text speaks of the fixed point $q_-$ of the smaller system; the statement of (1.185) does not contain $q$ and neither does ours.  Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift} (relating $q$ and $q_-$) is needed only in the next steps (1.186) and Lemma~1.7.6, where the fixed point of the \emph{full} system enters.
\item \emph{Part of (1.185) only.}  The book's Lemma~1.7.4 also contains (1.186), proved in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-b}, and the Gaussian structure of the cavity field (Lemma~1.7.6), proved in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-176}.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\subsection{Lemma~1.7.4, Eq.~(1.186): adding a spin barely moves an old magnetization}
\label{sec:sg-tal:fluct-old-spin}

\emph{Content.}  Eq.~(1.186) says $\mathbb E(\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-)^2\le K/N$ for an old site $i$.  We prove it for the $(N+1)$-spin system, $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$, in the following steps, following the book's proof of (1.213).
By the cavity formula $\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-=\langle\dot\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_-/\langle\cosh L\rangle_-$ with $\langle\cosh L\rangle_-\ge1$, so it suffices to bound $\mathbb E\langle\dot\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_-^2$.
Writing the square with two replicas, the Gaussian average over the new couplings $y$ is explicit (the replacement for the book's Eq.~(A.6)): $\mathbb E_y\cosh L(a)\cosh L(b)=\tfrac12\bigl(\cosh(2h)e^{B(1+R_{ab})}+e^{B(1-R_{ab})}\bigr)$ with $B=N(\beta/\sqrt{N+1})^2\le\beta^2$ and $R$ the overlap.
This kernel is Lipschitz in $R$, the Gram entry $T=N^{-1}\sum_i\dot\sigma_i^1\dot\sigma_i^2$ has Gibbs mean zero (so the kernel may be centered at $q$), and $|T||R-q|\le\frac12(T^2+(R-q)^2)$, whose Gibbs average is bounded by the overlap moment through the Gram-moment bound of Part~E.
This bounds the \emph{sum over the sites}; site symmetry of the disorder (Proposition~1.4.14) makes all sites equal.
\emph{Uses:} Parts~E, G, J and Proposition~1.4.14.  \emph{Status:} fully proved.

\begin{lemma}[Cavity formula for an old magnetization]
\label{lem:sg-tal:old-spin-cavity}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_comp_castSucc_count_tilted_snoc,
        SpinGlasses.integral_comp_castSucc_sub_integral,
        SpinGlasses.sk_old_spin_deviation_eq}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.4: ``$\langle\sigma_1\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E\rangle/\langle\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E\rangle-\langle\sigma_1\rangle$ is $\langle\sigma_1\rangle-\langle\sigma_1\rangle_-$ by the cavity method''}
For any finite old system with energy $A$, last-spin field $L$ and observable $\varphi$ of the old spins, $\langle\varphi\rangle_{N+1}=\langle\varphi\cosh L\rangle_A/\langle\cosh L\rangle_A$, hence $\langle\varphi\rangle_{N+1}-\langle\varphi\rangle_A=\langle(\varphi-\langle\varphi\rangle_A)\cosh L\rangle_A/\langle\cosh L\rangle_A$.  For the SK system and $\varphi(\rho)=\rho_i$, $A$ is the energy at $\beta_-$ with the old couplings and $L(\rho)=\frac\beta{\sqrt{N+1}}\sum_jy_j\rho_j+h$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Apply the finite cavity identity of Proposition~1.6.1 to $f(\sigma)=\varphi(\sigma|_{\le N})$: both values of the last spin give the same $\varphi(\rho)$, so the numerator is $\varphi(\rho)(e^L+e^{-L})/2=\varphi(\rho)\cosh L$.  For the SK system use the energy splitting of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:energy-cavity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Two replicas of the cavity field and a Lipschitz kernel]
\label{lem:sg-tal:cavity-kernel}
  \lean{cosh_mul_cosh_sum_mul_eq,
        integrable_cosh_mul_cosh_sum_mul_gaussianReal,
        integral_cosh_mul_cosh_sum_mul_gaussianReal,
        Real.abs_exp_sub_exp_le_of_abs_le,
        SpinGlasses.sum_sq_cavity_add,
        SpinGlasses.sq_integral_gibbs_cosh,
        SpinGlasses.integrable_sq_integral_gibbs_cosh,
        SpinGlasses.integral_gibbs_cosh_sq,
        SpinGlasses.integral_gibbs_cosh_sq_centered,
        SpinGlasses.abs_cavityKernel_sub_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of (1.213): $\mathrm E\,\mathcal E_1\mathcal E_2$ and $\mathrm{Av}\,\mathcal E_1\mathcal E_2$, from (A.6)}
Let $\mu$ be a Gibbs measure on a finite set carrying observables $\xi_i=\pm1$ ($i\le N$), $y$ a standard Gaussian vector, $L_y(a)=c\sum_jy_j\xi_j(a)+h$, and $S(a,b)=\sum_i\xi_i(a)\xi_i(b)$.  For any function $g$,
\[
\mathbb E_y\Bigl(\int g\cosh L_y\,d\mu\Bigr)^2=\iint g(a)g(b)\,\frac{\cosh(2h)e^{c^2(N+S)}+e^{c^2(N-S)}}2\,d\mu(a)\,d\mu(b).
\]
With $B=Nc^2$ and $R=S/N$ the kernel is $x\mapsto\frac12(\cosh(2h)e^{B(1+x)}+e^{B(1-x)})$ at $x=R(a,b)$, and for $|x|,|q|\le1$ it moves by at most $\frac12Be^{2B}(\cosh(2h)+1)\,|x-q|$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Expand the square of the Gibbs average as a double sum, use $\cosh u\cosh v=\frac12(\cosh(u+v)+\cosh(u-v))$ and $\mathbb E\cosh(\sum c_ew_e+m)=\cosh m\,e^{\sum c_e^2/2}$ for independent standard Gaussians $w_e$, and $\xi_i^2=1$ to evaluate $\sum_i c^2(\xi_i(a)\pm\xi_i(b))^2/2=c^2(N\pm S)$.  The Lipschitz bound follows from $|e^u-e^v|\le e^M|u-v|$ for $|u|,|v|\le M$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The sum over the sites of the cavity deviation]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cavity-deviation-sum}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:cavity-kernel, thm:sg-tal:gram-moments}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sum_integral_gibbs_cosh_sq_le,
        SpinGlasses.sum_sum_weight_mul_gram_eq_zero,
        SpinGlasses.sum_weight_mul_sub_integral}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of (1.213): ``it suffices to show $|\mathrm E\langle\frac1N\sum_i\dot\sigma_i^1\dot\sigma_i^2f(R_{1,2})\rangle|\le K/N$''}
In the setting of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cavity-kernel}, for $q\in[0,1]$ and $\dot\xi_i=\xi_i-\langle\xi_i\rangle$,
\[
\sum_{i\le N}\mathbb E_y\Bigl(\int\dot\xi_i\cosh L_y\,d\mu\Bigr)^2\le N\Lambda\,\tfrac{17}2\,\mathbb E_\mu(R_{1,2}-q)^2,\qquad\Lambda=\tfrac12Be^{2B}(\cosh(2h)+1).
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cavity-kernel} the left side is $N\iint T(a,b)\,F(R(a,b))\,d\mu\,d\mu$ with $T=N^{-1}\sum_i\dot\xi_i(a)\dot\xi_i(b)$ and $F$ the kernel.  Since $\iint T\,d\mu\,d\mu=N^{-1}\sum_i\bigl(\int\dot\xi_i\,d\mu\bigr)^2=0$ one may replace $F(R)$ by $F(R)-F(q)$, which is at most $\Lambda|R-q|$.  Then $|T||R-q|\le\frac12(T^2+(R-q)^2)$, and $\iint T^2\le16\,\mathbb E(R-q)^2$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:gram-moments} (case $k=1$).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Site symmetry of the cavity deviation]
\label{lem:sg-tal:cavity-site-symmetry}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:cavity-deviation-sum}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sum_sum_centered_comp_equiv,
        SpinGlasses.integral_sum_sum_centered_perm,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_sq_eq_sum,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_weights_symmetric,
        SpinGlasses.integrable_sk_cavity_sq,
        SpinGlasses.integral_sk_cavity_sq_perm,
        SpinGlasses.integral_sk_cavity_sq_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.4: ``using symmetry between sites''}
Let $S_i(g_{\mathrm{old}})=\mathbb E_y\langle\dot\sigma_i\cosh L_y\rangle_-^2$ for the $N$-spin SK system.  Then $S_i$ is a continuous function of the Gibbs weights (a double Gibbs sum), $\mathbb E S_i$ does not depend on $i$, and if $Nc^2\le\beta_0^2$ and $\mathbb E\langle(R-q)^2\rangle\le K'/N$ then $\mathbb E S_i\le\frac{17}2\Lambda_0K'/N$ with $\Lambda_0=\frac12\beta_0^2e^{2\beta_0^2}(\cosh(2h)+1)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Relabelling the sites by a permutation $\pi$ is a measure-preserving relabelling of the Gaussian couplings under which the Gibbs weights are relabelled (the same site-symmetric random Gibbs state as in the proof of Proposition~1.4.14); the bilinear functional $\sum_{a,b}v_av_b\dot\xi_i(a)\dot\xi_i(b)K(a,b)$ with a relabelling-invariant kernel $K$ is transformed into the same functional at the site $\pi(i)$.  Hence all $\mathbb E S_i$ agree; their sum is at most $N\Lambda_0\frac{17}2\mathbb E\langle(R-q)^2\rangle$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cavity-deviation-sum}, and $B=Nc^2\le\beta_0^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.7.4, Eq.~(1.186)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-b}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:old-spin-cavity, lem:sg-tal:cavity-site-symmetry, lem:sg-tal:disorder-split, thm:sg-tal:overlap-moments-all}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_old_spin_cavity_approx}
  \leanok
\talsource{Lemma~1.7.4, Eq.~(1.186), for the system of $N+1$ spins}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $h\in\mathbb R$.  There is $K>0$ such that for all $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, $N\ge1$ and every old site $i\le N$, with $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$,
\[
\mathbb E\bigl(\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-\bigr)^2\le\frac K{N+1},
\]
where $\langle\cdot\rangle$ is the Gibbs measure of the $(N+1)$-spin system and $\langle\cdot\rangle_-$ that of the first $N$ spins with the old couplings at $\beta_-$.  $K$ depends only on $(\beta_0,h)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Choose a fixed point $q$ of the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:old-spin-cavity} the integrand is $F(g_{\mathrm{old}},y)=\bigl(\langle\dot\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_-/\langle\cosh L\rangle_-\bigr)^2\le\langle\dot\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_-^2$ (as $\langle\cosh L\rangle_-\ge1$).  By Proposition~\ref{lem:sg-tal:disorder-split} and nonnegativity, the left side is at most $\mathbb E_{g_{\mathrm{old}}}\mathbb E_yF$, hence at most $\mathbb E S_i$, which is bounded in Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cavity-site-symmetry}, with $K'$ from the uniform overlap moment bound (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:overlap-moments-all}, $r=1$).  Finally $1/N\le2/(N+1)$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Lemma~1.7.6: the cavity field is a standard Gaussian independent of the old couplings}
\label{sec:sg-tal:fluct-gaussian-field}

\emph{Content.}  Lemma~1.7.6 asks for a standard Gaussian $z$ with $\mathbb E(\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle-\tanh(\beta z\sqrt q+h))^2\le K/(N+1)$, which is a function of all couplings of the $(N+1)$-spin system and is independent of the couplings among the first $N$ spins.
Write $m_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-$ for the old magnetizations, $A=\|m\|$, and $y_i=g_{i,N+1}$ for the new couplings.  The book's $z=A^{-1}\sum_iy_im_i$ is replaced by $z=u\cdot y$ with the unit vector $u=m/A$ (and $u=e_1$ where $A=0$), so no case split is needed.  The proof has four steps.
(i) For fixed old couplings $z$ is a unit vector paired with an i.i.d.\ standard Gaussian vector, hence standard Gaussian; a function whose conditional law given the old couplings is a fixed law is independent of them (a general measure-theoretic lemma, not specific to spin glasses).
(ii) Combine Eq.~(1.185) with $|\tanh x-\tanh y|\le|x-y|$; what is left is the $y$-average $\mathbb E_y(\beta(N+1)^{-1/2}m\cdot y-\beta\sqrt q\,z)^2=\beta^2(\sqrt q-A/\sqrt{N+1})^2$ (Eq.~(1.188)).
(iii) For $h\ne0$ the fixed point is bounded below, so $(\sqrt q-a)^2\le(q-a^2)^2/q$ costs a constant that blows up as $h\to0$; this is the ``discontinuity'' the book mentions.
(iv) The normalization trap of the book: $A^2/(N+1)=\frac N{N+1}\bar R$ where $\bar R=N^{-1}\sum_im_i^2=\langle R_{1,2}\rangle_-$ is the overlap of the \emph{$N$-spin} system, so $q-A^2/(N+1)=\frac N{N+1}(q-\bar R)+\frac q{N+1}$; the extra $\frac q{N+1}$ is harmless.  Then Jensen, the overlap moment at $q_-$ (Eq.~(1.88)) and the fixed-point shift (Lemma~1.7.5, Part~G) finish.
For $h=0$ one has $q=0$ and $\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle=0$ by symmetry.
\emph{Uses:} Parts~G, J and the overlap moments of Section~1.4.  \emph{Status:} fully proved.

\begin{lemma}[Constant fibre law implies independence]
\label{lem:sg-tal:fibre-independence}
  \lean{MeasureTheory.Measure.map_prod_fst_eq_prod,
        ProbabilityTheory.indepFun_fst_of_ae_map_eq,
        ProbabilityTheory.indepFun_fst_comp_of_measurePreserving}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.6: ``conditionally upon $(g_{ij})_{i<j\le N-1}$, the r.v.\ $z$ is standard Gaussian \dots\ therefore the r.v.\ $z$ is independent of the r.v.s $(g_{ij})_{i<j\le N-1}$''}
Let $A,B$ be probability measures, $F:\alpha\times\beta\to\gamma$ measurable and $\lambda$ a probability measure such that, for $A$-almost every $a$, the law of $b\mapsto F(a,b)$ under $B$ is $\lambda$.  Then under $A\otimes B$ the pair $(a,F(a,b))$ has law $A\otimes\lambda$; consequently $F$ has law $\lambda$ and is independent of $a$.  The same holds for $F\circ e$ and $\mathrm{pr}_1\circ e$ under $P$ if $e$ carries $P$ to $A\otimes B$ measure-preservingly.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
For a measurable set $s$, $(A\otimes B)\{(a,b):(a,F(a,b))\in s\}=\int B\{b:(a,F(a,b))\in s\}\,dA(a)=\int\lambda(s_a)\,dA(a)=(A\otimes\lambda)(s)$ by Tonelli and the hypothesis, where $s_a$ is the section of $s$ at $a$.  Independence is the factorization of the law of the pair (the product of the marginals), and the transported statement is the change of variables along $e$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A standard Gaussian projection independent of the old couplings]
\label{lem:sg-tal:gaussian-projection}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:fibre-independence, lem:sg-tal:disorder-split}
  \lean{ProbabilityTheory.map_dotProduct_pi_gaussianReal,
        ProbabilityTheory.map_dotProduct_pi_gaussianReal_of_dotProduct_self,
        exists_measurable_unit_field,
        SpinGlasses.exists_independent_gaussian_projection,
        SpinGlasses.exists_sk_cavity_gaussian}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.6: the definition $z=A^{-1}\sum_{i\le N-1}g_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-$ and Eq.~(1.188)}
(a) For $\xi$ a vector of $N$ i.i.d.\ standard Gaussians and $a\in\mathbb R^N$, $a\cdot\xi\sim\mathcal N(0,a\cdot a)$; in particular a unit vector gives a standard Gaussian.
(b) For $N\ge1$ and a measurable family of vectors $m(\omega)\in\mathbb R^N$ there is a measurable family of unit vectors $u(\omega)$ with $m=\sqrt{m\cdot m}\;u$.
(c) Let $e$ carry $P$ to $P'\otimes\gamma^N$ measure-preservingly, with components $(\omega',y)$, and let $m$ be a measurable vector of $\omega'$.  Then there is a measurable $Z(\omega',y)=u(\omega')\cdot y$ such that $Z\circ e$ is a standard Gaussian independent of $\omega'$, and for all $c,t$ the map $y\mapsto(c\,m\cdot y-t\,Z)^2$ is integrable with integral $(c\sqrt{m\cdot m}-t)^2$.
For the SK system $e=\mathrm{skCavityDisorderEquiv}$ and $m_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-$ at $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$ with the old couplings.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) The linear form is a centered Gaussian (the product measure is the push of the standard Gaussian of $\mathbb R^N$) with variance $\mathbb E(a\cdot\xi)^2=a\cdot a$.  (b) Put $u=m/\|m\|$ where $m\ne0$ and $u=e_1$ otherwise; measurability follows from that of $\|m\|$ and of the set $\{\|m\|=0\}$.  (c) By (a) the fibre law of $y\mapsto u(\omega')\cdot y$ is $\mathcal N(0,1)$ for every $\omega'$; apply Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fibre-independence}.  Since $c\,m-t\,u=(c\|m\|-t)u$, the last claim is (a) again.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lower bound for the fixed point when $h\ne0$]
\label{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-lower}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.fixedPoint_lower_bound,
        SpinGlasses.integral_tanh_affine_lower,
        SpinGlasses.two_div_sub_div_sq_le_inv,
        Real.tanh_add_tanh_sub,
        Real.abs_tanh_sub_tanh_le,
        Real.abs_tanh_le_abs,
        SpinGlasses.integral_sq_gaussianReal_zero_one,
        SpinGlasses.fixedPoint_eq_zero_of_field_zero}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.6: ``we can and do assume $h\ne0$, for otherwise $q=0$'' and the division by $q$ in the last display (the book gives no lower bound)}
Let $4\beta^2<1$ and $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$.  If $h\ne0$ then $q\ge\bigl(\sinh(2|h|)/(\cosh2h+e^{1/2})\bigr)^2>0$, independently of $\beta$.  If $h=0$ then $q=0$.  Moreover $\tanh$ is $1$-Lipschitz.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Let $s=\beta\sqrt q$, so $s^2\le\beta^2\le1/4$.  For $h>0$, $\tanh(h+x)+\tanh(h-x)=2\sinh2h/(\cosh2h+\cosh2x)$; by the symmetry $z\mapsto-z$, $\mathbb E\tanh(sz+h)=\mathbb E\sinh2h/(\cosh2h+\cosh2sz)$.  The tangent line bound $1/y\ge2/D-y/D^2$ with $D=\cosh2h+e^{1/2}$ and $\mathbb E\cosh2sz=e^{2s^2}\le e^{1/2}$ give $\mathbb E\tanh(sz+h)\ge\sinh2h/D$.  For $h<0$ use oddness of $\tanh$.  Jensen gives $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2\ge(\mathbb E\tanh)^2$.  For $h=0$, $\tanh^2x\le x^2$ gives $q\le\beta^2q\le q/4$.  The Lipschitz bound is the mean value theorem with $\tanh'=1-\tanh^2\in[0,1]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The renormalization defect of the old magnetizations]
\label{lem:sg-tal:old-overlap-defect}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:overlap-moments-all, lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_old_overlap_dev_bound,
        SpinGlasses.sq_sub_mul_le,
        SpinGlasses.integral_integral_overlap,
        integral_integral_finset_sum_mul,
        SpinGlasses.sq_meanOverlap_sub_le_overlapMoment}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.6, Eq.~(1.189) (with the normalization $N/(N+1)$ made explicit)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$, $q$ a fixed point at $\beta$, $N\ge1$, and let $\bar R=N^{-1}\sum_{i\le N}\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-^2$ be the mean overlap of the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$.  There is an integrable function $B$ of the old couplings with $\bigl(q-\frac N{N+1}\bar R\bigr)^2\le B$ and $\mathbb E B\le K/(N+1)$, where $K$ depends on $(\beta_0,h)$ only.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Let $q_-$ be a fixed point at $\beta_-$; by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift} (with $N+1$ for $N$) $|q-q_-|\le\frac1{N+1}$.  For $\varepsilon=\frac1{N+1}$, $q-(1-\varepsilon)\bar R=(1-\varepsilon)(q-\bar R)+\varepsilon q$ and $(a+b)^2\le2a^2+2b^2$ twice give $(q-(1-\varepsilon)\bar R)^2\le4(\bar R-q_-)^2+6\varepsilon^2$.  Since replicas are independent, $\bar R-q_-=\mathbb E\langle R_{1,2}-q_-\rangle_-$, so Jensen gives $(\bar R-q_-)^2\le\langle(R_{1,2}-q_-)^2\rangle_-$, whose disorder average is at most $K'/N$ by the overlap moment of Section~1.4 (uniform in $|\beta|\le\beta_0$).  Take $B=4\langle(R_{1,2}-q_-)^2\rangle_-+6\varepsilon^2$; $1/N\le2/(N+1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The cavity field against the renormalized Gaussian]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cavity-gaussian-sub}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:gaussian-projection, lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-lower, lem:sg-tal:old-overlap-defect}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_tanh_gaussian_sub_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Lemma~1.7.6: Eq.~(1.188) and the display after it}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $h\ne0$.  There is $K>0$ (depending on $(\beta_0,h)$) such that for $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q$ at $\beta$, $N\ge1$, and every pair $(m,Z)$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:gaussian-projection}(c) for the old magnetizations,
\[
\mathbb E\Bigl(\tanh\Bigl(\frac\beta{\sqrt{N+1}}\sum_{i\le N}y_im_i+h\Bigr)-\tanh(\beta\sqrt q\,Z+h)\Bigr)^2\le\frac K{N+1}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
By Proposition~\ref{lem:sg-tal:disorder-split} and nonnegativity, the left side is at most the iterated integral over $(g_{\mathrm{old}},y)$.  For fixed $g_{\mathrm{old}}$, $|\tanh x-\tanh y|\le|x-y|$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:gaussian-projection}(c) with $c=(N+1)^{-1/2}$, $t=\sqrt q$ bound the $y$-integral by $\beta^2(c\|m\|-\sqrt q)^2\le\beta^2(q-c^2\|m\|^2)^2/q$.  Here $c^2\|m\|^2=\frac N{N+1}\bar R$, so Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:old-overlap-defect} bounds the integrand by $B$, and $q\ge q_{\min}(h)>0$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-lower}.  Hence the bound $\beta_0^2q_{\min}^{-1}K_B/(N+1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.7.6]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-176}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-a, thm:sg-tal:cavity-gaussian-sub, lem:sg-tal:gaussian-projection, lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-lower}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_last_spin_gaussian_approx,
        SpinGlasses.integral_spin_eq_zero_of_field_zero,
        SpinGlasses.integrable_sq_sub_of_abs_le,
        SpinGlasses.abs_integral_spin_le_one,
        SpinGlasses.measurable_integral_count_tilted}
  \leanok
\talsource{Lemma~1.7.6, for the system of $N+1$ spins}
Let $\beta_0\ge0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $h\in\mathbb R$.  There is $K>0$ (depending on $(\beta_0,h)$ only) such that for all $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$, every solution $q$ of $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ and all $N\ge1$ there is a measurable function $z$ of the couplings of the $(N+1)$-spin system such that $z$ is a standard Gaussian, $z$ is independent of the couplings $(g_{ij})_{i<j\le N}$ among the first $N$ spins, and
\[
\mathbb E\bigl(\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle-\tanh(\beta z\sqrt q+h)\bigr)^2\le\frac K{N+1}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
For $h=0$: $q=0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-lower}), $\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle=0$ (the energy is even in the spins), and $z=Z\circ e$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:gaussian-projection} works.  For $h\ne0$ take $z=Z\circ e$ for the old magnetizations at $\beta_-$.  Write the integrand as $(X-W)^2$ with $X=\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle$, $W=\tanh(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ and let $T$ be the $\tanh$ of Eq.~(1.185).  Then $(X-W)^2\le2(X-T)^2+2(T-W)^2$; all three functions are measurable and bounded (the Gibbs mean is a measurable function of the couplings, bounded by $1$), so the integrals add.  The first term is bounded by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-a} ($k=1$) and the second by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cavity-gaussian-sub}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Differences from the book in Lemma~1.7.6]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-176}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Indexing and sign of $\beta$.}  We write $N+1$ spins and $N$ old ones; $\beta\ge0$, because the fixed-point shift (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift}) is stated for $\beta\ge0$ (the model is symmetric under $\beta\mapsto-\beta$).
\item \emph{Uniformity.}  $K$ is uniform in $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$ and in the solution $q$, as the induction of Theorem~1.7.1 needs; it depends on $h$ and degenerates as $h\to0$, as the book says (here through $q_{\min}(h)=(\sinh|2h|/(\cosh2h+e^{1/2}))^2$).
\item \emph{No case split on $A=0$.}  The renormalized Gaussian is $u\cdot y$ with a measurable unit vector $u$ (equal to $m/\|m\|$ when $m\ne0$) instead of $A^{-1}\sum_ig_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-$.
\item \emph{Normalization.}  The book writes $A^2=N\langle R_{1,2}^-\rangle_-$ with $N$ replaced by $N-1$ in the overlap of the smaller system; we keep the factor $N/(N+1)$ explicit and absorb the remainder $q/(N+1)$.
\item \emph{Lower bound for $q$.}  The book uses $1/q$ without comment; we prove $q\ge q_{\min}(h)>0$ explicitly.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\subsection{Theorem~1.7.1: the local magnetizations are asymptotically i.i.d.}
\label{sec:sg-tal:fluct-iid}

\emph{Content.}  Fix $n$.  Theorem~1.7.1 asserts that the first $n$ Gibbs means $\langle\sigma_i\rangle$ are close in $L^2$, up to $K/N$, to $\tanh(\beta z_i\sqrt q+h)$ for independent standard Gaussians $z_i$ that are functions of the couplings.
We follow the book's induction on $n$, in the uniform form (1.190): $K=K(n,\beta_0)$ for all $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$ (uniformity is needed because the induction step applies the hypothesis at $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$, which moves with $N$).
The step from $n$ to $n+1$ uses the system of $N+1$ spins:
(i) apply the hypothesis to the first $N$ spins at $\beta_-$ (with a fixed point $q_-$ of that system); this gives Gaussians $z^-_i$ that are functions of the old couplings and Eq.~(1.191);
(ii) replace $\beta_-\sqrt{q_-}$ by $\beta\sqrt q$ (bounded by $1/(N+1)$ through $|\sqrt a-\sqrt b|^2\le|a-b|$, Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift} and $\beta-\beta_-\le\beta/(N+1)$) and $\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-$ by $\langle\sigma_i\rangle$ (Eq.~(1.186));
(iii) append the new Gaussian of Lemma~1.7.6 for the last site; it is independent of the old couplings, hence of the $z^-_i$, so the vector has the i.i.d.\ law;
(iv) exchange the last site with the new site $n$.
\emph{The exchange must move the Gaussian vector as well:} if $\varphi$ is the measure-preserving relabelling of the couplings with $\langle\sigma_{\pi^{-1}i}\rangle_g=\langle\sigma_i\rangle_{\varphi g}$, the new vector is $z\circ\varphi$, whose law is unchanged and whose error terms are the same integrals transported by $\varphi$.  Exchanging only the expectations would not prove the theorem.
\emph{Uses:} Parts~G, J, K, L and Proposition~1.4.14.  \emph{Status:} fully proved.

\begin{lemma}[Appending an independent coordinate to an i.i.d.\ vector]
\label{lem:sg-tal:append-coordinate}
  \lean{measurePreserving_finSuccProd,
        map_finSuccProd_symm_eq_pi,
        SpinGlasses.integral_sq_coord_eq_one,
        SpinGlasses.integrable_sq_coord}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Theorem~1.7.1: ``$z$ is standard Gaussian and probabilistically independent of the r.v.s $(z_i)_{i\le n}$ \dots''}
Let $\mu$ be a probability measure, $Y$ a random vector in $X^n$ with law $\mu^{\otimes n}$ and $W$ a random variable with law $\mu$, independent of $Y$.  Then $(Y,W)$ (the vector with $W$ appended as the last coordinate) has the law $\mu^{\otimes(n+1)}$.  If a vector has the i.i.d.\ standard Gaussian law, each coordinate has second moment $1$ and an integrable square.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The law of the pair is $\mu^{\otimes n}\otimes\mu$ by independence, and the measurable equivalence $(X^{n+1})\simeq X^n\times X$ (splitting off the last coordinate) carries $\mu^{\otimes(n+1)}$ to this product.  The coordinate statement is the marginal of a product measure and $\int x^2\,d\gamma=1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Site exchange for the Gibbs means]
\label{lem:sg-tal:site-exchange}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.exists_sk_site_exchange,
        SpinGlasses.measurable_integral_spin_skGibbsEnergy,
        SpinGlasses.exists_fixedPoint_cavity,
        SpinGlasses.sq_cavity_param_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Theorem~1.7.1: ``exchanging the sites $N$ and $n+1$'' and the estimate $(\beta_-\sqrt{q_-}-\beta\sqrt q)^2\le L/N$}
(a) For every permutation $\pi$ of the sites of the $M$-spin SK system there is a measure-preserving map $\varphi$ of the couplings with $\langle\sigma_i\rangle_{\varphi g}=\langle\sigma_{\pi^{-1}(i)}\rangle_g$ for all $g,i$; Gibbs means of spins are measurable in the couplings.
(b) For $0\le\beta$, $4\beta^2<1$ and a fixed point $q$ at $\beta$, the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$ has a fixed point $q_-$ with $|q-q_-|\le\frac1{N+1}$, and then $(\beta_-\sqrt{q_-}-\beta\sqrt q)^2\le\frac1{N+1}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) The relabelling $g\mapsto g\circ\pi^{-1}$ of the pair couplings preserves the Gaussian product measure and relabels the Gibbs weights (Proposition~1.4.14); the Gibbs mean is then reindexed along the configuration relabelling.  (b) The first claim is Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift} with $N+1$ for $N$.  For the second write $s=\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$: $\beta s\sqrt{q_-}-\beta\sqrt q=\beta s(\sqrt{q_-}-\sqrt q)+\beta(s-1)\sqrt q$, $(\sqrt{q_-}-\sqrt q)^2\le|q_--q|$, $0\le1-s\le\frac1{N+1}$ and $4\beta^2<1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Induction step for Theorem~1.7.1]
\label{thm:sg-tal:theorem-171-step}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-b, thm:sg-tal:lemma-176, lem:sg-tal:append-coordinate, lem:sg-tal:site-exchange}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_local_magnetizations_step,
        MeasureTheory.integral_sq_add_add_le,
        MeasureTheory.integrable_sq_of_abs_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Theorem~1.7.1 (induction step from $n$ to $n+1$)}
Let $\beta_0\ge0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $h\in\mathbb R$.  Suppose that for $n$ there is $K_n$ such that for all $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q$ and every $N>n$ there is a measurable vector $z$ of the couplings with the i.i.d.\ standard Gaussian law and $\sum_{i<n}\mathbb E(\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\tanh(\beta z_i\sqrt q+h))^2\le K_n/N$.  Then the same holds for $n+1$ (and every $N>n+1$) with $K_{n+1}=3nK_{(1.186)}+6K_n+3n+K_{1.7.6}+1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Write the system size as $N+1$ with $N>n$.  Apply the hypothesis to the first $N$ spins at $\beta_-$ and a fixed point $q_-$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:site-exchange}(b)), take the Gaussian $z_{\mathrm{new}}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-176} for the last spin, and form $z'=(z^-\circ\mathrm{old},z_{\mathrm{new}})$, which has the i.i.d.\ law by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:append-coordinate}.  Let $\pi$ swap the new site $n$ and the last site and $\varphi$ the measure-preserving map of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:site-exchange}(a); the claimed vector is $z=z'\circ\varphi$.  By change of variables along $\varphi$ each summand is the corresponding summand for $z'$ at the site $\pi(i)$.  For an old site $i<n$ split $\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\tanh(\beta\sqrt q\,z^-_i)$ into three pieces: $\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-b}, $\le K/(N+1)$), $\langle\sigma_i\rangle_--\tanh(\beta_-\sqrt{q_-}z^-_i)$ (the hypothesis, summed over $i$ and transported from the old disorder to the full one), and $\tanh(\beta_-\sqrt{q_-}z^-_i)-\tanh(\beta\sqrt q\,z^-_i)$ (at most $(\beta_-\sqrt{q_-}-\beta\sqrt q)^2\mathbb E(z^-_i)^2\le\frac1{N+1}$, as $\tanh$ is $1$-Lipschitz).  Use $(a+b+c)^2\le3(a^2+b^2+c^2)$ and $\frac1N\le\frac2{N+1}$.  The last site is Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-176}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem~1.7.1]
\label{thm:sg-tal:theorem-171}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:theorem-171-step}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_local_magnetizations_uniform,
        SpinGlasses.sk_local_magnetizations_gaussian}
  \leanok
\talsource{Theorem~1.7.1, Eq.~(1.183) and the induction hypothesis (1.190)}
Let $\beta\ge0$ with $4\beta^2<1$, $h\in\mathbb R$, $n\in\mathbb N$ and $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$.  There is $K>0$ depending only on $(n,\beta,h)$ such that for every $N>n$ there are measurable functions $z_1,\dots,z_n$ of the couplings $(g_{ij})_{i<j\le N}$, independent standard Gaussians, with
\[
\sum_{i<n}\mathbb E\bigl(\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\tanh(\beta z_i\sqrt q+h)\bigr)^2\le\frac KN.
\]
More precisely (uniform form (1.190)): for $\beta_0\ge0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$ the constant can be chosen depending only on $(n,\beta_0,h)$, uniformly in $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$ and in the solution $q$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Induction on $n$ using Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:theorem-171-step}; for $n=0$ the sum is empty.  The independence and the marginals follow from the joint i.i.d.\ law.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Differences from the book in Theorem~1.7.1]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-171}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Hypotheses.}  $\beta\ge0$ (the model is symmetric in the sign of $\beta$, but the fixed-point shift is stated for $\beta\ge0$) and $N>n$ (the book says ``any $N$''; for $N\le n$ there are fewer couplings than Gaussians required, e.g.\ no pair coupling for $N=1$, so the statement cannot hold literally).
\item \emph{Independence is a joint-law statement.}  The Gaussians are given as one measurable vector with the law $\gamma^{\otimes n}$; independence and the marginals are corollaries.
\item \emph{Site sets.}  The sites are $0,\dots,n-1$ (the book uses $1,\dots,n$).  The induction runs through the $(N+1)$-spin system with the new site exchanged with the last one, and the Gaussian vector is transported with the exchange (see the discussion above).
\item \emph{Constants.}  $K$ is explicit in the induction: $K_{n+1}=3nK_{(1.186)}+6K_n+3n+K_{1.7.6}+1$, with $K_{(1.186)}$ and $K_{1.7.6}$ the constants of Theorems~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-b} and~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-176}.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Cavity formula for an old magnetization, ratio form]
\label{lem:sg-tal:old-spin-ratio}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_old_spin_eq_mul_cosh_div}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Theorem~1.7.7: ``$\langle\sigma_i\rangle=\langle\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_-/\langle\cosh L\rangle_-$''}
In the $(N+1)$-spin SK system, with $(g_{\rm old},y)$ the splitting of the couplings, $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$ and $L(\rho)=N^{-1/2}\beta_-\sum_jy_j\rho_j+h$, for every old site $i$
\[
\langle\sigma_i\rangle=\frac{\langle\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_-}{\langle\cosh L\rangle_-}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
By the cavity formula for an old magnetization, $\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-=\langle(\sigma_i-\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-)\cosh L\rangle_-/\langle\cosh L\rangle_-$; expand the numerator as $\langle\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_--\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-\langle\cosh L\rangle_-$ and use $\langle\cosh L\rangle_-\ge1>0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Algebra and domination for the TAP equation]
\label{lem:sg-tal:tap-algebra}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.abs_sub_tanh_tap_le,
        SpinGlasses.abs_tap_coef_le,
        SpinGlasses.integrable_pow_tap_field,
        MeasureTheory.integral_pow_le_of_abs_le_add_add}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.7, proof of Theorem~1.7.7 (steps 3 and 4)}
(a) If $c\sqrt N=b$ and $|s|\le1$, then for all reals $q,q_-,\beta,h$ and vectors $y,m,f$,
$|s-\tanh(c\sum y_if_i+h-\beta^2(1-q)s)|\le|s-\tanh(c\sum y_im_i+h)|+|b|\,|X|+|\beta^2(1-q)-b^2(1-q_-)|$, where $X=N^{-1/2}\sum y_if_i-b(1-q_-)s-N^{-1/2}\sum y_im_i$.
(b) If $4\beta^2<1$, $q,q_-\in[0,1]$ and $|q-q_-|\le\frac1{N+1}$ then $|\beta^2(1-q)-\beta^2\frac N{N+1}(1-q_-)|\le\frac1{N+1}$.
(c) If $|f_i|,|m_i|,|s|,|b|\le1$ (all measurable in the disorder and in $y$), then $X^{2k}$ is integrable for the product of any probability measure with $\gamma^{\otimes N}$.
(d) If $|\Delta|\le a+b+c$ pointwise and $a^{2k},b^{2k},c^{2k}$ are integrable, then $\int\Delta^{2k}\le2^{2k-1}\int a^{2k}+2^{2k-1}(2^{2k-1}\int b^{2k}+2^{2k-1}\int c^{2k})$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) $|\tanh u-\tanh v|\le|u-v|$ and $(c\sum y_im_i+h)-(c\sum y_if_i+h-\beta^2(1-q)s)=-bX+(\beta^2(1-q)-b^2(1-q_-))s$.  (b) Write the difference as $(\beta^2-b^2)(1-q)+b^2(q_--q)$ with $b^2=\beta^2\frac N{N+1}$, $\beta^2-b^2=\frac{\beta^2}{N+1}\le\frac1{4(N+1)}$ and $b^2\le\frac14$.  (c) $|X|\le2N^{-1/2}\sum|y_i|+1$ and the $2k$-th power of the right side is integrable because each $y_i$ is in every $L^p$.  (d) Two applications of $(x+y)^{2k}\le2^{2k-1}(x^{2k}+y^{2k})$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem~1.7.7, Eq.~(1.193): the TAP equations]
\label{thm:sg-tal:theorem-177}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:old-spin-ratio, lem:sg-tal:tap-algebra, thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-a, thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction-book, lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift, lem:sg-tal:site-exchange}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_tap_equation}
  \leanok
\talsource{Theorem~1.7.7, Eq.~(1.193), proof at source text lines 3152--3190}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, $h\in\mathbb R$ and $k\ge1$.  There is $K=K(\beta_0,h,k)>0$ such that for every $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ and every $N\ge1$, in the $(N+1)$-spin SK system with Gibbs mean $\langle\cdot\rangle$ and last-spin couplings $g_i$ ($i\le N$),
\[
\mathbb E\Bigl(\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle-\tanh\Bigl(\frac\beta{\sqrt{N+1}}\sum_{i\le N}g_i\langle\sigma_i\rangle+h-\beta^2(1-q)\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle\Bigr)\Bigr)^{2k}\le\frac K{(N+1)^k}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Let $\beta_-=\beta\sqrt{N/(N+1)}$ and let $q_-$ be a fixed point at $\beta_-$ with $|q-q_-|\le\frac1{N+1}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift}, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:site-exchange}(b)).  By the cavity formulas $\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle=\langle\sinh L\rangle_-/\langle\cosh L\rangle_-=:s$ and $\langle\sigma_i\rangle=f_i:=\langle\sigma_i\cosh L\rangle_-/\langle\cosh L\rangle_-$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:old-spin-ratio}).  Put $m_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle_-$.  Let $U=s-\tanh(\frac\beta{\sqrt{N+1}}\sum g_im_i+h)$, which satisfies $\mathbb EU^{2k}\le K_1/(N+1)^k$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-a} (its $2k$-version), and let $X$ be the integrand of Eq.~(1.210) in the book form (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction-book}) for the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-$, $q_-$, so $\mathbb EX^{2k}\le K_2/N^k$.  Since $\beta/\sqrt{N+1}=\beta_-/\sqrt N$, Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:tap-algebra}(a),(b) give pointwise $|\Delta|\le|U|+\beta_0|X|+\frac1{N+1}$ for the quantity $\Delta$ inside the expectation.  Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:tap-algebra}(c),(d) give $\mathbb E\Delta^{2k}\le2^{2k-1}K_1(N+1)^{-k}+2^{2k-1}\bigl(2^{2k-1}\beta_0^{2k}K_2N^{-k}+2^{2k-1}(N+1)^{-2k}\bigr)$, and $N^{-k}\le2^k(N+1)^{-k}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Differences from the book in Theorem~1.7.7]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-177}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Indexing.}  The system has $N+1$ spins and the last spin is the one singled out (the book writes $N$ and $N-1$); the sites are $0,\dots,N$ and $g_i$ is the coupling of the pair $(i,N)$.
\item \emph{Hypotheses.}  $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$ (the book: $\beta<1/2$), with $K$ depending only on $\beta_0,h,k$, and $q$ an arbitrary fixed point (no uniqueness is used).
\item \emph{Gibbs means.}  Both $\langle\sigma_i\rangle$ and $\langle\sigma_{N+1}\rangle$ are means in the $(N+1)$-spin system, as in the book; their cavity expressions are the ratios of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:old-spin-ratio}.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Exchangeability of the TAP defects]
\label{lem:sg-tal:tap-exchange}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.skSymmCoupling_comp_pairPerm,
        SpinGlasses.tap_defect_comp_pairPerm,
        SpinGlasses.sum_skSymmCoupling_last,
        SpinGlasses.integral_spin_comp_pairPerm,
        SpinGlasses.gibbsWeight_comp_pairPerm,
        MeasureTheory.integral_comp_eq_of_perm_equivariant}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.7.8, proof: ``by (1.193) and symmetry between sites''}
Let $g_{ij}=g_{ji}$ be the symmetric extension of the couplings $(g_{ij})_{i<j}$ ($g_{ii}=0$) and, in the $M$-spin SK system, $\Delta_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\tanh\bigl(c\sum_jg_{ij}\langle\sigma_j\rangle+h-b\langle\sigma_i\rangle\bigr)$ for fixed reals $c,b$.  For a permutation $\pi$ of the sites let $g^\pi$ be the couplings $g\circ(\mathrm{pairPerm}\,\pi)^{-1}$ (the pair $\{i,j\}$ is sent to the sorted pair $\{\pi i,\pi j\}$).  Then (a) $\langle\sigma_i\rangle_{g^\pi}=\langle\sigma_{\pi^{-1}i}\rangle_g$; (b) $(g^\pi)_{ij}=g_{\pi^{-1}i,\pi^{-1}j}$; (c) $\Delta_i(g^\pi)=\Delta_{\pi^{-1}i}(g)$.  Consequently, since $g\mapsto g^\pi$ preserves the Gaussian law, $\mathbb E\,G(\Delta_i)$ does not depend on $i$ for every measurable $G$ (generally: if every permutation of the index set is implemented by a measure preserving map $\varphi$ with $F_i\circ\varphi=F_{\pi i}$ then $\int G(F_i)$ does not depend on $i$).  Finally the symmetric field at the last site is $\sum_jg_{{\rm last},j}m_j=\sum_{i<N}y_im_i$ with $y_i$ the coupling of the pair $(i,N)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) The Gibbs weight of $\sigma$ under $g^\pi$ is the weight of $\sigma\circ\pi$ under $g$ (Proposition~1.4.14: the pair sum is relabelled by Lemma on $\mathrm{pairPerm}$, the field term is invariant), and the spin sum is reindexed along $\pi$.  (b) Case split on $i<j$, $i=j$, $j<i$ and on the order of $\pi^{-1}i,\pi^{-1}j$; the sorting of a pair is the identity on an ordered pair.  (c) Combine (a), (b) and reindex $\sum_j$ by $\pi^{-1}$.  The exchangeability statement follows by taking the swap of $i$ and $j$ and the change of variables $\int G(F_j(\varphi\omega))\,dP=\int G(F_j)\,dP$.  The last claim is $\mathrm{Fin.sum\_univ\_castSucc}$ and $g_{\rm last,last}=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The expectation of a maximum from high moments]
\label{lem:sg-tal:max-moment}
  \lean{integral_sup'_abs_le,
        integral_sup'_abs_le_rpow,
        rpow_neg_div_le_of_inv_le,
        le_add_pow_div}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.7.8, proof: ``$\mathbb E(\max|\Delta_i|)^{2k}\le\sum_i\mathbb E\Delta_i^{2k}$ ... so $\mathbb E\max|\Delta_i|\le K N^{-(1/2-1/2k)}$''}
Let $\Delta_1,\dots,\Delta_M$ be random variables with $\Delta_i^{2k}$ integrable.  (a) For every $a>0$, $\mathbb E\max_i|\Delta_i|\le a+a^{-(2k-1)}\sum_i\mathbb E\Delta_i^{2k}$.  (b) If $\mathbb E\Delta_i^{2k}\le K/M^k$ for all $i$, then $\mathbb E\max_i|\Delta_i|\le(1+K)M^{-(k-1)/(2k)}$.  (c) If $M\ge1$ and $\frac1{2k}\le\varepsilon$ then $M^{-(k-1)/(2k)}\le M^{-(1/2-\varepsilon)}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) For $x\ge0$ and $a>0$ one has $x\le a+x^{2k}/a^{2k-1}$ (trivial if $x\le a$; otherwise $x^{2k}\ge x\,a^{2k-1}$), and $|\Delta_i|^{2k}\le\sum_j\Delta_j^{2k}$; integrate.  This replaces the Lyapunov step of the book.  (b) Take $a=M^{-(k-1)/(2k)}$, so that $a^{2k}=M^{1-k}$; then $\sum_i\mathbb E\Delta_i^{2k}\le KM^{1-k}=Ka^{2k}$ and the bound of (a) is $a+Ka$.  (c) The exponent $-\frac{k-1}{2k}=-\frac12+\frac1{2k}\le-\frac12+\varepsilon$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Corollary~1.7.8, Eq.~(1.194): the TAP equations at all sites]
\label{cor:sg-tal:corollary-178}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:theorem-177, lem:sg-tal:tap-exchange, lem:sg-tal:max-moment}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_tap_uniform}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.7.8, Eq.~(1.194)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, $h\in\mathbb R$ and $\varepsilon>0$.  There is $K=K(\beta_0,h,\varepsilon)>0$ such that for every $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q$ and every $N\ge1$, in the $(N+1)$-spin SK system with Gibbs mean $\langle\cdot\rangle$ and symmetric couplings $g_{ij}$,
\[
\mathbb E\max_{i\le N+1}\Bigl|\langle\sigma_i\rangle-\tanh\Bigl(\frac\beta{\sqrt{N+1}}\sum_{j\ne i}g_{ij}\langle\sigma_j\rangle+h-\beta^2(1-q)\langle\sigma_i\rangle\Bigr)\Bigr|\le\frac K{(N+1)^{1/2-\varepsilon}}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Choose $k\ge1$ with $\frac1{2k}\le\varepsilon$ and let $K_k$ be the constant of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:theorem-177}.  The defect $\Delta_{\rm last}$ is the quantity of that theorem (the last claim of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:tap-exchange}), so $\mathbb E\Delta_{\rm last}^{2k}\le K_k/(N+1)^k$, and by exchangeability the same holds for every $\Delta_i$.  Each $\Delta_i$ is measurable and bounded by $2$, so Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:max-moment}(b),(c) gives the claim with $K=1+K_k$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Differences from the book in Corollary~1.7.8]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-178}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Indexing and hypotheses} as in Remark~\ref{rem:sg-tal:dev-177}: $N+1$ spins, $0\le\beta\le\beta_0$, any fixed point $q$.  The sum over $j\ne i$ is written as a sum over all $j$ with $g_{ii}=0$.
\item \emph{No Lyapunov inequality.}  The book passes from $\mathbb E\max^{2k}$ to $\mathbb E\max$ by Jensen; the formalization uses the pointwise bound $x\le a+x^{2k}/a^{2k-1}$ with a tuned $a$, which gives the same exponent with the constant $1+K$.
\item \emph{Symmetric couplings.}  Only the couplings $g_{ij}$ with $i<j$ are stored; $g_{ij}$ for $i>j$ is $g_{ji}$.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[What remains of Section~1.7]
\label{rem:sg-tal:remaining-17}
Of Section~1.7 the following are formalized: the general Gaussian fluctuation principle (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:general-fluct}), the fixed-point shift (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:fixed-point-shift}),
Corollary~1.7.13 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1713}), Lemma~1.7.14 (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:ratio}) and Corollary~1.7.15 (Theorems~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-tanh} and~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction}).
Also formalized are Eq.~(1.185) of Lemma~1.7.4 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-a}), obtained by applying Eq.~(1.209) to the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-$ for the $(N+1)$-spin system, and Eq.~(1.186) (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-b}).
Lemma~1.7.6 (the cavity field as a disorder-measurable Gaussian) is formalized as Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-176}.
Theorem~1.7.1 (the local magnetizations are asymptotically i.i.d.) is formalized as Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:theorem-171}, by the book's induction from Eqs.~(1.185), (1.186) and Lemma~1.7.6.
Theorem~1.7.7 (the TAP equations, in the $(N+1)$-spin indexing) is formalized as Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:theorem-177}.
Corollary~1.7.8 (all sites at once) is formalized as Theorem~\ref{cor:sg-tal:corollary-178}; Section~1.7 is therefore complete in this chapter.
Eq.~(1.210) is available in the book form (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cor-1715-reaction-book}).  The Corollaries are stated for the $N$-spin system with constants uniform in $|\beta|\le\beta_0<1/2$ (Remark~\ref{rem:sg-tal:dev-1713}), so the application to the $N$-spin system at $\beta_-$ is by relabeling $N$ and $\beta$ (done in Theorems~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-a} and~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-174-b}).
Nothing in Sections~1.1--1.6 depends on Section~1.7.
\end{remark}

\section{Second moment computations and the Almeida--Thouless line (Talagrand Section~1.8)}
\label{sec:sg-tal:second-moment}

\paragraph{The idea.}
Theorem~1.4.1 gives $\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)\le K/N$ for $\beta<1/2$, and Section~1.8 computes the limit of $N\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)$.
Write $\nu_t$ for the average along the cavity path of Section~1.6, so that $\nu_1=\nu$ is the SK average of the $(N+1)$-spin system and $\nu_0$ is the decoupled average in which the last spin has the field $\beta z\sqrt q+h$.
The method has three steps.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{Expansion along the path} (Proposition~1.8.1): $\nu(f)=\nu_0(f)+\nu_0'(f)+\text{error}$, where the first derivative is Eq.~(1.151), the error is controlled by a Hoelder product of $\nu(|f|^{\tau_1})^{1/\tau_1}$ and a power of $\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)$, and the second derivative is the first-derivative formula applied to the observable $f\cdot(\text{replica kernel combination})$ on $n+2$ replicas.
\item[(ii)] \emph{Algebra at the decoupled endpoint} (Lemmas~1.8.2 and~1.8.4): by Lemma~1.6.2 the last spins decouple, so $\nu_0$ of a product of last spins is a power of $\mathbb E\tanh Y$ times an average over the old spins.  For the rectangle $(\varepsilon_1-\varepsilon_2)(\varepsilon_3-\varepsilon_4)$ this produces the factor $\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)$, where $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4Y$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{A closed linear equation} (Proposition~1.8.5 and Corollary~1.8.6): site symmetry turns $\nu((\text{rectangle})^2)$ into a last-site quantity, (i) and (ii) express it through itself with coefficient $\lambda=\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)<1$, and one solves for it.
\end{enumerate}
The quantity $1-2q+\hat q=\mathbb E(1-\tanh^2Y)^2=\mathbb E\,\mathrm{ch}^{-4}Y$ governs the Almeida--Thouless line $\beta^2\mathbb E\,\mathrm{ch}^{-4}Y=1$.

\paragraph{How the section is organized.}
\begin{enumerate}
\item[A.] \emph{Proposition~1.8.1}: Eq.~(1.215) and Eq.~(1.216).  Fully proved.
\item[B.] \emph{Lemmas~1.8.2 and~1.8.4}: the rectangular combination, Lemma~1.8.4.  Fully proved.
\item[C.] \emph{Proposition~1.8.5 and Corollary~1.8.6}.  Fully proved.
\item[D.] \emph{General Lemma~1.8.2 (Eq.~1.230) and the $3\times3$ AT matrix algebra (Eqs.~1.234--1.236)}.  Fully proved.
\end{enumerate}
Parts E and F below add the combinations of pair covariances, Lemma~1.8.9, the covariance system (1.231)--(1.233), the $T$-basis orthogonality, Propositions~1.8.7--1.8.11 and Theorem~1.8.12; all are proved.

\begin{remark}[Differences from the book in Section~1.8]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-18}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Indexing.}  The system has $N+1$ spins, the singled-out spin is the last one, and $M=N+1$ replaces the book's $N$.  The truncated overlap is $R^-_{\ell\ell'}=M^{-1}\sum_{i<N}\sigma^\ell_i\sigma^{\ell'}_i$, so that $R=R^-+\varepsilon\varepsilon'/M$ exactly (the book normalizes $R^-$ by $N-1$ and absorbs the difference into $\mathcal R$).
\item \emph{Hoelder pairs.}  In Eq.~(1.215) all pairs $(\tau_1,\tau_2)$ with $\tau_1<\infty$ are proved, and $(\infty,1)$ separately; in Eq.~(1.216) all conjugate pairs $(p,r)$ are proved with $\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^{2r})$ on the right, and only $(2,2)$ is used.  The deviations in the Hoelder products are $R-q$ with the full overlap, not $R^--q$; the passage $R^-\to R$ costs $1/M$ and is absorbed in the explicit constants.
\item \emph{No $\mathcal R$ notation.}  The error terms of the book's $\mathcal R$ and $O(3)$ are explicit: for $|\beta|\le\beta_0$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$ they are bounded by $K/M^{3/2}$ with $K$ depending only on $\beta_0$ and $h$.
\item \emph{Second-order expansion.}  Instead of the integral Taylor formula in $t=\cos^2s$ the formalization uses the angle $s$ and the fencing lemma \texttt{abs\_sub\_sub\_half\_le\_of\_second\_order}, which needs no continuity of $\nu_t'$ at the endpoints.  The constant in Eq.~(1.216) is $4n^2(n+2)^2\beta^4e^{4(n+4)^2\beta^2}$.
\item \emph{Fixed point.}  $q$ is a hypothesis $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ (any fixed point), as in the rest of the chapter.
\item \emph{The AT quantity.}  It is written $1-2q+\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4Y-2q+1$; that this equals $\mathbb E\,\mathrm{ch}^{-4}Y$ is not stated in Lean, only $0\le1-2q+\hat q\le1$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-algebra}(c)), which gives $\lambda\le\beta^2<1/4$.
\item \emph{Corollary~1.8.6} is stated for two distinct sites $i\ne j$ of the $(N+1)$-spin system; the book's $\sigma_1,\sigma_2$ are two sites, and by symmetry the value does not depend on the pair.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\subsection{Part A: Proposition~1.8.1}

\begin{lemma}[Replica marginals and block independence]
\label{lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}
  \lean{MeasureTheory.measurePreserving_pi_comp_injective,
        SpinGlasses.integral_pi_count_tilted_comp_injective,
        SpinGlasses.integral_pi_fin_add_mul,
        SpinGlasses.integral_four_replicas_cov_sq,
        SpinGlasses.integral_gibbs_finset_sum}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proofs of Proposition~1.8.1 (``Hoelder's inequality'') and of Corollary~1.8.6 (independence of the replica pairs)}
(a) If $X=(X_{r'})_{r'\in R'}$ is i.i.d.\ with law $\mu$ and $\rho:R\to R'$ is injective then $(X_{\rho(r)})_{r\in R}$ is i.i.d.\ with law $\mu$; hence a function of $n$ of $n'$ replicas of a finite Gibbs measure has the same average as under $n$ replicas.  (b) For $m,n\ge0$ and i.i.d.\ coordinates, $\mathbb E\,F(X_1,\dots,X_m)G(X_{m+1},\dots,X_{m+n})=\mathbb EF\cdot\mathbb EG$.  (c) For the Gibbs measure $\mu_A\propto e^A$ of any energy $A$ on $\{-1,1\}^M$ and any two sites $i,j$, the average over four replicas of $(\sigma^1_i-\sigma^2_i)(\sigma^1_j-\sigma^2_j)(\sigma^3_i-\sigma^4_i)(\sigma^3_j-\sigma^4_j)$ is $4(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle\langle\sigma_j\rangle)^2$.  (d) The disorder average of the Gibbs average of a finite combination of observables is the combination of the averages.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) Check the product-set formula for the law of the restriction (\texttt{Measure.pi\_eq}): the preimage of $\prod_rs_r$ is $\prod_{r'}s'_{r'}$ with $s'_{\rho(r)}=s_r$ and $s'_{r'}=\mathrm{univ}$ off the range.  (b) Identify $\{-1,1\}^{m+n}$ with a product through the sum-type equivalence and apply Fubini.  (c) By (b) the average is $(\mathbb E_{\mu\otimes\mu}(x_i-y_i)(x_j-y_j))^2$ and the inner average is $2(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle\langle\sigma_j\rangle)$.  (d) Linearity of the integral over a finite space and of the outer integral, with integrability from boundedness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Two calculus facts along the angle path]
\label{lem:sg-tal:second-order-calculus}
  \lean{Real.sub_le_of_deriv_le_Icc,
        Real.abs_sub_le_of_exists_hasDerivAt_Icc,
        Real.abs_sub_sub_half_le_of_second_order}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Proposition~1.8.1: $|\nu(f)-\nu_0(f)-\nu_0'(f)|\le\sup_t|\nu_t''(f)|$}
(a) If $\varphi'=\sin s\cos s\,D$ with $|D|\le K$ on $[0,\pi/2]$ and $0\le a\le b\le\pi/2$, then $|\varphi(b)-\varphi(a)|\le\frac K2(\sin^2b-\sin^2a)$.  (b) If $\Psi'=\sin s\cos s\,G$ and $G'=\sin s\cos s\,D$ with $|D|\le K$ on $[0,\pi/2]$ then $|\Psi(\pi/2)-\Psi(0)-\frac12G(\pi/2)|\le K/4$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) $\varphi-\frac K2\sin^2$ is non-increasing on $[a,b]$ and $\varphi+\frac K2\sin^2$ is non-decreasing, because the derivative of $\sin^2$ is $2\sin s\cos s\ge0$.  (b) By (a) with $b=\pi/2$, $|G(s)-G(\pi/2)|\le\frac K2\cos^2s\le\frac K2$ for $s\in[0,\pi/2]$; apply the one-derivative form of the fencing lemma to $\Psi$ with $C=G(\pi/2)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The replica pairs of Eq.~(1.152) and Hoelder with two deviations]
\label{lem:sg-tal:replica-holder}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.exists_replica_pair_weights,
        SpinGlasses.sk_replica_holder,
        SpinGlasses.sk_replica_holder_dev_dev,
        SpinGlasses.abs_sk_cavity_angle_derivative_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Proposition~1.8.1: ``bound $\nu_t'(f)$ using (1.151), (1.154) and Hoelder's inequality''; iteration for $\nu_t''$}
Fix $n$.
