(a) The three families of replica pairs in Eq.~(1.152) (the pairs $\ell<\ell'\le n$ with weight $1$, the pairs $(\ell,n+1)$ with weight $n$ and the pair $(n+1,n+2)$ with weight $n(n+1)/2$) form a finite weighted list: there are an index type, nonnegative weights $c_j$ with $\sum_jc_j=2n^2$ and pairs $(a_j,b_j)$ of distinct replicas such that every sum $\sum_{\ell<\ell'}D(\tau_\ell,\tau_{\ell'})+n\sum_\ell D(\tau_\ell,\tau_{n+1})+\frac{n(n+1)}2D(\tau_{n+1},\tau_{n+2})$ equals $\sum_jc_jD(\tau_{a_j},\tau_{b_j})$.  (b) For any set $R$ of replicas, nonnegative observables $A,B$ of $R$ and conjugate $p,r$: $\nu(AB)\le\nu(A^p)^{1/p}\nu(B^r)^{1/r}$.  (c) If $f$ is an observable of $n$ replicas, $\rho:\{1,\dots,n\}\to R$ is injective and $a\ne b$, $c\ne d$ are replicas, then $\nu(|f(\sigma_\rho)|\,|R_{ab}-q|\,|R_{cd}-q|)\le\nu(|f|^p)^{1/p}\,\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^{2r})^{1/r}$.  (d) For $s\in[0,\pi/2]$ the angle derivative of Eq.~(1.151) satisfies $|\ldots|\le2\beta^2e^{4(n+2)^2\beta^2}\,\nu(|f|\,W)$, where $W=\sum_jc_j|R_{a_jb_j}-q|$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) Take the index type $\mathrm{FinPairs}\,n\oplus(\mathrm{Fin}\,n\oplus\mathrm{Unit})$ and match the three sums.  (b) Hoelder for the iterated average (\texttt{integral\_integral\_mul\_le\_Lp\_mul\_Lq}); the measurability of the inner averages is Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}(d)'s companion \texttt{measurable\_integral\_pi\_count\_tilted}.  (c) Apply (b) with $A=|f\circ\rho|$, $B=|R_{ab}-q||R_{cd}-q|$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}(a) $\nu(A^p)=\nu(|f|^p)$.  Since $(xy)^r\le\frac12(x^{2r}+y^{2r})$, $\nu(B^r)\le\frac12(\nu(|R_{ab}-q|^{2r})+\nu(|R_{cd}-q|^{2r}))$ and each term is a two-replica average (\texttt{integral\_pi\_count\_tilted\_pair}).  (d) The bound $|U|\le2\beta^2W$ for the kernel combination (Lemma~1.6.3's algebra) and the domination of Lemma~1.6.4 for the nonnegative observable $|f|W$ on $n+2$ replicas.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.8.1, Eq.~(1.215): first order]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-181-first}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:replica-holder}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_nu_sub_nu_zero_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_nu_sub_nu_zero_le_two,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_nu_sub_nu_zero_le_of_abs_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Proposition~1.8.1, Eq.~(1.215); the case $\tau_1=\tau_2=2$ and $\tau_1=\infty,\ \tau_2=1$}
Let $q\in[0,1]$, $f$ an observable of $n$ replicas of the $(N+1)$-spin system, $\nu$ the SK average and $\nu_0$ the decoupled average (cavity path at angle $\pi/2$).  For conjugate exponents $p,r$,
\[
|\nu(f)-\nu_0(f)|\le2n^2\beta^2e^{4(n+2)^2\beta^2}\,\nu(|f|^p)^{1/p}\,\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^r)^{1/r},
\]
in particular $\le2n^2\beta^2e^{4(n+2)^2\beta^2}\nu(f^2)^{1/2}\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)^{1/2}$ and, if $|f|\le M_f$, $\le2n^2\beta^2e^{4(n+2)^2\beta^2}M_f\,\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Integrate the bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-holder}(d) along $[0,\pi/2]$ (fencing lemma), use $\nu_{s=0}=\nu$ and apply Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-holder}(b) with $\sum c_j=2n^2$; for $\tau_1=\infty$ bound $|f|\le M_f$ pointwise.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.8.1, Eq.~(1.216): second order]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-181-second}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:second-order-calculus, lem:sg-tal:replica-holder, thm:sg-tal:prop-181-first}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_abs_lifted_mul_dev_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_nu_sub_nu_zero_sub_deriv_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_nu_sub_nu_zero_sub_deriv_le_two}
  \leanok
\talsource{Proposition~1.8.1, Eq.~(1.216)}
With the notation of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-181-first} let $\nu_0'(f)=\nu_0\bigl(f\cdot(\sum_{\ell<\ell'}K_{\ell\ell'}-n\sum_\ell K_{\ell,n+1}+\frac{n(n+1)}2K_{n+1,n+2})\bigr)$ with $K_{\ell\ell'}=\beta^2\varepsilon_\ell\varepsilon_{\ell'}(R^-_{\ell\ell'}-q)$ (Eq.~(1.151) at the decoupled endpoint).  For conjugate $p,r$,
\[
|\nu(f)-\nu_0(f)-\nu_0'(f)|\le4n^2(n+2)^2\beta^4e^{4(n+4)^2\beta^2}\,\nu(|f|^p)^{1/p}\,\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^{2r})^{1/r},
\]
in particular for $p=r=2$ with $\nu(f^2)^{1/2}\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^4)^{1/2}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Write $\Psi(s)=\nu_s(f)$.  By Eq.~(1.151) $\Psi'=\sin s\cos s\,G$ with $G(s)$ the angle form of $\nu_s'(f)$, which is the $(n+2)$-replica average of $F_2=f\cdot(\text{kernel combination})$ (the replicas $\{1,\dots,n,n+1,n+2\}$ identified with $\mathrm{Fin}(n+2)$ by an equivalence).  Applying Eq.~(1.151) again to $F_2$ gives $G'=\sin s\cos s\,D$ with $D$ the $(n+4)$-replica average of $F_2\cdot(\text{kernel combination})$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-holder}(d) for $F_2$, $|D|\le2\beta^2e^{4(n+4)^2\beta^2}\nu(|F_2|W)$, and $|F_2|\le2\beta^2|f|\sum_jc_j|R-q|$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-holder}(a)), so by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-holder}(c) summed over the $2n^2\cdot2(n+2)^2$ pairs of pairs, $|D|\le16n^2(n+2)^2\beta^4e^{4(n+4)^2\beta^2}\nu(|f|^p)^{1/p}\nu(|R-q|^{2r})^{1/r}$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:second-order-calculus}(b) gives the claim with $\Psi(0)=\nu(f)$, $\Psi(\pi/2)=\nu_0(f)$ and $\nu_0'(f)=-\frac12G(\pi/2)$ (the sign and the factor from $dt/ds=-2\sin s\cos s$, and $K^-$ differs from the kernel of Eq.~(1.151) by a constant that cancels because the weights sum to $0$).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Part B: Lemma~1.8.4}

\begin{lemma}[The decoupled average of sign monomials]
\label{lem:sg-tal:decoupled-monomials}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_endpoint_replicas,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_endpoint_quartic,
        exists_finset_prod_eq_quartic}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Lemma~1.8.2: ``by Lemma~1.6.2''}
(a) Lemma~1.6.2 holds for an arbitrary finite set $R$ of replicas: $\nu_0(f^-\prod_{r\in I}\varepsilon_r)=\mathbb E\tanh^{|I|}Y\cdot\nu_0^-(f^-)$ for every observable $f^-$ of the old spins.  (b) If $x\ne y$ and $a\ne b$ are replicas then $\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b=\prod_{r\in I}\varepsilon_r$ for a set $I$ with $|I|=4-2c$, where $c$ is the number of coincidences among $x,y,a,b$; hence $\nu_0(f^-\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b)=\mathbb E\tanh^{4-2c}Y\cdot\nu_0^-(f^-)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) Reindex the replicas by $\mathrm{Fin}(|R|)$ (\texttt{integral\_pi\_count\_tilted\_reindex}) and use Lemma~1.6.2 for $\mathrm{Fin}\,n$.  (b) Case split on the possible coincidences ($x=a$, $x=b$, $y=a$, $y=b$; seven consistent cases) using $\varepsilon^2=1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Algebra of the rectangle]
\label{lem:sg-tal:rect-algebra}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.rect_coeff,
        SpinGlasses.tanh_fourth_sub_two_add_one_mem_Icc,
        MeasureTheory.integrable_tanh_pow,
        sum_filter_lt_add_swap}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Lemma~1.8.4: the coefficient $b(2)-2b(1)+b(0)$, and the remark $1-2q+\hat q=\mathbb E\,\mathrm{ch}^{-4}Y$}
(a) Let $m_0,m_2,m_4$ be reals, $x_1,x_2,y_1,y_2$ replicas and $a\ne b$.  With $\delta_u=1_{u=a}+1_{u=b}\in\{0,1\}$ one has
$\sum_{u,v}\pm m_{4-2(\delta_{x_u}+\delta_{y_v})}=(m_0-2m_2+m_4)(\delta_{x_1}-\delta_{x_2})(\delta_{y_1}-\delta_{y_2})$ (signs $+,-,-,+$).  (b) For every $X:\iota\times\iota\to\mathbb R$ on a finite linear order, $\sum_{a<b}(X_{ab}+X_{ba})=\sum_{a,b}X_{ab}-\sum_aX_{aa}$.  (c) For $w,h\in\mathbb R$: $\mathbb E\tanh^4(wz+h)-2\mathbb E\tanh^2(wz+h)+1=\mathbb E(1-\tanh^2(wz+h))^2\in[0,1]$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) $m_{4-2k}$ with $k=\delta+\delta'\in\{0,1,2\}$ equals $m_4-(\delta+\delta')(m_4-m_2)+\delta\delta'(m_4-2m_2+m_0)$ for $\delta,\delta'\in\{0,1\}$ (check the three values); since $\sum_u\pm1=0$ only the last term survives.  (b) The off-diagonal symmetric sum is twice the ordered half.  (c) Expand the square and use $0\le1-\tanh^2\le1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.8.4, Eq.~(1.237): the rectangular combination]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-184}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:decoupled-monomials, lem:sg-tal:rect-algebra, thm:sg-tal:prop-181-second}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_rect_monomial,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_deriv_zero_rect}
  \leanok
\talsource{Lemma~1.8.4, Eq.~(1.237)}
Let $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, and let $i_1,i_2$ be replicas distinct from the replicas $j_1,j_2$ among $n$ replicas.  For every observable $f^-$ of the first $N$ spins of the $n$ replicas, with $f=f^-\cdot(\varepsilon_{i_1}-\varepsilon_{i_2})(\varepsilon_{j_1}-\varepsilon_{j_2})$,
\[
\nu_0'(f)=\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)\,\nu_0^-\bigl(f^-\,(R^-_{i_1j_1}-R^-_{i_1j_2}-R^-_{i_2j_1}+R^-_{i_2j_2})\bigr),
\]
with $\nu_0'$ as in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-181-second}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Expand $\nu_0'(f)$ over the pairs $(a,b)$ of the $n+2$ replicas and over the four monomials $\pm\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:decoupled-monomials}(b), $\nu_0(f^-\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y\,\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b(R^-_{ab}-q))=m_{|I|}\nu_0^-(f^-(R^-_{ab}-q))$, and by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-algebra}(a) the sum over $(x,y)$ has coefficient $(1-2q+\hat q)(\vartheta_i(a)+\vartheta_i(b))(\vartheta_j(a)+\vartheta_j(b))$ with $\vartheta_i(r)=1_{r=i_1}-1_{r=i_2}$.  Since $\vartheta_i\vartheta_j=0$ and $\vartheta$ vanishes on the two extra replicas, only $\vartheta_i(a)\vartheta_j(b)+\vartheta_i(b)\vartheta_j(a)$ with $a,b\le n$ survives; the sum over $a<b$ is then $\sum_{a,b}\vartheta_i(a)\vartheta_j(b)(\cdot)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-algebra}(b)), which is the four-term combination.  The constants $-q$ cancel since $1-1-1+1=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Decoupled values for the rectangle]
\label{lem:sg-tal:rect-decoupled}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:decoupled-monomials}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_rect_zero,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_rect_sq}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Proposition~1.8.5: $\nu_0(f^*)=0$ and Eq.~(1.242)}
With four replicas: (a) $\nu_0(f^-(\varepsilon_1-\varepsilon_2)(\varepsilon_3-\varepsilon_4))=0$ for every observable $f^-$ of the old spins; (b) $\nu_0(((\varepsilon_1-\varepsilon_2)(\varepsilon_3-\varepsilon_4))^2)=4(1-2q+\hat q)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) The four monomials $\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y$ each have decoupled average $q\,\nu_0^-(f^-)$ and the signs sum to $0$.  (b) $((\varepsilon_1-\varepsilon_2)(\varepsilon_3-\varepsilon_4))^2=4(1-\varepsilon_1\varepsilon_2)(1-\varepsilon_3\varepsilon_4)$ has decoupled average $4(1-q-q+\hat q)$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Part C: Proposition~1.8.5 and Corollary~1.8.6}

\begin{lemma}[Bookkeeping for $\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)$]
\label{lem:sg-tal:rect-structure}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_rect_sq_eq_last_site,
        SpinGlasses.sk_replica_avg_calculus,
        SpinGlasses.sk_rect_nu_structure,
        SpinGlasses.rect_sq_le,
        SpinGlasses.rect_pow_four_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_rect_moment_le,
        SpinGlasses.sq_sign_rect_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_rect_nu_bounds,
        SpinGlasses.sk_dev_moment_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_abs_dev_le}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Proposition~1.8.5, Eqs.~(1.239)--(1.242) and the estimates $\nu(|R^--q|^k)=O(k)$}
Let $a_i=(\sigma^1_i-\sigma^2_i)(\sigma^3_i-\sigma^4_i)$, $\mathrm{rect}=R_{13}-R_{14}-R_{23}+R_{24}=M^{-1}\sum_ia_i$ and $\mathrm{rect}^-=\mathrm{rect}-a_N/M$.  Then (a) $\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)=\nu(a_N\,\mathrm{rect})$ (site symmetry); (b) $\nu(a_N\mathrm{rect})=\nu(f^*)+\nu(a_N^2)/M$ with $f^*=a_N\mathrm{rect}^-$, and $\nu((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)=\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)(1-2/M)+\nu(a_N^2)/M^2$; (c) $0\le\nu(a_N^2)\le16$.  If $\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)\le K_1/M$ and $\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^4)\le K_2/M^2$ then (d) $\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)\le16K_1/M$, $\nu((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)\le(16K_1+16)/M$, $\nu(f^{*2})\le(512K_1+512)/M$ and $\nu((\mathrm{rect}^-)^4)\le(2048K_2+2048)/M^2$; and $\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)\le(K_1+1)/(2\sqrt M)$.  (e) Both hypotheses of (d) hold with $K_1,K_2$ depending only on $\beta_0$, uniformly in $|\beta|\le\beta_0<1/2$ (Eq.~(1.88)).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) Swapping sites $i$ and $N$ preserves $\mathrm{rect}$ and exchanges $a_i$ and $a_N$ (\texttt{sk\_replica\_site\_perm}); average over $i$.  (b) $\mathrm{rect}=\mathrm{rect}^-+a_N/M$ pointwise; expand.  (c) $|a_N|\le4$.  (d) $\mathrm{rect}=\sum\pm(R_{ab}-q)$ over four pairs of replicas, $(u_1-u_2-u_3+u_4)^m\le4^{m-1}\sum u_k^m$ for $m=2,4$ and each pair has the same average (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}(a)); then $(x-y)^4\le8(x^4+y^4)$ and $|a_N|\le4$.  For the last claim $|x|\le\frac{\sqrt M}2x^2+\frac1{2\sqrt M}$.  (e) Eq.~(1.88) as in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:overlap-moments-all}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The decoupled comparisons for the rectangle]
\label{lem:sg-tal:rect-comparisons}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:prop-181-first, thm:sg-tal:prop-181-second, thm:sg-tal:lemma-184, lem:sg-tal:rect-decoupled, lem:sg-tal:rect-structure}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_rect_nu_decoupled}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Proposition~1.8.5: the applications of (1.216), (1.237) and (1.215)}
Let $c_2=1-2q+\hat q$, $\mathrm{rect}^-$ and $f^*$ as above.  There is a decoupled average $\nu_0$ such that
(a) $|\nu(f^*)-\beta^2c_2\,\nu_0((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)|\le2304\beta^4e^{256\beta^2}\nu(f^{*2})^{1/2}\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^4)^{1/2}$,
(b) $|\nu((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)-\nu_0((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)|\le32\beta^2e^{144\beta^2}\nu((\mathrm{rect}^-)^4)^{1/2}\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)^{1/2}$,
(c) $|\nu(a_N^2)-4c_2|\le512\beta^2e^{144\beta^2}\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) Eq.~(1.216) with $n=4$, $p=r=2$ and $f=f^*=\mathrm{rect}^-\cdot(\varepsilon_1-\varepsilon_2)(\varepsilon_3-\varepsilon_4)$; $\nu_0(f^*)=0$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-decoupled}(a) and $\nu_0'(f^*)=\beta^2c_2\nu_0((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)$ by Lemma~1.8.4 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-184}) since $\mathrm{rect}^-=R^-_{13}-R^-_{14}-R^-_{23}+R^-_{24}$.  (b) Eq.~(1.215) with $\tau_1=\tau_2=2$ for $(\mathrm{rect}^-)^2$.  (c) Eq.~(1.215) with $\tau_1=\infty$, $|a_N^2|\le16$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-decoupled}(b).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.8.5, Eq.~(1.238)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-185}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:rect-structure, lem:sg-tal:rect-comparisons}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.second_moment_assembly,
        SpinGlasses.rect_error_arith,
        SpinGlasses.sk_rect_second_moment_aux,
        SpinGlasses.sk_rect_second_moment}
  \leanok
\talsource{Proposition~1.8.5, Eq.~(1.238)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $h\in\mathbb R$.  There is $K>0$ such that for $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q$ and every $N$, in the $(N+1)$-spin system with $M=N+1$, $c_2=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)-2q+1$,
\[
\Bigl|\nu\Bigl(\Bigl(\tfrac{(\boldsymbol\sigma^1-\boldsymbol\sigma^2)\cdot(\boldsymbol\sigma^3-\boldsymbol\sigma^4)}{M}\Bigr)^2\Bigr)-\frac{4c_2}{M(1-\beta^2c_2)}\Bigr|\le\frac K{M^{3/2}}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Put $X=\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)$, $P_f=\nu(f^*)$, $P_a=\nu(a_N^2)$, $Z_0=\nu_0((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)$, $Z_1=\nu((\mathrm{rect}^-)^2)$ and $\lambda=\beta^2c_2\in[0,\frac14]$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-algebra}(c)).  Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-structure}(a)--(b) give $X=P_f+P_a/M$ and $Z_1=X-2X/M+P_a/M^2$; Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-comparisons} gives $P_f\approx\lambda Z_0$, $Z_0\approx Z_1$ and $P_a\approx4c_2$ with the errors $e_1,e_2,e_4$, and $|X|\le e_3$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-structure}(d).  Solving, $(1-\lambda)X=(P_f-\lambda Z_0)+\lambda(Z_0-Z_1)-2\lambda X/M+\lambda P_a/M^2+P_a/M$, so $|X-\frac{4c_2}{M(1-\lambda)}|\le\frac43(e_1+\frac{e_2}4+\frac{e_3}{2M}+\frac4{M^2}+\frac{e_4}M)$ since $1-\lambda\ge\frac34$.  With $\sqrt M=s$ the bounds of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-structure}(d),(e) make every term at most a constant times $s^{-3}$ (e.g.\ $e_1\le c\sqrt{(512K_1+512)/M}\sqrt{K_2/M^2}$).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Corollary~1.8.6, Eq.~(1.244)]
\label{cor:sg-tal:corollary-186}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:prop-185, lem:sg-tal:rect-comparisons, lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_replica_avg_finset_sum,
        SpinGlasses.sk_rect_sq_eq_diag_add_offdiag,
        SpinGlasses.sk_nu_a_prod_eq_cov_sq,
        SpinGlasses.pair_variance_arith,
        SpinGlasses.sk_truncated_pair_variance}
  \leanok
\talsource{Corollary~1.8.6, Eq.~(1.244)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $h\in\mathbb R$.  There is $K>0$ such that for $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q$, every $N$ and any two distinct sites $i\ne j$ of the $(N+1)$-spin system, with $M=N+1$ and $c_2$ as above,
\[
\Bigl|\mathbb E\bigl(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle\langle\sigma_j\rangle\bigr)^2-\frac{\beta^2c_2^2}{M(1-\beta^2c_2)}\Bigr|\le\frac K{M^{3/2}}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
By site symmetry (swaps of sites, \texttt{sk\_replica\_site\_perm}) $\nu(a_ka_l)$ depends only on whether $k=l$, so $\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)=M^{-2}\sum_{k,l}\nu(a_ka_l)=\nu(a_N^2)/M+\frac{M-1}M\nu(a_ia_j)$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:replica-marginals}(c), $\nu(a_ia_j)=4V$ with $V=\mathbb E(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle-\langle\sigma_i\rangle\langle\sigma_j\rangle)^2$.  Solving, $V=(M\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)-\nu(a_N^2))/(4(M-1))$.  Insert $\nu(\mathrm{rect}^2)=\frac{4c_2}{M(1-\lambda)}+O(M^{-3/2})$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-185}) and $\nu(a_N^2)=4c_2+O(M^{-1/2})$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-comparisons}(c) with Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:rect-structure}(d)): the main term is $\frac{c_2\lambda}{(1-\lambda)M}$ and the elementary estimate \texttt{pair\_variance\_arith} bounds the remainder by $\frac2{3M^2}+\frac E2+\frac e{2M}$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Part D: General Lemma~1.8.2 and the AT Matrix System}

\begin{lemma}[Endpoint pair covariance and coincidences]
\label{lem:sg-tal:endpoint-pair-cov}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.skCavityCoincidences,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_coincidences_le_two,
        SpinGlasses.skCavityBUnscaled,
        SpinGlasses.skCavityBCoef,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_b_unscaled_eq,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_endpoint_pair_cov}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Lemma~1.8.2, Eqs.~(1.229)--(1.230)}
Let $x \ne y$ and $a \ne b$ be replica indices in $\mathrm{Fin}\,n$, and let $c = 1_{x=a} + 1_{x=b} + 1_{y=a} + 1_{y=b} \in \{0, 1, 2\}$ be their number of coincidences.  Let $b(c) = \beta^2 (\mathbb E\tanh^{4-2c} Y - q^2)$.  For every bounded observable $G(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^-)$ of the old spins, the cavity decoupled average along the path satisfies:
\[
\nu_0\bigl(G(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^-)\,(\varepsilon_x \varepsilon_y - q)\,\varepsilon_a \varepsilon_b\bigr) = (\mathbb E\tanh^{4-2c} Y - q^2)\,\nu_0(G(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^-)).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Apply \texttt{sk\_cavity\_decoupled\_endpoint\_quartic} and expand the monomial product using $\varepsilon_r^2 = 1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[General Lemma~1.8.2, Eq.~(1.230): cavity derivative of pair observable]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:endpoint-pair-cov}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.SKCavityReplicaPair,
        SpinGlasses.skCavityReplicaPairMap,
        SpinGlasses.skCavityReplicaPairWeight,
        SpinGlasses.skCavityReplicaPairCoincidences,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_deriv_zero_pair}
  \leanok
\talsource{Lemma~1.8.2, Eq.~(1.230)}
Let $x \ne y$ in $\mathrm{Fin}\,n$ and let $f^-$ be an observable of the old spins.  The cavity path derivative at $t=0$ of $(\varepsilon_x \varepsilon_y - q) f^-$ evaluates to:
\[
\nu_0'((\varepsilon_x \varepsilon_y - q) f^-) = \sum_{j \in \mathrm{SKCavityReplicaPair}\,n} w_j \cdot b(c_j) \cdot \nu_0\bigl(f^- (R^-_{a_j, b_j} - q)\bigr),
\]
where $w_j$ are the replica pair weights and $c_j$ counts coincidences with $\{x, y\}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Differentiate along the cavity path via \texttt{hasDerivAt\_integral\_sk\_cavity\_path\_square}, apply the decoupled endpoint identity \texttt{sk\_cavity\_decoupled\_endpoint\_pair\_cov} to each monomial $\varepsilon_a \varepsilon_b$, and collect the terms into the three replica families.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The $3\times3$ AT matrix, universal eigenvectors, and eigenvalues]
\label{thm:sg-tal:at-matrix}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182}
  \lean{Matrix.atMatrix,
        Matrix.atEigenvectorOne,
        Matrix.atEigenvectorTwo,
        Matrix.atMatrix_transpose_mulVec_one,
        Matrix.atMatrix_transpose_mulVec_two,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_eigenvalue_one_eq,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_eigenvalue_two_eq}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Eqs.~(1.231)--(1.236)}
Let $b(2), b(1), b(0)$ be elements of any commutative ring $K$ (in the application $K = \mathbb R$) and let $M$ be the matrix of Eq.~(1.234):
\[
M = \begin{pmatrix}
b(2) & -4 b(1) & 3 b(0) \\
b(1) & b(2) - 2 b(1) - 3 b(0) & 6 b(0) - 3 b(1) \\
b(0) & 4 b(1) - 8 b(0) & b(2) - 8 b(1) + 10 b(0)
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Then:
(a) $\mathbf{v}_1 = (1, -2, 1)^T$ is an eigenvector of $M^T$ with eigenvalue $\lambda_1 = b(2) - 2 b(1) + b(0) = \beta^2 (1 - 2 q + \hat{q})$ (Eq.~1.235).
(b) $\mathbf{v}_2 = (1, -4, 3)^T$ is an eigenvector of $M^T$ with eigenvalue $\lambda_2 = b(2) - 4 b(1) + 3 b(0) = \beta^2 (1 - 4 q + 3 \hat{q})$ (Eq.~1.236).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The two eigenvector identities are polynomial identities in $b(2), b(1), b(0)$, proved by coordinate expansion over an arbitrary commutative ring (module \texttt{ReplicaMatrix3x3}, which does not mention spin glasses).  Only the expressions of the eigenvalues in $(\beta, q, \hat q)$ use the specific values $b(c) = \beta^2(\mathbb E\tanh^{4-2c}Y - q^2)$ and $q = \mathbb E\tanh^2 Y, \hat{q} = \mathbb E\tanh^4 Y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The $3\times3$ AT covariance equations and decoupled solutions]
\label{thm:sg-tal:at-covariance-equations}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:at-matrix}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.skAtSource,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_source_dot_eigenvector_one,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_source_dot_eigenvector_two,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_covariance_equations_iff,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_system_dot_eigenvector_one,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_system_dot_eigenvector_two,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_system_solve_one,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_system_solve_two,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_system_solve_one_beta}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Eqs.~(1.231)--(1.236)}
Let $\mathbf{v} = (U, V, W)^T$ and $\mathbf{S} = \frac{1}{M}(1 - q^2, q - q^2, \hat{q} - q^2)^T$.
The three covariance equations of Talagrand (1.231)--(1.233):
\begin{align*}
U &= \frac{1 - q^2}{M} + b(2) U - 4 b(1) V + 3 b(0) W + \mathcal{R}_0, \\
V &= \frac{q - q^2}{M} + b(1) U + (b(2) - 2 b(1) - 3 b(0)) V + (6 b(0) - 3 b(1)) W + \mathcal{R}_1, \\
W &= \frac{\hat{q} - q^2}{M} + b(0) U + (4 b(1) - 8 b(0)) V + (b(2) - 8 b(1) + 10 b(0)) W + \mathcal{R}_2,
\end{align*}
are equivalent to the vector system $\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{S} + M \mathbf{v} + \boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}$.
Projecting along the transposed universal eigenvectors $\mathbf{v}_1 = (1, -2, 1)^T$ and $\mathbf{v}_2 = (1, -4, 3)^T$ decouples the modes with eigenvalues $\lambda_1 = \beta^2(1 - 2q + \hat{q})$ and $\lambda_2 = \beta^2(1 - 4q + 3\hat{q})$. When $\lambda_1, \lambda_2 \ne 1$, the exact solutions are:
\begin{align*}
(U - 2 V + W) - \frac{1 - 2q + \hat{q}}{M (1 - \lambda_1)} &= \frac{\mathbf{v}_1^T \boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}}{1 - \lambda_1}, \\
(U - 4 V + 3 W) - \frac{1 - 4q + 3\hat{q}}{M (1 - \lambda_2)} &= \frac{\mathbf{v}_2^T \boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}}{1 - \lambda_2}.
\end{align*}
In particular, multiplying the first solution by $4$ recovers the leading term $4 (1 - 2q + \hat{q}) / (M (1 - \lambda_1))$ of Proposition~1.8.5.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Follows by algebraic projection using the universal eigenvectors $\mathbf{v}_1$ and $\mathbf{v}_2$, the eigenvalue formulas, and fraction cancellation.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Part E: Combinations of pair covariances, Lemma~1.8.9 and the algebra of the $T$-basis}

\emph{Status.}  Proved here (all \texttt{sorry}-free): the linear-combination form of
Lemma~1.8.2, the sum form of Lemma~1.8.9 (Eq.~(1.253)), and the pure algebra needed for
Propositions~1.8.7--1.8.12 (coefficients of Eq.~(1.264), $\hat q\le q$, the decomposition (1.246)).
The Gibbs-mean trick for $T_{1,2}^2$ is also proved (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:gibbs-mean-trick}).
Proposition~1.8.7 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-187}) and the exact $T$-basis form of the overlap covariances (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:overlap-covariances-T}) and the separate zero identities of Proposition~1.8.8 and Eq.~(1.257) (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:T-orthogonality}) are proved.
Propositions~1.8.10, 1.8.11 and Theorem~1.8.12 are proved in Part~F by deriving and solving the covariance system.

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.8.2 for a linear combination of pair covariances]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182-combo}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_endpoint_pair_cov_combo,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_deriv_zero_pair_combo}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Lemma~1.8.2, Eq.~(1.230); used in Lemma~1.8.9}
Let $\iota$ be a finite index set, $c:\iota\to\mathbb R$, and $x_m\ne y_m$ replicas in
$\mathrm{Fin}\,n$ for $m\in\iota$.  Let $F$ be a function of the old spins.  The cavity path
derivative at the decoupled endpoint of
$F\sum_m c_m(\varepsilon_{x_m}\varepsilon_{y_m}-q)$ is the sum over the three replica families $j=(a,b)$
of Eq.~(1.152), with the weights $w_j$, of
$\bigl(\sum_m c_m\, b(\mathrm{coinc}_m(j))\bigr)\,\nu_0\bigl(F\,(R^-_{ab}-q)\bigr)$, where
$\mathrm{coinc}_m(j)=|\{x_m,y_m\}\cap\{a,b\}|$ and $b(c)=\beta^2(\mathbb E\tanh^{4-2c}Y-q^2)$.
Lemma~1.8.2 is the case of one term with $c=1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The endpoint identity (Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:endpoint-pair-cov}) is linear in the pair
covariance, so the combination is handled by the finite-sum linearity of the three-level replica
average; the proof of Lemma~1.8.2 then goes through with the coefficient replaced by
$\sum_m c_m b(\mathrm{coinc}_m(j))$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The coefficients $c(\ell,\ell')$ of Lemma~1.8.9]
\label{lem:sg-tal:coincidences-sub}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_coincidences_sub_eq}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Lemma~1.8.9}
Let $i_1,i_2,k$ be distinct, $a\ne b$, and let $B:\mathbb N\to\mathbb R$.  With
$\vartheta(r)=1_{r=i_1}-1_{r=i_2}$ one has
$B(\mathrm{coinc}(i_1,k;a,b))-B(\mathrm{coinc}(i_2,k;a,b))=(\vartheta(a)+\vartheta(b))\cdot
\begin{cases}B(2)-B(1)&k\in\{a,b\},\\ B(1)-B(0)&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}$
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Finite case distinction on the equalities among $i_1,i_2,k,a,b$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.8.9, Eq.~(1.253), as a sum over the replica families]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-189}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182-combo, lem:sg-tal:coincidences-sub}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_deriv_zero_sub_third}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Lemma~1.8.9, Eq.~(1.253)}
For distinct replicas $i_1,i_2,k$ and a function $F$ of the old spins, the cavity path derivative at
the decoupled endpoint of $F(\varepsilon_{i_1}-\varepsilon_{i_2})\varepsilon_k$ equals
$\sum_j w_j(\vartheta(a_j)+\vartheta(b_j))\,d_j\,\nu_0\bigl(F(R^-_{a_jb_j}-q)\bigr)$, where
$d_j=b(2)-b(1)$ if $k\in\{a_j,b_j\}$ and $b(1)-b(0)$ otherwise.  Regrouping the sum by the
position of the pair gives the book's form (1.253), which is Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-189-displayed}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182-combo} with the two terms $(i_1,k)$ and $(i_2,k)$ and
coefficients $1,-1$ (the constants $-q$ cancel), then Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:coincidences-sub}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regrouping the replica families of Lemma~1.8.9, and moving one replica]
\label{lem:sg-tal:regroup-189}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sum_eq_three_add_sum_filter,
        SpinGlasses.card_filter_ne_three,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_replica_pair_sum_sub_third,
        SpinGlasses.integral_pi_count_tilted_replace_replica}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Lemma~1.8.9, computation of $c(\ell,\ell')$, and the last sentence of the proof}
Let $i_1,i_2,k$ be distinct elements of $\{1,\dots,n\}$ and $s=\{\ell:\ell\notin\{i_1,i_2,k\}\}$, so that
$|s|+3=n$.  (a) For a symmetric kernel $J$ on $n+2$ replicas, the sum over the three families of
Eq.~(1.152) (weights $1$, $-n$, $n(n+1)/2$) of $w_j(\vartheta(a_j)+\vartheta(b_j))\,d_j\,J(a_j,b_j)$,
with $\vartheta=1_{i_1}-1_{i_2}$, $d_j=b(2)-b(1)$ if $k\in\{a_j,b_j\}$ and $b(1)-b(0)$ otherwise, equals
$(b(2)-b(1))\bigl(J(i_1,k)-J(i_2,k)\bigr)+(b(1)-b(0))\Bigl(\sum_{\ell\in s}\bigl(J(i_1,\ell)-J(i_2,\ell)\bigr)
-n\bigl(J(i_1,n+1)-J(i_2,n+1)\bigr)\Bigr)$.
(b) If $F$ does not depend on replica $k$, $i\ne k$, and $x_0$ is a replica outside the range of
an injection $e$ of the $n$ replicas, then the Gibbs average of $F\cdot K(\sigma_i,\sigma_k)$ equals that of
$F\cdot K(\sigma_i,\sigma_{x_0})$ (the replica $k$ may be moved to a fresh replica).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) Write the family sum as the ordered-pair sum, the sum over $r$ of the pairs $(r,n+1)$ and the
pair $(n+1,n+2)$ (Eq.~(1.152) reindexing).  The last term vanishes since $\vartheta(n+1)=\vartheta(n+2)=0$;
the old--extra sum evaluates $\sum_r\vartheta(r)(\cdots)$ at $r=i_1,i_2$; for the old--old pairs
use that $J$ is symmetric to write the ordered half as the full double sum minus its diagonal, and
evaluate $\sum_a\vartheta(a)\sum_c d(a,c)J(a,c)$ at $a=i_1,i_2$ after splitting $c\in\{i_1,i_2,k\}\cup s$.
(b) Both averages equal the average over the $n$ replicas of $F\cdot K(\sigma_i,\sigma_k)$, by the marginal
along the injections $e$ and $e$ with the value at $k$ replaced by $x_0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.8.9, Eqs.~(1.253) and (1.254), displayed form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-189-displayed}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-189, lem:sg-tal:regroup-189, lem:sg-tal:t-basis-algebra}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_deriv_zero_sub_third_displayed,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_deriv_zero_sub_third_of_indep}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Lemma~1.8.9, Eqs.~(1.253) and (1.254)}
Let $i_1,i_2,k$ be distinct replicas of $n$ replicas (the book's $1,2,3$), $F$ a function of the old spins, and
$J(x,y)=\nu_0\bigl(F\cdot(R^-_{xy}-q)\bigr)$ (the decoupled averages of Lemma~1.8.2, with
$R^-_{xy}=M^{-1}\sum_{i\le N}\sigma^x_i\sigma^y_i$).
(a) The cavity derivative at the decoupled endpoint satisfies
$$\nu_0'\bigl((\varepsilon_{i_1}-\varepsilon_{i_2})\varepsilon_kF\bigr)=(b(2)-b(1))\,\nu_0\bigl(F(R^-_{i_1k}-R^-_{i_2k})\bigr)
+(b(1)-b(0))\Bigl(\sum_{\ell\notin\{i_1,i_2,k\}}\nu_0\bigl(F(R^-_{i_1\ell}-R^-_{i_2\ell})\bigr)
-n\,\nu_0\bigl(F(R^-_{i_1,n+1}-R^-_{i_2,n+1})\bigr)\Bigr).$$
(b) If moreover $F$ does not depend on replica $k$, this equals
$$\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)\,\nu_0\bigl(F(R^-_{i_1k}-R^-_{i_2k})\bigr)+\beta^2(q-\hat q)\sum_{\ell\notin\{i_1,i_2,k\}}
\nu_0\bigl(F(R^-_{i_1\ell}-R^-_{i_2\ell}-R^-_{i_1,n+1}+R^-_{i_2,n+1})\bigr).$$
In Lean the derivative is the explicit cavity-path integral of Eq.~(1.152), and $J$ is given by its
defining integral.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) is Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-189} followed by the regrouping of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:regroup-189}(a)
($J$ is symmetric because the dot product is).  (b): by Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:regroup-189}(b),
$J(i,k)=J(i,n+1)$ for $i\in\{i_1,i_2\}$; apply Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:t-basis-algebra}(a) and the identities
$b(2)-4b(1)+3b(0)=\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)$, $b(1)-b(0)=\beta^2(q-\hat q)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Algebra of the $T$-basis]
\label{lem:sg-tal:t-basis-algebra}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_at_deriv_coef_split,
        SpinGlasses.sk_t_coeff_T_sq,
        SpinGlasses.sk_t_coeff_B_sq,
        MeasureTheory.integral_tanh_pow_four_le_sq,
        SpinGlasses.qhat_le_of_fixedPoint,
        SpinGlasses.three_quarters_le_one_sub_beta_sq_mul,
        SpinGlasses.dotProduct_div_sub_decomp}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Eqs.~(1.246), (1.254), (1.260), (1.264)}
(a) If the term of replica $3$ equals the term of replica $n+1$, then
$(b(2)-b(1))S_3+(b(1)-b(0))\bigl(\sum_{4\le\ell\le n}S_\ell-nS_{n+1}\bigr)=(b(2)-4b(1)+3b(0))S_3
+(b(1)-b(0))\sum_{4\le\ell\le n}(S_\ell-S_{n+1})$ (from (1.253) to (1.254)).
(b) $b(2)+4b(1)+b(0)-4(2b(1)+2b(0))+10b(0)=\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)$ and
$4b(1)+2b(0)-4(2b(0))=2\beta^2(2q+q^2-3\hat q)$ (the coefficients in (1.264)).
(c) $\hat q\le q$ for the fixed point $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2Y$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4Y$, and
$4\beta^2<1$, $0\le q$ imply $1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)\ge 3/4$.
(d) For vectors $x,y,\mathbf b$ and $N\ne0$,
$x\cdot y/N-q=T_{xy}+T_x+T_y+T$ with $T_{xy}=(x-\mathbf b)\cdot(y-\mathbf b)/N$,
$T_x=(x-\mathbf b)\cdot\mathbf b/N$, $T=\mathbf b\cdot\mathbf b/N-q$ (Eq.~(1.246)).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a), (b), (d) are polynomial identities (\texttt{ring}); (c) uses $\tanh^4\le\tanh^2$ and
monotonicity of the integral.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The Gibbs-mean trick for $T_{12}^2$ (proof of Proposition~1.8.7)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:gibbs-mean-trick}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_dev_mul_dev,
        SpinGlasses.integral_dev_pair_sq,
        SpinGlasses.integral_three_replicas_cov,
        SpinGlasses.integral_six_replicas_cov_sq,
        SpinGlasses.integral_dev_dot_sq_eq_six_replicas,
        SpinGlasses.integral_disorder_dev_dot_sq_eq_six_replicas}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proof of Proposition~1.8.7 (the technique ``replace each occurrence of $\mathbf b$ by a replica never used before'')}
Let $\mu$ be a Gibbs measure on $\{\pm1\}^M$ (the tilt of the counting measure by any energy $A$), $b_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle$ and $C_{ij}=\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle-b_ib_j$.  Then
\[
\Bigl\langle\Bigl(\tfrac1M\sum_i(\sigma^1_i-b_i)(\sigma^2_i-b_i)\Bigr)^2\Bigr\rangle
=\frac1{M^2}\sum_{i,j}C_{ij}^2=
\Bigl\langle\tfrac1M(\boldsymbol\sigma^1-\boldsymbol\sigma^2)\cdot(\boldsymbol\sigma^4-\boldsymbol\sigma^5)\;
\tfrac1M(\boldsymbol\sigma^1-\boldsymbol\sigma^3)\cdot(\boldsymbol\sigma^4-\boldsymbol\sigma^6)\Bigr\rangle,
\]
the left side over two independent replicas and the right side over six.  The left side is the Gibbs average of $T_{1,2}^2$ of Eq.~(1.245).  The same identity holds after averaging over any disorder $\eta$ with energies $E_\eta$ and means $b_\eta$.  The right side has the shape of Proposition~1.8.5 (with $f=R_{1,4}-R_{1,6}-R_{3,4}+R_{3,6}$ after relabelling), which is what the remaining proof of Proposition~1.8.7 will use.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Both sides reduce, after expanding the squares of the sums over sites, to $M^{-2}\sum_{i,j}C_{ij}^2$.  For two replicas the average factorizes (Fubini for two coordinates) into $C_{ij}\cdot C_{ij}$.  For six replicas, the integrand is a product of a function of replicas $1,2,3$ and the same function of replicas $4,5,6$, so it factorizes by independence of the two blocks of an i.i.d.\ vector; each block is $\langle(\sigma^1_i-\sigma^2_i)(\sigma^1_j-\sigma^3_j)\rangle=\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle-b_ib_j$ by expanding into four terms of at most two replicas each.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.8.7: the second moment of $T_{1,2}$]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-187}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:prop-185, thm:sg-tal:gibbs-mean-trick}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_rect_sq_eq_four_mul_dev_dot_sq,
        SpinGlasses.sk_dev_pair_second_moment}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Proposition~1.8.7, Eqs.~(1.247)--(1.248)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $h\in\mathbb R$.  There is $K>0$ such that for $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, every fixed point $q$ of Eq.~(1.74) and every $N$, with $M=N+1$, $\mathbf b=\langle\boldsymbol\sigma\rangle$ the Gibbs mean of the $(N+1)$-spin SK system and $T_{1,2}=(\boldsymbol\sigma^1-\mathbf b)\cdot(\boldsymbol\sigma^2-\mathbf b)/M$,
\[
\Bigl|\nu(T_{1,2}^2)-\frac{1-2q+\hat q}{M\,(1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q))}\Bigr|\le \frac{K}{M^{3/2}}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The book repeats the proof of Proposition~1.8.5 with six replicas.  Here it is a corollary of Proposition~1.8.5: for every Gibbs measure, $\langle((\boldsymbol\sigma^1-\boldsymbol\sigma^2)\cdot(\boldsymbol\sigma^3-\boldsymbol\sigma^4)/M)^2\rangle=4\langle T_{1,2}^2\rangle$ exactly, because both sides equal $4M^{-2}\sum_{i,j}C_{ij}^2$ (the cross terms of $\mathrm{rect}=T_{13}-T_{14}-T_{23}+T_{24}$ vanish and the four squares agree).  Average over the disorder and divide Proposition~1.8.5 by $4$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The overlap covariances in the $T$-basis, exact form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:overlap-covariances-T}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_overlap_sq_eq,
        SpinGlasses.integral_overlap_mul_overlap_eq,
        SpinGlasses.integral_overlap_mul_overlap_disjoint_eq}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Eq.~(1.246) and the last step of Theorem~1.8.12}
Let $\mu$ be a Gibbs measure on $\{\pm1\}^M$ with moments $m_{ij}=\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle$, means $b_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle$, $C_{ij}=m_{ij}-b_ib_j$ and $q\in\mathbb R$.  For independent replicas, with $R_{\ell\ell'}=M^{-1}\sum_i\sigma^\ell_i\sigma^{\ell'}_i$,
\begin{align*}
\langle(R_{12}-q)^2\rangle&=a+2B+c,\\
\langle(R_{12}-q)(R_{13}-q)\rangle&=B+c,\\
\langle(R_{12}-q)(R_{34}-q)\rangle&=c,
\end{align*}
where $a=M^{-2}\sum_{ij}C_{ij}^2=\langle T_{1,2}^2\rangle$, $B=M^{-2}\sum_{ij}C_{ij}b_ib_j=\langle T_1^2\rangle$ and $c=(M^{-1}\sum_ib_i^2-q)^2=T^2$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Expand the products of overlaps in coordinates; independent replicas make each monomial factor into moments $m_{ij}$, $b_i$ (average of a product over an i.i.d.\ vector), and $m_{ij}^2=C_{ij}^2+2C_{ij}b_ib_j+b_i^2b_j^2$.  The aggregate identities are equivalent to the vanishing cross terms of Proposition~1.8.8 and Eq.~(1.257), which are proved separately, one by one, in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:T-orthogonality}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Orthogonality of the $T$-basis (Proposition~1.8.8 and Eq.~(1.257))]
\label{thm:sg-tal:T-orthogonality}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sum_prod_weight_mul_linear_eq_zero,
        MeasureTheory.integral_pi_centered_replica_eq_zero,
        SpinGlasses.integral_spin_sub_mean,
        SpinGlasses.integral_dev_dot_dev_mul_eq_zero,
        SpinGlasses.integral_dev_dot_mean_mul_eq_zero,
        SpinGlasses.integral_T_pair_mul_T_pair_of_share,
        SpinGlasses.integral_T_pair_mul_T_pair_of_disjoint,
        SpinGlasses.integral_T_pair_mul_T_one,
        SpinGlasses.integral_T_pair_mul_const,
        SpinGlasses.integral_T_one_mul_T_one,
        SpinGlasses.integral_T_one_mul_const}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Proposition~1.8.8 and Eq.~(1.257)}
Let $\mu$ be a Gibbs measure on $\{\pm1\}^M$ with mean $\mathbf b$, and let $\boldsymbol\sigma^1,\boldsymbol\sigma^2,\dots$ be independent replicas.  Put $T_{st}=M^{-1}(\boldsymbol\sigma^s-\mathbf b)\cdot(\boldsymbol\sigma^t-\mathbf b)$, $T_s=M^{-1}(\boldsymbol\sigma^s-\mathbf b)\cdot\mathbf b$ and let $T$ be any constant (the book's $T=M^{-1}\mathbf b\cdot\mathbf b-q$ does not depend on the replicas).  Then
$\langle T_{st}T_{su}\rangle=\langle T_{st}T_{uv}\rangle=\langle T_{st}T_u\rangle=\langle T_{st}T\rangle=0$ for $t\notin\{s,u,v\}$, and $\langle T_sT_t\rangle=\langle T_sT\rangle=0$ for $s\ne t$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
In each product some replica occurs in one factor only (replica $t$ in the first group, replica $s$ in the second), and $\sigma^t-\mathbf b$ has Gibbs mean zero.  For independent replicas, $\langle H\cdot\sum_j z_j(\sigma^\ell)c_j\rangle=0$ whenever $\langle z_j\rangle=0$ and $H,c_j$ do not depend on $\sigma^\ell$ (sum over $\sigma^\ell$ first); this is the same mechanism as the parity vanishing in the proof of Theorem~1.7.11.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Part F: The covariance system and Theorem~1.8.12}

\emph{Status.}  Proved (all \texttt{sorry}-free).  The book reaches Theorem~1.8.12 through the
orthogonal basis $T_{\ell\ell'},T_\ell,T$ and four cavity expansions (Propositions~1.8.7, 1.8.10,
1.8.11).  Here the three covariances $U,V,W$ are obtained from one cavity expansion of
$\nu\bigl((R_{12}-q)(R_{ab}-q)\bigr)$ for the three pairs $(a,b)\in\{(1,2),(1,3),(3,4)\}$, i.e.\ the
system (1.231)--(1.233) is derived with explicit errors and solved with the algebra of Part~D and
Part~E; the values of $\nu(T_{1,2}^2)$, $\nu(T_1^2)$, $\nu(T^2)$ are then read off from the exact
$T$-basis identities of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:overlap-covariances-T}.

\begin{theorem}[One row of the cavity expansion: Taylor step, comparison and last-site term]
\label{thm:sg-tal:row-expansion}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182-combo}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_mul_eq_last_site_of_perm,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_pair_zero,
        SpinGlasses.sk_pair_taylor,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_structure,
        SpinGlasses.sk_old_spin_compare,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_last_site,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_conv}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, proofs of Propositions~1.8.5 and 1.8.11, Eqs.~(1.215)--(1.223)}
Let $x\ne y$ and $a\ne b$ be replicas of an $n$-replica $(N+1)$-spin system, $M=N+1$, $\varepsilon$ the
last spin and $R^-_{ab}=M^{-1}\sum_{i\le N}\sigma^a_i\sigma^b_i$.  Then:
(a) $\nu((R_{xy}-q)(R_{ab}-q))=\nu\bigl((R^-_{ab}-q)(\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q)\bigr)+M^{-1}\nu\bigl((\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q)\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b\bigr)$ (site symmetry);
(b) $\nu_0\bigl(F(\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q)\bigr)=0$ and, by Eq.~(1.216) with Lemma~1.8.2, $\nu(F(\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q))$ differs from $\sum_j w_j\,b(c_j)\,\mathbb E\langle F(R^-_j-q)\rangle_{\mathrm{old}}$ by at most $4n^2(n+2)^2\beta^4e^{4(n+4)^2\beta^2}\,\nu(f^2)^{1/2}\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^4)^{1/2}$;
(c) for an observable $G$ of the first $N$ spins, $|\nu(G)-\mathbb E\langle G\rangle_{\mathrm{old}}|\le 2m^2\beta^2e^{4(m+2)^2\beta^2}\nu(G^2)^{1/2}\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)^{1/2}$ (Eq.~(1.215));
(d) $|\nu((\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q)\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b)-b_0(c)|\le 4n^2\beta^2e^{4(n+2)^2\beta^2}\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)$;
(e) replacing $R^-$ by $R$ in $\nu((R^-_{pq}-q)(R^-_{rs}-q))$ costs at most $2\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)/M+M^{-2}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) is the site-symmetry argument of Proposition~1.8.5 for an arbitrary site-symmetric factor and the exact split $R_{ab}=R^-_{ab}+\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b/M$; (b) combines the decoupled endpoint (Lemma~1.6.2) with the second-order expansion of Proposition~1.8.1; (c) and (d) are Eq.~(1.215) with the Cauchy--Schwarz and the sup-norm H\"older pairs; (e) expands the product $(X-\delta)(Y-\delta')$ with $|\delta|,|\delta'|\le 1/M$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The covariance system (1.231)--(1.233) with explicit errors]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cov-system}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:row-expansion, thm:sg-tal:at-covariance-equations}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_row_equation,
        SpinGlasses.sk_nu_overlap_class,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_sum_zero_j,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_sum_one_j,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_sum_two_j,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cov_row_bound,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cov_system}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Eqs.~(1.231)--(1.236)}
Let $4\beta^2<1$, $q$ the fixed point, $\mathbb E\langle(R_{12}-q)^2\rangle\le K_1/M$ and $\mathbb E\langle(R_{12}-q)^4\rangle\le K_2/M^2$.  Let $U,V,W$ be $\nu((R_{12}-q)^2)$, $\nu((R_{12}-q)(R_{13}-q))$, $\nu((R_{12}-q)(R_{34}-q))$.  Then there is a remainder $\mathbf R\in\mathbb R^3$ with $|R_i|\le E=O(M^{-3/2})$ (explicit in $\beta,K_1,K_2,M$) such that $(U,V,W)^T=\mathbf S+\mathcal M\,(U,V,W)^T+\mathbf R$, where $\mathbf S=M^{-1}(1-q^2,q-q^2,\hat q-q^2)^T$ and $\mathcal M$ is the matrix of Eq.~(1.234).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:row-expansion} with $(x,y)=(1,2)$ and $(a,b)=(1,2),(1,3),(3,4)$.  Every term $\nu_{n+2}((R_{ab}-q)(R_j-q))$ of the sum over the replica families $j$ equals $U$, $V$ or $W$ according to whether the pairs $\{a,b\}$, $\{a_j,b_j\}$ coincide, share one replica or are disjoint (marginal of an i.i.d.\ replica vector along an injection).  Summing the signed weights over the $11$ families for $n=4$ gives the three rows of $\mathcal M$ (a finite computation, checked by \texttt{simp}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem~1.8.12, Eq.~(1.265)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:cov-system}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_at_scalar_solution,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_exact_errors,
        Matrix.dotProduct_system_of_transpose_eigenvector,
        SpinGlasses.cov_error_arith,
        SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_covariances_nu,
        SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_covariances}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Theorem~1.8.12, Eqs.~(1.248), (1.256), (1.262), (1.265)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$.  There is $K$ such that for $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, $q$ the fixed point, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q z+h)$ and all $N$, with $M=N+1$, $c=1-2q+\hat q$, $d=1-4q+3\hat q$, $D_1=1-\beta^2c$, $D_2=1-\beta^2d$,
\begin{align*}
A^2&=\frac{c}{MD_1},\qquad B^2=\frac{q-\hat q}{MD_1D_2},\qquad
C^2=\frac1{D_2}\Bigl(\frac{\hat q-q^2}{M}+\beta^2(\hat q-q^2)A^2+2\beta^2(2q+q^2-3\hat q)B^2\Bigr),\\
&\bigl|\nu((R_{12}-q)^2)-(A^2+2B^2+C^2)\bigr|,\ \bigl|\nu((R_{12}-q)(R_{13}-q))-(B^2+C^2)\bigr|,\ \bigl|\nu((R_{12}-q)(R_{34}-q))-C^2\bigr|\ \le\ \frac K{M^{3/2}}.
\end{align*}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cov-system} gives the system with errors $|R_i|\le E$.  Projecting on $(1,-2,1)$ and $(1,-4,3)$ (Eqs.~(1.235), (1.236)) decouples $U-2V+W$ and $U-4V+3W$; the third equation, after substituting $U=\alpha+2B+W$, $V=B+W$ with $\alpha=U-2V+W$, $B=V-W$, reads $(1-\beta^2d)W=(\hat q-q^2)/M+\beta^2(\hat q-q^2)\alpha+2\beta^2(2q+q^2-3\hat q)B+R_2$.  All denominators are $\ge3/4$ because $4\beta^2<1$ and $\hat q\le q$, and the errors are bounded by $44E$.
The exact part of this algebra (the three displayed fractions for the deviations of $\alpha$, $B$ and $W$ from $A^2$, $B^2$, $C^2$, valid whenever $D_1,D_2\ne0$) is the lemma \texttt{sk\_at\_exact\_errors}, which is shared with the solver beyond the AT line (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-194}); the projection on a left eigenvector is the ring-generic \texttt{Matrix.dotProduct\_system\_of\_transpose\_eigenvector}.  Only the size estimates differ between the two solvers.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Propositions~1.8.10 and 1.8.11: second moments of $T_1$ and $T$]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-18-10-11}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812, thm:sg-tal:overlap-covariances-T}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_T1_sq,
        SpinGlasses.overlap_eq_sum,

\talsource{Section~1.8, Propositions~1.8.10 and 1.8.11, Eqs.~(1.255)--(1.256), (1.261)--(1.262)}
With $T_1=(\boldsymbol\sigma^1-\mathbf b)\cdot\mathbf b/M$ and $T=\mathbf b\cdot\mathbf b/M-q$ ($\mathbf b=\langle\boldsymbol\sigma\rangle$) and the notation of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812}, $|\nu(T_1^2)-B^2|\le 2K/M^{3/2}$ and $|\nu(T^2)-C^2|\le 2K/M^{3/2}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
For every Gibbs measure $\langle T_1^2\rangle=V_G-W_G$ and $T^2=W_G$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:overlap-covariances-T} and $\langle T_1^2\rangle=M^{-2}\sum_{ij}C_{ij}b_ib_j$).  Average over the disorder and apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What remains of Section~1.8]
\label{rem:sg-tal:remaining-18}
Proved here: Proposition~1.8.1 (Eqs.~(1.215), (1.216)), Lemma~1.8.4 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-184}), Proposition~1.8.5 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-185}), Corollary~1.8.6 (Theorem~\ref{cor:sg-tal:corollary-186}), General Lemma~1.8.2 (Eq.~(1.230), Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-182}), the $3\times3$ AT matrix system (Eqs.~(1.231)--(1.236), Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:at-matrix}), and the decoupled covariance equations (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:at-covariance-equations}).
Part~E adds the combination form of Lemma~1.8.2, the sum form of Lemma~1.8.9 and the algebra of the $T$-basis.
Part~F adds the derivation of the covariance system (1.231)--(1.233) with explicit errors, Theorem~1.8.12 and Propositions~1.8.10, 1.8.11.
Lemma~1.8.9 is also proved in the book's displayed form (1.253)--(1.254) (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-189-displayed}), although the covariance system is used instead of the six-replica proofs of Propositions~1.8.8 and 1.8.10 (their conclusions are proved in exact form).
Not formalized: the book's $O(3)$ remainder is an explicit $K/M^{3/2}$ only under $4\beta^2<1$; the version with the remainder $K(M^{-3/2}+\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3))$ needed beyond the AT line is a separate development, valid for every $\beta$ (Section~\ref{sec:sg-tal:beyond-at}, Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-194}).
\end{remark}

\section{Beyond the Almeida--Thouless line (Talagrand Section~1.9)}
\label{sec:sg-tal:beyond-at}

\paragraph{Status.}
Section~1.9 is partly formalized.  Proved here, with no condition $4\beta^2<1$ and no moment bound on $R_{1,2}-q$:
Lemma~1.9.3 for a fixed point $q>0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-193}); the cavity expansions of Section~1.8 with the
cubic remainder $K(M^{-3/2}+\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3))$ (Theorems~\ref{thm:sg-tal:holder-three}, \ref{thm:sg-tal:row-three});
Lemma~1.9.4 for the SK model (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-194}); and the concrete Proposition~1.9.2 for the SK model
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-192}); and Proposition~1.9.5 for $h\ne0$ (Theorems~\ref{thm:sg-tal:residual}, \ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-195}).
The real-variable deduction (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:beyond-at-deduction}) is the last step of Proposition~1.9.2.
The coupled free energy of Eqs.~(1.283)--(1.286) is formalized in finite volume: its derivative and convexity
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:coupled-calculus}), its Gaussian concentration and the lower bound from a Gibbs tail
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:coupled-core}), and Proposition~1.9.7 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-197}).
Theorem~1.9.6 is formalized only in the conditional form of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-196}: pointwise convergence of
$\psi_{N+1}$ near $\lambda=0$ is an explicit hypothesis, and the conclusion is that the limit is not differentiable at $0$
(via the model-independent Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:convex-kink}).
Not formalized: the existence of $\lim_N\psi_N(\lambda)$, which the book defers to another reference (finite-size convexity and concentration do not
give it); Proposition~1.9.5 at $h=0$ (the zero fixed point coexists with the selected positive one, so the global residual
bound fails at $x=0$). Proposition~A.14.1 for general $\varphi$ is Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:latala-guerra} (under explicit regularity at $0$), and the solution of (1.74) is unique for $h\ne0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:fixed-point-unique}).

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.9.3: $\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)<1$]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-193}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:latala-guerra}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_beta_sq_mul_one_sub_four_add_three_lt_one,
        SpinGlasses.integral_tanh_core_lt,
        Real.deriv_tanh_eq,
        Real.contDiff_one_tanh,
        Real.monotone_tanh,
        Real.hasDerivAt_deriv_tanh,
        Real.neg_two_mul_tanh_mul_lt_zero,
        SpinGlasses.integral_tanh_sq_deriv_mul_id_eq,
        ProbabilityTheory.integral_gaussianReal_add_const_eq}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Lemma~1.9.3, Eqs.~(1.272)--(1.274), and Appendix A.14, Proposition~A.14.1}
Let $\beta,h\in\mathbb R$ and let $q>0$ satisfy $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ with $z\sim N(0,1)$,
and $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$.  Then $\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)<1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Put $w=\beta\sqrt q$ (the case $\beta=0$ is trivial), $Y=wz+h$, $\varphi=\tanh$.
\emph{Key inequality} (the case $\varphi=\tanh$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:latala-guerra}(a); its proof is recalled here): $w\,\mathbb E[2\varphi(Y)\varphi'(Y)z]<2\,\mathbb E\varphi(Y)^2$.  This is
$x^2\Psi'(x)<0$ for $\Psi(x)=\mathbb E\varphi(\sqrt xz+h)^2/x$ at $x=w^2$, in the proof of Proposition~A.14.1:
write $wz=Y-h$, so that the difference equals $2\,\mathbb E[\varphi(Y)(Y\varphi'(Y)-\varphi(Y))]-2h\,\mathbb E[\varphi\varphi'(Y)]$.
The first term is negative, since $\psi(y)=y\varphi'(y)-\varphi(y)$ satisfies $\varphi(y)\psi(y)<0$ for $y\ne0$
(equivalently $2y<\sinh2y$ for $y>0$, and oddness) and $Y\ne0$ almost surely.  The second satisfies
$h\,\mathbb E[g(Y)]\ge0$ for the odd function $g=\varphi\varphi'$, which is $\ge0$ on $[0,\infty)$:
shifting the Gaussian by $a=h/w$ (a change of density by $e^{au-a^2/2}$) and symmetrizing $u\mapsto-u$
gives $h\,\mathbb E[g(Y)]=e^{-a^2/2}\,\mathbb E[h\,g(wu)\sinh(au)]$ and $h\,g(wu)\sinh(hu/w)\ge0$.
\emph{Gaussian integration by parts} (Stein's lemma) turns the left-hand side into
$w^2\,\mathbb E[2(1-\varphi^2)(1-3\varphi^2)]=2w^2(1-4q+3\hat q)$, using $\mathbb E\varphi^2=q$, $\mathbb E\varphi^4=\hat q$.
Hence $\beta^2q(1-4q+3\hat q)<q$, and one divides by $q>0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Eqs.~(1.270) and (1.271)]
\label{lem:sg-tal:indicator-lower-bound}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.cubic_le_add_indicator,
        SpinGlasses.indicator_lower_bound_of_cubic,
        SpinGlasses.sk_indicator_lower_bound_of_cubic}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Eqs.~(1.270) and (1.271)}
(a) For $|x|\le2$ and $\delta>0$: $|x|^3\le\frac\delta2x^2+8\cdot\mathbf 1_{\{|x|\ge\delta/2\}}$.
(b) Let $\nu$ be an average (additive, homogeneous, monotone) on functions on a set $\Omega$ and $|X|\le2$.
If $\delta\,\nu(X^2)\le\nu(|X|^3)$ then $\nu(\mathbf 1_{\{|X|\ge\delta/2\}})\ge\frac\delta{16}\nu(X^2)$.
(c) For the two-replica SK average on $N+1$ spins, $|q|\le1$, $X=R_{1,2}-q$, this is the statement
$\delta\,\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)\le\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)\Rightarrow\nu(\mathbf 1_{\{|R_{1,2}-q|\ge\delta/2\}})\ge\frac\delta{16}\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) Split $|x|<\delta/2$ (then $|x|^3\le\frac\delta2x^2$) and $|x|\ge\delta/2$ (then $|x|^3\le8$).  (b) Apply (a) pointwise,
use monotonicity and linearity, and rearrange.  (c) $|R_{1,2}|\le1$ and the SK average is additive, homogeneous and
monotone (Section~1.8, Part~E).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.9.2 from Lemma~1.9.4 (conditional)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:beyond-at-deduction}
  \uses{lem:sg-tal:indicator-lower-bound}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.exists_cubic_lower_bounds}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, proof of Proposition~1.9.2 from Lemma~1.9.4, Eqs.~(1.275)--(1.278)}
Let $a>0$, $\beta^2a>1$ (in the book $a=1-2q+\hat q=\mathbb E\,\mathrm{ch}^{-4}Y$, which is positive, and $\beta^2a>1$ is (1.266)),
$K>0$, and for $M\ge1$ let $S_2,S_3,T,A,R_1,R_2$ be real numbers with:
$T\ge0$ and $T=\frac4M\frac a{1-\beta^2a}+R_1$ (Eq.~(1.275)); $S_2=A+R_2$ with $A\le K/M$ (Eq.~(1.276); beyond the AT line $A=A^2+2B^2+C^2$ may be negative, only the upper bound is used);
$|R_i|\le K(M^{-3/2}+S_3)$ (Eq.~(1.277)); $0\le S_3\le2S_2$.
Then there are $\delta>0$ and $M_0$ such that for all $M\ge M_0$: $\delta S_2\le S_3$ and $S_2\ge\delta/M$; hence
$S_3\ge\delta S_2\ge\delta^2/M$ (Eq.~(1.269)).  Here $S_2=\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)$, $S_3=\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)$, $T$ is the
rectangular second moment and $A=A^2+2B^2+C^2$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Since $\beta^2a>1$ the leading term of $T$ equals $-c/M$ with $c=4a/(\beta^2a-1)>0$, so $T\ge0$ forces
$R_1\ge c/M$ and therefore $K(M^{-3/2}+S_3)\ge c/M$.  For $\sqrt M\ge2K/c$ one has $KM^{-3/2}\le c/(2M)$, hence
$S_3\ge c/(2KM)$ (Eq.~(1.278)).  Then $S_2\le K/M+K(M^{-3/2}+S_3)\le K'S_3$ with $K'=2K^2/c+2K$, and
$S_2\ge S_3/2\ge c/(4KM)$.  Take $\delta=\min(1/K',c/(4K))$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The two H\"older steps with exponents $(3,3/2)$ and $(3/2,3)$]
\label{thm:sg-tal:holder-three}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_pair_taylor_three,
        SpinGlasses.sk_old_spin_compare_three,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_Vf_three,
        SpinGlasses.sk_row_Gb_three,
        SpinGlasses.sk_inv_mul_abs_le_cube,
        rpow_mul_rpow_le_of_le,
        mul_sq_le_cube_third}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.8, Proposition~1.8.1, Eqs.~(1.215), (1.216), used with $\tau_1=3,\tau_2=3/2$ and $\tau_1=3/2,\tau_2=3$ in the proof of Lemma~1.9.4}
Let $\beta,h\in\mathbb R$, $q$ a fixed point, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, $M=N+1$, $\Delta=\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)$.
(a) For $x\ne y$, an observable $F$ of the old spins and $f=F\,(\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q)$, the second-order Taylor
error of Eq.~(1.216) is at most $4n^2(n+2)^2\beta^4e^{4(n+4)^2\beta^2}\,\nu(|f|^3)^{1/3}\Delta^{2/3}$.
(b) For an observable $G$ of the old spins of $m$ replicas, $|\nu(G)-\mathbb E\langle G\rangle_{\rm old}|\le 2m^2\beta^2e^{4(m+2)^2\beta^2}\nu(|G|^{3/2})^{2/3}\Delta^{1/3}$.
(c) For $f=(R^-_{ab}-q)(\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q)$ one has $\nu(|f|^3)\le32\Delta+32/M^3$; for a product of two truncated overlaps,
$\nu(|G|^{3/2})\le4\Delta+4/M^3$; and $M^{-1}\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)\le\Delta/3+\frac23M^{-3/2}$.
(d) If $A\le cD'$, $D\le D'$, $c\ge1$ and $s+t=1$, then $A^sD^t\le cD'$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a), (b) are the second- and first-order cavity estimates of Section~1.6--1.8 (they carry general conjugate exponents $p,r$)
with $(p,r)=(3,3/2)$ resp.\ $(3/2,3)$; the exponent $2r=3$ in (a) is why the same $\Delta$ appears.
(c) $R^-_{ab}-q=(R_{ab}-q)-\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b/M$, $|\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q|\le2$, $(x+y)^3\le4(x^3+y^3)$,
$(uv)^{3/2}\le(u^3+v^3)/2$ and the marginal of $\nu$ on two distinct replicas; the last bound is Young's inequality
$tm^2\le t^3/3+2m^3/3$ with $m=M^{-1/2}$.  (d) is $A^sD^t\le c^sD'^s D'^t=c^sD'$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One row of the covariance system, and the system, with the cubic remainder]
\label{thm:sg-tal:row-three}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:holder-three}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_row_equation_of_bounds,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cov_row_bound_three,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cov_system_of_row}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, proof of Lemma~1.9.4: ``all the computations there are done modulo an error term as in (1.277)''; Eqs.~(1.231)--(1.233)}
Let $\beta,h\in\mathbb R$ be arbitrary, $q$ a fixed point, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, $M=N+1$,
$\Delta=\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)$ and $E=K(\beta)(\Delta+M^{-3/2})$, with $K(\beta)=2304\cdot32\,\beta^4e^{256\beta^2}+32\beta^2(288\,\beta^2e^{256\beta^2}+3)+64\beta^2e^{144\beta^2}$.
For $a\ne c$ among four replicas, $\nu_4((R_{12}-q)(R_{ac}-q))$ differs from $M^{-1}b(c)+\sum_jw_jb(c_j)\nu_6((R_{ac}-q)(R_j-q))$ by at most $E$; and
if every row is bounded by $E$ then $(U,V,W)=S+A(U,V,W)+R$ with $|R_i|\le E$, where $U,V,W$ are the covariances of Eqs.~(1.231)--(1.233).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The row equation of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cov-system} is split into a core (the Taylor error and the $\nu_0\to\nu$ error enter as hypotheses
with bounds $E_T$, $E_H$) and its high-temperature instance; the cubic instance inserts Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:holder-three}.
The remaining terms ($R^-\to R$, the last-site error) are $\le3(\Delta+M^{-3/2})$ and $\Delta+M^{-3/2}$ times constants.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma~1.9.4 for the SK model: Eqs.~(1.275)--(1.277)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:lemma-194}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:row-three, thm:sg-tal:lemma-193}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_lemma_194,
        SpinGlasses.sk_beyond_at_remainders,
        SpinGlasses.sk_rect_nu_eq,
        SpinGlasses.sk_at_scalar_solution_of_abs,
        SpinGlasses.at_error_bound_of_abs,
        Matrix.abs_atEigenvectorOne_dot_le,
        Matrix.abs_atEigenvectorTwo_dot_le,
        SpinGlasses.abs_at_leading_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_cov_row_bound_three}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Lemma~1.9.4, Eqs.~(1.275)--(1.277)}
Let $q>0$ be a fixed point of $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, $c_2=1-2q+\hat q$ and assume
$\beta^2c_2>1$ (Eq.~(1.266)).  There is $K$, depending only on $\beta$ and $\delta=\min(\beta^2c_2-1,\,1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q))>0$, such that for every $N$, with $M=N+1$ and $\Delta=\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)$:
$\nu\bigl(((\sigma^1-\sigma^2)\cdot(\sigma^3-\sigma^4)/M)^2\bigr)=\frac4M\frac{c_2}{1-\beta^2c_2}+\mathcal R_1$,
$\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)=A+\mathcal R_2$ with $A=A^2+2B^2+C^2$ of Eqs.~(1.248), (1.256), (1.262), $|A|\le K/M$,
and $|\mathcal R_i|\le K(M^{-3/2}+\Delta)$.  No condition $4\beta^2<1$ and no moment bound on $R_{1,2}-q$ is used.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:row-three} gives the system (1.231)--(1.233) with $|R_i|\le E$.  The rectangular second moment is exactly
$4(U-2V+W)$ since $(\sigma^1-\sigma^2)\cdot(\sigma^3-\sigma^4)/M=(R_{13}-q)-(R_{14}-q)-(R_{23}-q)+(R_{24}-q)$, and
$D_1(U-2V+W)=c_2/M+(R_0-2R_1+R_2)$ with $D_1=1-\beta^2c_2$.  The denominators $D_1<0$ and $D_2=1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)>0$ (Lemma~1.9.3,
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-193}) satisfy $|D_i|\ge\delta$, so one divides;
the errors are multiplied by constants depending on $\beta,\delta$ only (the analogue of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812} with $|D_i|\ge\delta$ instead of $D_i\ge3/4$).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.9.2 for the SK model]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-192}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-194, thm:sg-tal:beyond-at-deduction}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_proposition_192,
        SpinGlasses.abs_overlap_sub_le_two}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Proposition~1.9.2, Eq.~(1.269)}
Let $q>0$ be a fixed point and $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$ with $\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)>1$.  There are $\delta>0$ and $N_0$ such that for all
$N\ge N_0$ the $(N+1)$-spin SK system satisfies $\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)\ge\delta\,\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)$ and $\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)\ge\delta/(N+1)$; hence
$\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)\ge\delta\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)\ge\delta^2/(N+1)$, which is (1.269).  By Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:indicator-lower-bound}, the set
$\{|R_{1,2}-q|\ge\delta/2\}$ has $\nu$-measure at least $\delta^2/(16(N+1))$ (Eq.~(1.271)).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:beyond-at-deduction} with $a=c_2$, $T=$ the rectangular second moment, $R_1,R_2$ the remainders of
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:lemma-194}, $S_2=\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)$, $S_3=\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)$; $T\ge0$ is the average of a square and $S_3\le2S_2$
because $|R_{1,2}-q|\le2$.  Here $a>0$ follows from $\beta^2a>1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~A.14.1 (Latala--Guerra lemma), general $\varphi$]
\label{thm:sg-tal:latala-guerra}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.integral_odd_comp_affine_nonneg,
        SpinGlasses.mul_mul_deriv_sub_self_neg,
        SpinGlasses.deriv_mem_Icc,
        SpinGlasses.integral_mul_deriv_mul_id_lt,
        SpinGlasses.hasDerivAt_integral_sq_comp_affine,
        SpinGlasses.strictAntiOn_integral_sq_comp_sqrt_div,
        SpinGlasses.tendsto_integral_sq_comp_sqrt_div_atTop}
  \leanok
\talsource{Appendix A.14, Proposition~A.14.1 (used for Proposition~1.3.8 and Lemma~1.9.3)}
Let $\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ be bounded, increasing and odd, and assume the regularity that the book uses implicitly: $\varphi$ is $C^1$ on all of $\mathbb R$ and $\varphi'$ is
differentiable at every $y>0$ with $\varphi''(y)<0$. Let $z$ be standard Gaussian, $h,s\in\mathbb R$ with $s\ne0$ or $h\ne0$, and $\Psi(x)=\mathbb{E}[\varphi(s\sqrt x\,z+h)^2]/x$.
Then (a) for $w\ne0$ and $Y=wz+h$, $w\,\mathbb{E}[\varphi(Y)\varphi'(Y)z]<\mathbb{E}[\varphi(Y)^2]$; (b) $\Psi$ is strictly decreasing on $(0,\infty)$; (c) $\Psi(x)\to0$ as $x\to\infty$
(this needs only boundedness of $\varphi$).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Regularity at $0$ is an extra hypothesis on purpose: the odd extension of $\sqrt y/(1+\sqrt y)$ is bounded, increasing and concave on $(0,\infty)$ but has no derivative at $0$, so the book's
statement needs a reading at $0$. From $\varphi''<0$ on $(0,\infty)$ and $\varphi'\ge0$ (monotonicity) one gets that $\varphi'$ is strictly decreasing on $[0,\infty)$, hence $0<\varphi'\le\varphi'(0)$ there; $\varphi'$ is even, so
$0\le\varphi'\le\varphi'(0)$ on $\mathbb R$, and $\varphi(y)>0$ for $y>0$. The function $\psi(y)=y\varphi'(y)-\varphi(y)$ has $\psi(0)=0$ and $\psi'(y)=y\varphi''(y)<0$ for $y>0$, so $\varphi(y)\psi(y)<0$ for $y\ne0$ (by oddness for $y<0$).
Writing $wz=Y-h$, $w\,\varphi\varphi'(Y)z-\varphi(Y)^2=\varphi(Y)\psi(Y)-h\,\varphi(Y)\varphi'(Y)$; the first term has negative expectation, and the second is nonpositive because
$h\,\mathbb{E}[g(wz+h)]\ge0$ for $g=\varphi\varphi'$, which is bounded, continuous, odd and nonnegative on $[0,\infty)$: shifting the Gaussian and averaging $u$ and $-u$ gives the integrand
$g(wu)\sinh(hu/w)e^{-h^2/(2w^2)}\cdot h$ of the book, which is nonnegative. This gives (a). Dominated by $2B\varphi'(0)|z|$, differentiation under the integral gives
$\frac{d}{dw}\mathbb{E}\varphi(wz+h)^2=\mathbb{E}[2\varphi\varphi'(wz+h)z]$; with $w=s\sqrt x$ and (a) one gets $x\Psi'(x)\cdot x<0$, i.e.\ (b) (for $s=0$, $\Psi(x)=\varphi(h)^2/x$ with $\varphi(h)\ne0$). For (c),
$0\le\mathbb{E}\varphi^2\le B^2$.
Formalized in \texttt{SpinGlasses/LatalaGuerra.lean}. The book's statement allows a random $h$ and conditions on it; the Lean statement has a deterministic $h$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~A.14.1 for $\tanh$, and the fixed-point residual]
\label{thm:sg-tal:residual}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:lemma-193, thm:sg-tal:latala-guerra}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.hasDerivAt_fixedPointMap_sqrt,
        SpinGlasses.fixedPointMap_pos,
        SpinGlasses.x_mul_deriv_fixedPointMap_lt,
        SpinGlasses.strictAntiOn_fixedPointMap_div,
        SpinGlasses.exists_abs_sub_le_mul_abs_sub_fixedPointMap,
        SpinGlasses.integral_tanh_sq_second_deriv_eq}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Lemma~1.9.3 and the proof of Proposition~1.9.5; Appendix A.14, Proposition~A.14.1}
Let $h\ne0$, $\beta\in\mathbb R$ and $\Phi(x)=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt xz+h)$, $z\sim N(0,1)$.  Then $\Phi>0$, $\Phi$ is differentiable on
$(0,\infty)$ with $\Phi'(x)=\beta^2\mathbb E[(1-\tanh^2)(1-3\tanh^2)]$, $x\Phi'(x)<\Phi(x)$, and $x\mapsto\Phi(x)/x$ is strictly decreasing
on $(0,\infty)$.  If $q$ is a fixed point of $\Phi$ and $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, there is $K>0$ with $|x-q|\le K|x-\Phi(x)|$
for all $x\in[0,1]$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
$\Phi(x)=H(\beta\sqrt x)$ with $H(w)=\mathbb E\tanh^2(wz+h)$ and $H'(w)=w\,\mathbb E[2(1-\tanh^2)(1-3\tanh^2)]$ (Stein), so the chain rule gives $\Phi'$.
The key inequality $w\,\mathbb E[2\tanh(1-\tanh^2)z]<2\mathbb E\tanh^2$ of Lemma~1.9.3 (proved at every $w\ne0$) gives $x\Phi'<\Phi$ (and $\Phi>0$ handles
$\beta=0$); hence $(\Phi/x)'<0$. In Lean the strict decrease of $\Phi/x$ is the case $\varphi=\tanh$, $s=\beta$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:latala-guerra}(b) (with the hypotheses checked for $\tanh$: $\tanh'=1-\tanh^2$, $\tanh''=-2\tanh(1-\tanh^2)<0$ on $(0,\infty)$). Residual: near $q$ use $(x-\Phi)'(q)=1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)>0$ (Lemma~1.9.3); for $x\ge q+\varepsilon$, $x-\Phi(x)=x(1-\Phi/x)\ge q\,c_1$ by
monotonicity of $\Phi/x$; for $\delta_0\le x\le q-\varepsilon$ likewise; for $x<\delta_0$ use continuity of $\Phi$ at $0$ and $\Phi(0)=\tanh^2h>0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.9.5 (nonzero field)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-195}
  \uses{thm:sg-tal:residual, thm:sg-tal:prop-192}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_mean_sub_fixedPointMap_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_abs_mean_overlap_sub_le,
        SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_center_bound,
        SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_center_indicator}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Proposition~1.9.5, Eqs.~(1.279)--(1.282) and (1.280)}
Let $h\ne0$, $q$ a fixed point, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, and $\nu$ the $(N+1)$-spin two-replica SK average.
(a) There is $K=K(\beta,h)$ such that for every $N$ and every $x\ge0$: $|x-q|\le K\,\nu(|R_{1,2}-x|)$ (the book's $1/N$ term is absent because
the last-spin product $\varepsilon_1\varepsilon_2$ is handled exactly).
(b) If moreover $\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)>1$, there are $\delta'>0$ and $N_0$ such that for all $N\ge N_0$ and all $x\ge0$:
$\nu(\mathbf 1_{\{|R_{1,2}-x|\ge\delta'\}})\ge\delta'/(N+1)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) For $x\in[0,1]$ the first-order cavity estimate (Proposition~1.8.1, Eq.~(1.215) with $\tau_1=\infty$, $\tau_2=1$, centered at $x$; no fixed-point property is
used) applied to $f=\varepsilon_1\varepsilon_2$ bounds $|\nu(f)-\nu_0(f)|$ by $8\beta^2e^{64\beta^2}\nu(|R_{1,2}-x|)$, with $\nu_0(f)=\Phi(x)$.  Site symmetry gives
$\nu(f)=\nu(R_{1,2})$ and Jensen gives $|\nu(R_{1,2})-x|\le\nu(|R_{1,2}-x|)$.  Hence $|x-\Phi(x)|\le C\nu(|R_{1,2}-x|)$ and the residual bound of
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:residual} applies.  For $x>1$ compare with the center $1$: $|R-1|\le|R-x|$ and $\nu(|R-x|)\ge x-1$.
(b) Let $\delta$ be as in Proposition~1.9.2.  If $|x-q|\le\delta/4$, then $\{|R-q|\ge\delta/2\}\subset\{|R-x|\ge\delta/4\}$ and (1.271) applies.  If $|x-q|>\delta/4$ and $x\le2$, then
(a) gives $\nu|R-x|\ge c_0=\delta/(4K)$ and $|R-x|\le3$ gives $\nu(\mathbf 1_{\{|R-x|\ge c_0/2\}})\ge c_0/6$.  If $x>2$ then $|R-x|\ge1$.  Take $\delta'$ the minimum of the
resulting thresholds.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Eq.~(1.286), finite form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:coupled-log}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.log_sum_sum_exp_coupled_sub}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Eqs.~(1.283)--(1.286)}
Let $\alpha$ be a finite nonempty set, $f:\alpha\to\mathbb R$ an energy and $\Theta:\alpha\times\alpha\to\mathbb R$.  Then
$\log\sum_{x,y}e^{f(x)+f(y)+\Theta(x,y)}-\log\sum_{x,y}e^{f(x)+f(y)}=\log\mathbb E_{\sigma^1,\sigma^2}e^{\Theta(\sigma^1,\sigma^2)}$,
the expectation being over two independent replicas of the Gibbs measure of $f$.  For the SK system with $f=-H_N$ and
$\Theta=\lambda N R_{1,2}$ this is $\log Z_{N,\lambda}-\log Z_{N,0}=\log\langle\exp\lambda NR_{1,2}\rangle$, i.e.\ Eq.~(1.286) before taking $\mathbb E$ and dividing by $N$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
$\sum_{x,y}e^{f(x)+f(y)}=Z^2$ and the two-replica Gibbs average of $e^{\Theta}$ equals $Z^{-2}\sum_{x,y}e^{f(x)+f(y)+\Theta(x,y)}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Coupled free energy: derivative and convexity (after Eq.~(1.286))]
\label{thm:sg-tal:coupled-calculus}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.skCoupledPartition,
        SpinGlasses.skCoupledFreeEnergy,
        SpinGlasses.skCoupledOverlapMean,
        SpinGlasses.exists_sk_coupled_data,
        SpinGlasses.skCoupledPartition_eq,
        SpinGlasses.skCoupledFreeEnergy_zero,
        SpinGlasses.hasDerivAt_skCoupledFreeEnergy,
        SpinGlasses.convexOn_skCoupledFreeEnergy,
        SpinGlasses.skCoupledPartition_pos,
        SpinGlasses.skCoupledOverlapMean_zero_nonneg,
        SpinGlasses.convexOn_log_sum_exp_add_mul,
        SpinGlasses.convexOn_integral_log_sum_exp_tilt,
        SpinGlasses.hasDerivAt_integral_log_sum_exp_tilt}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Eqs.~(1.283)--(1.285) and the lines after (1.286)}
Let $M\ge0$, $\beta,h\in\mathbb R$, $g$ the i.i.d. standard Gaussian pair disorder of the $M$-spin SK model, and
$E_g(\sigma)$ its energy.  Put $Z_{M,\lambda}(g)=\sum_{\sigma,\tau}\exp(E_g(\sigma)+E_g(\tau)+\lambda\sum_i\sigma_i\tau_i)$
(here $\lambda\sum_i\sigma_i\tau_i=\lambda M R_{1,2}$), $\psi_M(\lambda)=M^{-1}(\mathbb E\log Z_{M,\lambda}-\mathbb E\log Z_{M,0})$ and
$m_M(\lambda)=M^{-1}\mathbb E\big[\sum_{\sigma,\tau}(\sum_i\sigma_i\tau_i)e^{(\cdot)}/Z_{M,\lambda}\big]=\mathbb E\langle R_{1,2}\rangle_\lambda$.
Then $\psi_M(0)=0$, $\psi_M$ is convex on $\mathbb R$, differentiable with $\psi_M'(\lambda)=m_M(\lambda)$, and $m_M(0)\ge0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The pair $(\sigma,\tau)$ is one configuration of a finite space, and $E_g(\sigma)+E_g(\tau)=a(\sigma,\tau)\cdot g+d(\sigma,\tau)$ is a linear form of $g$.
Convexity of $s\mapsto\log\sum_xe^{d_x+sb_x}$ is H\"older's inequality (in the proof, the weighted AM--GM inequality for the normalised weights at two
values of $s$); integrating a pointwise convex inequality gives convexity of $\mathbb E\log Z$.  The derivative is obtained by differentiation under
the integral (Lemma \texttt{hasDerivAt\_integral\_log\_sum\_exp\_path} with the constant bound $\sum_x|b_x|$ on the $s$-derivative of the energy).
At $\lambda=0$ the two replicas are independent, so the numerator of $m_M(0)$ is $\sum_i(\sum_\sigma\sigma_ie^{E_g(\sigma)})^2\ge0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Concentration of $\log Z_{M,\lambda}$ and the bound from a Gibbs tail]
\label{thm:sg-tal:coupled-core}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.measureReal_abs_sub_integral_log_sum_exp_tilt_ge_le,
        SpinGlasses.measureReal_abs_sub_integral_log_skCoupledPartition_ge_le,
        SpinGlasses.log_sum_exp_add_mul_sub_ge,
        SpinGlasses.measureReal_ge_half_of_le_integral,
        SpinGlasses.lt_integral_sub_of_tail,
        SpinGlasses.measurable_gibbs_mass,
        SpinGlasses.gibbs_mass_mem_Icc,
        SpinGlasses.integrable_gibbs_mass,
        SpinGlasses.integrable_skCoupled_mass,
        SpinGlasses.sum_filter_exp_pair_div,
        SpinGlasses.skCoupledFreeEnergy_gt}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, proof of Proposition~1.9.7 (Eqs.~(1.287)--(1.289) and the concentration step)}
(a) For every $\lambda$ and $y\ge0$, $\mathbb P(|\log Z_{M,\lambda}-\mathbb E\log Z_{M,\lambda}|\ge y)\le 2\exp(-y^2/(8\beta^2M))$.

(b) Let $\beta\ne0$, $\lambda\ne0$, $\delta'>0$, $x\in\mathbb R$, and let $T$ be a set of pairs $(\sigma,\tau)$ with
$\lambda\sum_i\sigma_i\tau_i\ge\lambda Mx+|\lambda|M\delta'$ on $T$, whose Gibbs mass $X(g)=\sum_{(\sigma,\tau)\in T}e^{E_g(\sigma)+E_g(\tau)}/Z_{M,0}(g)$
has $\mathbb E X\ge\delta'/(2M)$.  If $16M\exp(-(\lambda^2\delta'^2/(2048\beta^2))M)<\delta'$ and $\exp(-(|\lambda|\delta'/8)M)\le\delta'/(4M)$ then
$\psi_M(\lambda)>\lambda x+\tfrac34|\lambda|\delta'$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
(a) is Gaussian concentration of the free energy of the Gaussian field $a(\sigma,\tau)\cdot g$ on pairs, whose variance is
$a\cdot a\le2\beta^2M$ (the number of pairs $i<j$ is $\binom M2$ and $(\sigma_i\sigma_j+\tau_i\tau_j)^2\le4$).
(b) Since $X\le1$ and $\mathbb EX\ge e:=\delta'/(2M)$ one has $\mathbb P(X\ge e/2)\ge e/2$ (the inequality $\mathbb EX\le e/2+\mathbb P(X\ge e/2)$).
On $\{X\ge e/2\}$, $\log Z_{M,\lambda}-\log Z_{M,0}\ge\lambda Mx+|\lambda|M\delta'+\log(e/2)$.  By (a) with $y=M|\lambda|\delta'/16$ for $\log Z_{M,\lambda}$
and $\log Z_{M,0}$ separately (no independence is used) the set where one of them deviates by $y$ has probability $\le4\exp(\cdot)<e/2$, so
the two events meet and $\mathbb E\log Z_{M,\lambda}-\mathbb E\log Z_{M,0}>\lambda Mx+|\lambda|M\delta'+\log(e/2)-2y$.  The size conditions give
$\log(e/2)\ge-|\lambda|\delta'M/8$, and dividing by $M$ gives the claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition~1.9.7: derivative separation beyond the AT line]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-197}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_coupled_derivative_separation,
        SpinGlasses.exists_tail_mass_ge,
        SpinGlasses.sum_filter_abs_sub_le,
        SpinGlasses.exists_nat_forall_mul_exp_neg_lt,
        SpinGlasses.skGibbsEnergy_succ_eq,
        SpinGlasses.deriv_sub_deriv_gt_of_convex}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Proposition~1.9.7 and its proof}
Let $h\ne0$, $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$ and $\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)>1$ (Eq.~(1.266)).
Then there is $\delta'>0$ (that of Eq.~(1.280)) such that for every $\lambda>0$ there is $N_0$ with, for all $N\ge N_0$ and $M=N+1$,
$\psi_M'(\lambda)-\psi_M'(-\lambda)>\delta'/2$, where $\psi_M'(\lambda)=\mathbb E\langle R_{1,2}\rangle_\lambda$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Take $x_M=\mathbb E\langle R_{1,2}\rangle=\psi_M'(0)\ge0$ as centre in Eq.~(1.280) (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-195}): the averaged Gibbs mass of
$\{|R_{1,2}-x_M|\ge\delta'\}$ is $\ge\delta'/M$, so the mass of $\{R_{1,2}\ge x_M+\delta'\}$ or of $\{R_{1,2}\le x_M-\delta'\}$ is $\ge\delta'/(2M)$
(Eqs.~(1.287), (1.288)).  In the first case Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:coupled-core}(b) with $\lambda$, in the second with $-\lambda$, gives
$\psi_M(\lambda)>\lambda x_M+\tfrac34\lambda\delta'$ resp.\ $\psi_M(-\lambda)>-\lambda x_M+\tfrac34\lambda\delta'$ once $M$ is large (the two size
conditions hold for all large $M$, as $Me^{-\kappa M}\to0$).  Convexity of $\psi_M$ with $\psi_M(0)=0$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:coupled-calculus}) turns this into $\psi_M'(\lambda)\ge(\psi_M(\lambda)-\psi_M(0))/\lambda>x_M+\tfrac34\delta'$ and
$\psi_M'(-\lambda)\le\psi_M'(0)=x_M$ (monotone derivative), respectively $\psi_M'(-\lambda)<x_M-\tfrac34\delta'$ and $\psi_M'(\lambda)\ge x_M$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Convex functions with a derivative gap: limits have a corner]
\label{thm:sg-tal:convex-kink}
  \lean{not_differentiableAt_of_convexOn_tendsto_of_derivative_gap}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, the convexity argument in the proof of Theorem~1.9.6}
Let $r>0$, $c>0$, let $l$ be a nontrivial filter on an index type $I$ (for example $n\to\infty$ on $\mathbb N$), and let $f_i:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ ($i\in I$)
be convex on $(-r,r)$ and differentiable there with derivative $f_i'$.  Suppose $f_i(x)\to g(x)$ along $l$
for every $x\in(-r,r)$, and that for every $\lambda\in(0,r)$ one has $f_i'(\lambda)-f_i'(-\lambda)\ge c$ for $l$-eventually all $i$.
Then $g$ is not differentiable at $0$.  No uniformity of the threshold in $\lambda$, and no convergence of $f_i'$, is assumed; the strict
inequality $>c$ of Proposition~1.9.7 is more than needed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
Fix $0<\lambda<r/2$, so that $\pm\lambda,\pm2\lambda\in(-r,r)$.  Convexity gives $f_i'(\lambda)\le(f_i(2\lambda)-f_i(\lambda))/\lambda$ and $f_i'(-\lambda)\ge(f_i(-\lambda)-f_i(-2\lambda))/\lambda$ for each $i$.  For this
$\lambda$ the right-hand sides converge to the same secant slopes of $g$, so the difference of these slopes of $g$ is $\ge c$.  If $g$ had derivative $d$ at $0$,
then $|g(x)-g(0)-dx|\le\tfrac c8|x|$ for $|x|\le2\lambda$ and $\lambda$ small, and the two slopes lie within $3c/8$ of $d$ each, so their difference is $\le3c/4<c$, a contradiction.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem~1.9.6, conditional on the existence of the limit]
\label{thm:sg-tal:thm-196}
  \lean{SpinGlasses.sk_coupled_limit_not_differentiableAt}
  \leanok
\talsource{Section~1.9, Theorem~1.9.6}
Let $h\ne0$, $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt qz+h)$ and $\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)>1$.  Let $r>0$ and suppose that for every
$\lambda\in(-r,r)$ the limit $\psi(\lambda)=\lim_N\psi_{N+1}(\lambda)$ exists.  Then $\psi$ is not differentiable at $0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
The functions $\psi_{N+1}$ are convex with derivative $\mathbb E\langle R_{1,2}\rangle_\lambda$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:coupled-calculus}), and Proposition~1.9.7
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-197}) gives the gap $>\delta'/2$ for every fixed $\lambda>0$ and large $N$; apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:convex-kink} with $c=\delta'/2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Deviations and the open items of Section~1.9]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-19}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Indexing.} $M$ is the number of spins of the system, as in Section~1.8.  The book's $N$-dependence $K/N$ is $K/M$.
\item \emph{Averages.} The conclusions of the deduction are stated for sequences of real numbers, and the indicator bound for an abstract average.  No measure of the form $\nu$ is constructed.
\item \emph{History: what Lemma~1.9.4 required.} Theorems~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cov-system} and~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812} carry the hypotheses $4\beta^2<1$ and the moment bounds $K_1/M$, $K_2/M^2$ (they enter through $\exp(c\beta^2)$ Hoelder factors and the Cauchy--Schwarz errors $\sqrt{K_1/M}\sqrt{K_2/M^2}$).  Beyond the AT line these moment bounds are false in general, so those errors are re-bounded by $\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)$ using the Hoelder pairs $(3,3/2)$ and $(3/2,3)$ of the book (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:holder-three}).  The high-temperature theorems are unchanged; the cubic versions share their row equation (\texttt{sk\_row\_equation\_of\_bounds}) and covariance system (\texttt{sk\_cov\_system\_of\_row}).
\item \emph{Lemma~1.9.3} is proved for a fixed point $q>0$.  The hypothesis $q>0$ is automatic for $h\ne0$ (as $\mathbb E\tanh^2>0$) but is a genuine selection of the fixed point for $h=0$, where $q=0$ always solves the equation and the strict inequality fails for $\beta\ge1$.  The derivative $\Phi'$ is not formed: the inequality $x\Phi'(x)-\Phi(x)<0$ of Proposition~A.14.1 is proved directly at $x=\beta^2q$, Proposition~A.14.1 for general $\varphi$, including the vanishing at infinity, is Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:latala-guerra}, under the explicit regularity hypotheses stated there.
\item \emph{Coupled free energy.} $\psi_M$, $Z_{M,\lambda}$ and $m_M$ are defined for the explicit SK energy with the coupling written as
$\lambda\sum_i\sigma_i\tau_i=\lambda MR_{1,2}$, so no normalisation by $M$ appears in $Z_{M,\lambda}$.  The concentration constant is $K=8\beta^2$
