from the variance bound $2\beta^2M$ (the book's $K$ is unspecified); the separation bound proved is $3\delta'/4>\delta'/2$.  Proposition~1.9.7 inherits
$h\ne0$ from Proposition~1.9.5; the threshold $N_0$ depends on $\lambda$ (the size conditions degenerate as $\lambda\to0$), as in the book.
\item \emph{Theorem~1.9.6.} Convergence of $\psi_N(\lambda)$ to a finite real-valued function $\psi$, pointwise on a neighbourhood of $0$, is assumed; its existence is not proved. the formalized statement is the implication ``limit exists $\Rightarrow$ not differentiable at $0$''.  The corner (the one-sided derivatives $\psi'(0\pm)$) is not constructed.
\item \emph{Research Problem~1.9.1} (the pointwise-in-$N$ bound $\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|)>\delta$ beyond the AT line, Eq.~(1.268)) is an open problem of the book, not a theorem; it is recorded in Remark~\ref{rem:sg-tal:research-191} and is not part of the formalization targets.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Research Problem~1.9.1 is an open problem, not a theorem]
\label{rem:sg-tal:research-191}
\talsource{Research Problem~1.9.1, Eqs.~(1.266)--(1.268); source lines 3848--3870}
This remark records a statement that Talagrand marks as an \emph{open problem} (``Level~2''). It is not a theorem of the book, it has no Lean declaration, and it is deliberately not stated as a \texttt{theorem} environment, so that it cannot be mistaken for a result that is proved or planned.

\emph{The setting.} Let $q$ be the solution of Eq.~(1.74), $\widehat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4 Y$ and $Y=\beta z\sqrt q+h$. ``Beyond the AT line'' means Eq.~(1.266),
\[
\beta^2(1-2q+\widehat q)=\beta^2\,\mathbb E\,\frac1{\cosh^4 Y}>1 .
\]
The book announces that it will prove \emph{much later} that under~(1.266) the left-hand side of Eq.~(1.171) is bounded below independently of $N$, and hence that for some $\delta>0$ independent of $N$,
\[
\frac1N\sum_{M\le N}\nu_M\bigl(|R_{1,2}-q|\bigr)>\delta>0 \qquad(1.267).
\]
That later proof is not part of this formalization.

\emph{The problem.} Prove the pointwise-in-$N$ strengthening: beyond the AT line, for \emph{every} $N$ (not only on average over $M\le N$),
\[
\nu\bigl(|R_{1,2}-q|\bigr)>\delta \qquad(1.268).
\]
Talagrand states that (1.268) can be deduced from (1.267), with considerable work, in many cases, for instance when the external field terms $h_i$ in $\sum_{i\le N}h_i\sigma_i$ are i.i.d.\ Gaussian with non-zero variance, but that it is not known how to prove (1.268) for a constant field $h_i=h\ne0$, which is the case treated in this chapter.

\emph{Relation to what is formalized.} Nothing here proves (1.268). The results of this section are of a different kind. Proposition~1.9.2 gives only the lower bound $\nu(|R_{1,2}-q|^3)\ge\delta\,\nu((R_{1,2}-q)^2)\ge\delta^2/N$ for large $N$, which tends to $0$. Proposition~1.9.5 (for $h\ne0$) and Proposition~1.9.7 give lower bounds on $\nu(\mathbf 1_{\{|R_{1,2}-x|\ge\delta'\}})$ for all centers $x\ge0$ and for large $N$, and the resulting gap in $\psi_N'$. These are exactly the facts the book proves and uses for Theorem~1.9.6, and they are weaker than~(1.268), which asks for a bound on the first absolute moment around $q$ itself, uniform in the system size.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Existence of the coupled limit $\lim_N\psi_N(\lambda)$ (first half of Theorem~1.9.6): context, importance and plan]
\label{rem:sg-tal:coupled-limit-existence}
\talsource{Theorem~1.9.6 and the paragraph after it, Eqs.~(1.283)--(1.286); source lines 4037--4045; reference~[76]}
\emph{Not formalized.} Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-196} is an implication ``if $\psi_N(\lambda)$ converges pointwise near $0$ then the limit is not differentiable at $0$''; the convergence is a hypothesis. This remark explains what is missing, why the book does not prove it, how much it matters, and how it could be proved.

\emph{Context.} Two copies of the SK system, with the same disorder $g$ and field $h$, are coupled by the term $\lambda N R_{1,2}$ (Eq.~(1.283)); the coupled free energy is $\psi_N(\lambda)=\frac1N\mathbb E\log\langle\exp\lambda NR_{1,2}\rangle$ (Eq.~(1.286)). It is convex with $\psi_N'(\lambda)=\mathbb E\langle R_{1,2}\rangle_\lambda$. The physical content of Theorem~1.9.6 is that, beyond the Almeida--Thouless line, an arbitrarily small coupling $\lambda$ changes the mean overlap by at least $\delta'/2$: the replica-symmetric solution, in which $R_{1,2}$ concentrates at one value $q$, is unstable. Proposition~1.9.7 proves this for every large finite $N$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-197}). To state the result as a property of a \emph{function} $\psi$ one needs a limit; Talagrand then deduces non-differentiability of $\psi$ at $0$ from convexity and Griffiths' lemma (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:convex-kink}, which is formalized).

\emph{What the book does.} Talagrand writes that the existence of $\lim_N\psi_N(\lambda)$ ``is only a side story'' which ``requires significant work'', and refers to Guerra and Toninelli [76] for a proof. That reference is F.~Guerra and F.~L.~Toninelli, \emph{The infinite volume limit in generalized mean field disordered models}, Markov Process. Related Fields 9 (2003), 195--207, arXiv:cond-mat/0208579, which extends their interpolation proof of the existence of the SK limit (Theorem~1.3.9, already formalized as \texttt{GuerraToninelli.lean}) to several replicas coupled through functions of their mutual overlaps. The book proves nothing about it, so the Lean theorem keeps the convergence as an explicit hypothesis, which is faithful to the book.

\emph{Is it important to formalize?} Low to moderate. (i) The mathematical content the book calls ``important'' (the instability, and non-differentiability of any limit) is already proved: for every large $N$ by Proposition~1.9.7, and for any pointwise limit by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-196}. (ii) No later result of the volume uses the existence of the limit. (iii) What is lost without it is only the literal first sentence of Theorem~1.9.6, ``$\psi(\lambda)$ exists for all $\beta,h$''; this is the reason the milestone box for Section~1.9 stays unticked. (iv) The cost is high: it is a research-paper proof, not a corollary of results here. It should be done after Section~1.10 and Hanen's theorem, and only on an explicit scope decision.

\emph{Cheap partial result that avoids the limit (not yet formalized).} Each $\psi_N$ is convex with $\psi_N(0)=0$ and $|\psi_N'|\le1$ (because $|R_{1,2}|\le1$), hence the family is equi-Lipschitz on compact sets. By the Arzela--Ascoli theorem every subsequence has a further subsequence converging locally uniformly to a convex function, and the filter-generalized kink theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:convex-kink}) applied along that subsequence shows that \emph{every} subsequential limit is non-differentiable at $0$. In particular the sequence $\psi_N$ cannot converge to a function that is differentiable at $0$. This uses only existing results and compactness, and is the natural unconditional substitute for the existence of the limit.

\emph{General approach to the existence proof (Guerra--Toninelli).} Write $p_N(\lambda)=\frac1N\mathbb E\log Z_{N,\lambda}$, so that $\psi_N(\lambda)=p_N(\lambda)-p_N(0)$ and $p_N(0)=2\cdot\frac1N\mathbb E\log Z_N$ has a limit by Theorem~1.3.9. It remains to show that $p_N(\lambda)$ converges.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{A priori bounds.} $-2|h|-|\lambda|\le p_N(\lambda)\le 2\log2+2|h|+|\lambda|+\beta^2$, by one configuration for the lower bound and Jensen with Gaussian exponential moments for the upper bound.
\item \emph{Interpolation.} Split $N=n+m$ and compare the glued system with two independent blocks of sizes $n$ and $m$ along the trigonometric (cosine/sine) Gaussian interpolation path used throughout this chapter. The derivative is $\sin t\cos t\,\mathbb E\langle D(a,a)-D(a,b)\rangle_t$ with $D=\mathrm{Cov}(\text{blocks})-\mathrm{Cov}(\text{glued})$ on pair configurations. For a single SK system $D(a,a)=0$ and $D(a,b)$ has a sign, which gives superadditivity and Fekete's lemma. For coupled replicas this fails: $D(a,a)=\beta^2\bigl(c\,(r^1(a)-r^2(a))^2-2\bigr)$ depends on the internal overlaps $r^1,r^2$ of the two blocks (with $c=nm/N$), and explicit four-site examples have either sign for the integrand. A direct superadditivity proof is therefore not available.
\item \emph{Restriction to overlap cells (the Guerra--Toninelli idea).} Partition pair configurations into finitely many cells in which the internal overlap is fixed up to a width $\eta$, with the number of cells bounded independently of $N$. On a pair of matching cells $|r^1-r^2|\le\eta$, so the diagonal defect is at most $\beta^2c\eta^2$ and the comparison holds up to an explicit error. This needs restricted partition sums, a rule for gluing block cells into a global cell, and a recombination over cells. One must not replace the expectation of the random maximum over cells by the maximum of the expectations; concentration of the restricted log-partition functions controls this.
\item \emph{Approximate superadditivity and the limit.} The outcome is $p_{n+m}\ge\frac{n}{n+m}p_n+\frac{m}{n+m}p_m-\varepsilon(\eta)-o(1)$ with $\varepsilon(\eta)\to0$; a Fekete-type lemma for approximately superadditive sequences (de~Bruijn--Erd\H{o}s type) then yields convergence. A geometric subsequence of sizes is not enough: the limit must be proved for every $N$.
\end{enumerate}
Step~2 shows why the closure draft's proposal of an unrestricted comparison was withdrawn: it had the sign reversed and ignored the dependence of the diagonal on the self-overlap (see the closure review in the repository documents). The Gaussian estimates of Step~1 and the interpolation derivative are already available (\texttt{hasDerivAt\_integral\_log\_sum\_exp\_trig} and the Gaussian-comparison file); the new work is Steps~3 and~4.
\end{remark}

\section{Central Limit Theorem for the Overlaps (Talagrand Section~1.10)}\label{sec:sg-tal:overlap-clt}

Section~1.10 of Talagrand establishes the quantitative central limit theorem for the fluctuations of the replica overlaps $X_{\ell,\ell'} = \sqrt{N}(R_{\ell,\ell'} - q)$ in the high-temperature regime $\beta < 1/2$. The asymptotic covariance matrix is determined by the $T$-basis limits $(A^2, B^2, C^2)$ computed in Section~1.8 (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812}):
$$\Sigma(\{\ell_1, \ell_2\}, \{\ell_3, \ell_4\}) = A^2 \mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_1, \ell_2\} = \{\ell_3, \ell_4\}} + B^2 |\{\ell_1, \ell_2\} \cap \{\ell_3, \ell_4\}| + C^2.$$

\begin{theorem}[Overlap covariance function]\label{thm:sg-tal:overlap-cov}
\lean{SpinGlasses.skOverlapCov}
\leanok
\talsource{Section~1.10, above Eq.~(1.298)}
For pairs $p_1,p_2$ of replicas, \texttt{skOverlapCov} is the combinatorial formula $A^2\mathbf 1_{p_1=p_2}+B^2|p_1\cap p_2|+C^2$; it equals $A^2+2B^2+C^2$ on the diagonal and $C^2$ for disjoint pairs (\texttt{skOverlapCov\_self}, \texttt{skOverlapCov\_disjoint}). It is only a formula: it is not yet connected to the covariance of the overlaps.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Formalized in \texttt{SpinGlasses/SKOverlapCLT.lean}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Higher-order cavity errors}

The following preparation is proved for a deterministic external field. Write $m=N+1$
for the number of sites, $R^-_{xy}=m^{-1}\sum_{i<N}\sigma_i^x\sigma_i^y$,
and $D^-_{xy}=R^-_{xy}-q$. The old-spin Gibbs measure retains the coefficient
$\beta/\sqrt m$, rather than changing the inverse temperature. The endpoints $\nu$
and $\nu_0$ and the derivative $\nu'_0$ are the cavity averages from
Section~\ref{sec:sg-tal:second-moment}.

\begin{lemma}[Truncated overlap moments and products]\label{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-moments}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_dev_even_moment_le, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_prod_sq_le}
\leanok
\talsource{Eqs.~(1.296)--(1.297)}
Fix $\beta_0\in\mathbb R$ with $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $r\in\mathbb N$.
There is $K_r>0$, independent of $N,\beta,h,q$ and of the finite replica type,
such that, whenever $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$ and
$q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, every pair $x\ne y$ satisfies
\[
  \nu((D^-_{xy})^{2r})\le K_r/m^r.
\]
For each $k\in\mathbb N$ and $M\in\mathbb R$ there is also $H>0$, independent
of $N,\beta,h,q$, the replica count and the replica labels, such that
\[
 f=W\prod_{j=1}^k D^-_{a_jb_j},\quad |W|\le M,\quad a_j\ne b_j
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \nu(f^2)\le H/m^k.
\]
The pairs may repeat or share indices. The bounded factor $W$ can depend on the last spins.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use the exact last-spin split and $|\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y|=1$ to bound
$(D^-_{xy})^{2r}$ by $2^{2r-1}((R_{xy}-q)^{2r}+m^{-2r})$.
The uniform high-temperature overlap moments and $m^{-2r}\le m^{-r}$ give the first claim.
For a nonempty product, the existing finite-product inequality
$\prod_j|u_j|\le\sum_j|u_j|^k$, applied to $u_j=(D^-_{a_jb_j})^2$,
reduces the second claim to $2k$-th moments. The empty product is handled separately.
No independence of the factors is assumed.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Higher-order cavity expansions]\label{thm:sg-tal:cavity-higher-expansion}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_expansion_of_sq_le, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_prod_expansion,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_prod_old_spin_compare, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_prod_expansion_uniform,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_prod_old_spin_compare_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-moments}
\talsource{Eqs.~(1.298)--(1.299)}
Fix deterministic $\beta,h,q\in\mathbb R$ with $4\beta^2<1$ and
$q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, a replica count $n$, a degree $k$,
and a bound $M\in\mathbb R$. There is $C>0$, chosen before $N$, the distinct
pairs $a_j\ne b_j$ and the bounded factor $W$, such that, for the above $f$,
\[
 |\nu(f)-\nu_0(f)|\le \frac C{m^{(k+1)/2}},\qquad
 |\nu(f)-\nu_0(f)-\nu'_0(f)|\le \frac C{m^{(k+2)/2}}.
\]
For a pure product of $k$ such factors, evaluating $\nu_0$ on the old spins
gives the same first bound with the actual old-spin Gibbs average in place of $\nu_0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply the existing first- and second-order cavity bounds with both Holder exponents equal
to two. The preceding lemma bounds $\nu(f^2)^{1/2}$ by $\sqrt H\,m^{-k/2}$;
the full overlap second and fourth moments supply the additional factors
$m^{-1/2}$ and $m^{-1}$. Enlarge the constants to obtain one positive $C$.
The analytic bound is exposed for any observable with $\nu(f^2)\le H/m^k$;
the monomial and linear-combination results use this shared statement.
For the old-spin form, use the exact decoupled endpoint identity with no last-spin factors.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Uniformity of the higher-order expansion chain]
\lean{SpinGlasses.abs_skCavityBUnscaled_le}
\leanok
\talsource{Eqs.~(1.298)--(1.302), constants independent of the system size}
The monomial Taylor bounds, their old-spin comparison, Lemma~1.10.2, its finite
linear-combination extension and both higher-order corollaries below admit the following
stronger quantifier order. Fix $4\beta_0^2<1$, the replica and factor counts, coefficients
and distinguished labels. One positive constant works for every deterministic $h$, every
$\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, every $q$ satisfying the fixed-point equation, the corresponding
$\widehat q$, and every $N$. The original fixed-parameter declarations specialize these
uniform results. In the signed pair sum the existing bound
$|b_{\rm unscaled}(r)|\le1$ gives
$\sum_j|w_j b_j|\le\beta_0^2\sum_j|w_j|$, so its error coefficient is chosen before
$\beta,h,q,\widehat q$. No uniformity in unbounded coefficient families is asserted.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Higher-order pair expansion]\label{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-pair}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_pair_prod_expansion, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_pair_prod_expansion_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:cavity-higher-expansion}
\talsource{Lemma~1.10.2, Eq.~(1.300)}
Fix deterministic $\beta,h,q\in\mathbb R$ with $4\beta^2<1$ and
$q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, and set
$\widehat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$.
For fixed $n,k\in\mathbb N$ and $x\ne y$ among the first $n$ replicas,
there is $C>0$, chosen before $N$ and the pairs defining
$f^-=\prod_{j=1}^k D^-_{a_jb_j}$ with $a_j\ne b_j$, such that
\[
\left|\nu\bigl((\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q)f^-\bigr)
 -\sum_{\ell<\ell'\le n} b(c_{\ell\ell';xy})\nu(f^-D^-_{\ell\ell'})
 +n\sum_{\ell\le n}b(c_{\ell,n+1;xy})\nu(f^-D^-_{\ell,n+1})
 -\frac{n(n+1)}2 b(0)\nu(f^-D^-_{n+1,n+2})\right|
 \le \frac C{m^{(k+2)/2}}.
\]
Here $c_{ab;xy}=|\{a,b\}\cap\{x,y\}|$ and
$b(2)=\beta^2(1-q^2)$, $b(1)=\beta^2(q-q^2)$,
$b(0)=\beta^2(\widehat q-q^2)$.
The right-hand averages are full Gibbs averages with two additional replicas.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The decoupled expectation is zero by the fixed-point equation and independence of the last
spins. Evaluate the decoupled derivative by the proved pair formula of Section~1.8.
Each resulting old-spin observable is a product of $k+1$ centered overlaps; apply the
old-spin comparison above to replace its average by the full average with error
$C_1m^{-(k+2)/2}$. Reindex the two additional replicas and sum these errors using
\texttt{Weights.abs\_weighted\_sum\_le}. A valid uniform constant is
$C_0+C_1\sum_j|w_jb(c_j)|$, where $w_j$ are the three signed replica-family weights.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Finite linear combinations and Corollary~1.10.3}

The following extension and both displayed formulas of Corollary~1.10.3 are proved.
They use the full Gibbs averages, rather than assuming an error identity for a Gaussian model.

\begin{lemma}[A linear functional on products of sums]\label{lem:sg-tal:finite-product-bound}
\lean{LinearMap.abs_apply_prod_sum_le}
\leanok
\talsource{Finite multilinearity underlying the paragraph after Eq.~(1.300)}
Let $\Omega$ be any type, $I$ a finite factor index set, and $I_j$ a finite summand
index set for each $j\in I$. Let $L:\mathbb R^\Omega\to\mathbb R$ be linear,
$c_{ji}\in\mathbb R$, $f_{ji}:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, and $E\in\mathbb R$.
If $|L(\prod_j f_{j,t_j})|\le E$ for every choice $t_j\in I_j$, then
\[
 \left|L\left(\prod_j\sum_{i\in I_j}c_{ji}f_{ji}\right)\right|
 \le \left(\prod_j\sum_{i\in I_j}|c_{ji}|\right) E.
\]
No positivity or independence is required; the index sets may be empty.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Distribute the product using Mathlib's finite product-of-sums identity,
apply linearity, and use the existing signed weighted-sum bound.
The sum of absolute product coefficients is the product of the sums of absolute coefficients.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Square bounds under finite multilinearity]
\label{lem:sg-tal:finite-product-square-bound}
\lean{LinearMap.apply_sq_sum_le, LinearMap.apply_sq_prod_sum_le}
\leanok
\talsource{Finite multilinearity and positivity underlying Eq.~(1.297)}
Let $L:\mathbb R^\Omega\to\mathbb R$ be linear and monotone for the pointwise order.
With the finite families $c_{ji},f_{ji}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:finite-product-bound},
if $L((\prod_j f_{j,t_j})^2)\le E$ for every selection $t$, then
\[
 L\left(\left(\prod_j\sum_i c_{ji}f_{ji}\right)^2\right)
 \le \left(\prod_j\sum_i|c_{ji}|\right)^2 E.
\]
The one-sum version is also proved. The coefficients need not be nonnegative.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Positivity and $\pm2uv\le u^2+v^2$ give $|L(uv)|\le E$ whenever
$L(u^2),L(v^2)\le E$. Expand the square of the finite sum and bound its signed
cross terms. Distributing the finite product gives the product version.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Positive-functional estimates and square-root scaling]
\label{lem:sg-tal:positive-functional-square}
\lean{LinearMap.abs_apply_le_apply_abs, LinearMap.sq_apply_le_apply_sq,
Real.sqrt_pow_nat}
\leanok
\talsource{Elementary inequalities used in Eqs.~(1.297)--(1.299) and (1.310)}
Let $L:\mathbb R^X\to\mathbb R$ be a monotone real linear functional. Then
$|L(f)|\le L(|f|)$. If $L(1)=1$, then $L(f)^2\le L(f^2)$.
For $x\ge0$ and $j\in\mathbb N$, $\sqrt{x^j}=(\sqrt{x})^j$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply monotonicity to $f$ and $-f$ for the first estimate, and to $(f-L(f))^2$
for the second. The square-root identity follows by comparing nonnegative squares.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform Taylor errors and arbitrary finite replica indices]
\label{lem:sg-tal:cavity-uniform-finite}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_expansion_of_sq_le_uniform,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_lincomb_first_order_finset}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-moments, thm:sg-tal:prop-181-second}
\talsource{Eqs.~(1.298)--(1.299), including the uniform temperature range}
Fix $4\beta_0^2<1$, $n,k\in\mathbb N$ and $H\ge0$. There is $C>0$, chosen before
$\beta,h,q,N,f$, such that for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $q$ the fixed point, and
$\nu(f^2)\le H/(N+1)^k$, both Taylor errors are bounded by
$C/(N+1)^{(k+1)/2}$ and $C/(N+1)^{(k+2)/2}$ respectively.
For the first-order comparison, the replica type may be any fixed finite type $R$ and
the product may run over a finite set $s$ of factors. If
$f=W\prod_{j\in s}\sum_i c_{ji}(R^-_{a_{ji}b_{ji}}-q)$ with $|W|\le M$ and
$a_{ji}\ne b_{ji}$ for $j\in s$, there is one positive constant, before all the model
parameters and labels, bounding $|\nu(f)-\nu_0(f)|$ by $C/(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}$.
The finite type $R$, coefficient families, $s$ and $M$ are fixed before this constant.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use the uniform overlap moments at $\beta_0$ and bound the explicit nonnegative Taylor
coefficients by their values at $\beta_0^2$. The previous fixed-parameter result is its
specialization. For arbitrary $R$, reindex once by $R\simeq\mathrm{Fin}(|R|)$ using
the existing Gibbs replica equivalence, and apply the subset square-moment bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Moment and Taylor bounds for the full product class]
\label{thm:sg-tal:cavity-higher-linear-moments}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_lincomb_sq_le, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_lincomb_expansion}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:finite-product-square-bound, lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-moments,
thm:sg-tal:cavity-higher-expansion}
\talsource{Eqs.~(1.297)--(1.299), functions of order $k$}
For fixed finite coefficient families, let
\[
 f=W\prod_{j=1}^k\sum_{i\in I_j}c_{ji}D^-_{a_{ji}b_{ji}},
 \qquad |W|\le M,\quad a_{ji}\ne b_{ji}.
\]
The second-moment estimate $\nu(f^2)\le H/m^k$ holds with $H>0$ chosen
before the size, replica count and labels, and $W$, uniformly for
$\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $4\beta_0^2<1$, and all deterministic $h$ and fixed points $q$.
For fixed $\beta,h,q,n$, the two Taylor estimates of
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:cavity-higher-expansion} hold for this $f$, with one
positive constant before $N$, the labels and $W$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use the positive linear functional $L(g)=\nu(W^2g)$ and the monomial square bounds.
If $H_0$ is the monomial constant, $1+H_0(\prod_j\sum_i|c_{ji}|)^2$ is a valid
positive square-bound constant. Apply the shared observable-general Taylor theorem.
The factor $W$ may depend on the last spins; no extra independence is imposed.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lemma~1.10.2 for products of linear combinations]
\label{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-linear}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_pair_lincomb_expansion,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_pair_lincomb_expansion_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:finite-product-bound, lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-pair}
\talsource{Paragraph following Eq.~(1.300)}
Fix deterministic $\beta,h,q\in\mathbb R$ with $4\beta^2<1$ and
$q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, and set
$\widehat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$.
Fix $n,k\in\mathbb N$, $x\ne y$ among the first $n$ replicas, finite sets $I_j$
and coefficients $c_{ji}\in\mathbb R$ for $1\le j\le k$.
The conclusion of Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-pair} holds for
\[
 f^-=\prod_{j=1}^k\sum_{i\in I_j}c_{ji}D^-_{a_{ji}b_{ji}},\qquad a_{ji}\ne b_{ji},
\]
with one positive error constant chosen before $N$ and all the replica labels $a_{ji},b_{ji}$.
The error is $C m^{-(k+2)/2}$, where $m=N+1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The difference between the two sides of (1.300) is a linear functional of the old-spin
observable $f^-$. Linearity of the finite Gibbs averages is valid for every observable;
the disorder integrability follows from the existing deterministic-observable Gibbs API.
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:finite-product-bound} to the monomial bound.
If $C_0$ is the monomial constant, $1+C_0\prod_j\sum_i|c_{ji}|$ is a valid positive constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Rectangular and third-replica formulas]
\label{cor:sg-tal:cavity-higher-rect-third}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_rect_lincomb_expansion,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_sub_third_lincomb_expansion,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_replica_pair_sum_rect, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_rect_lincomb_expansion_uniform,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_sub_third_lincomb_expansion_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-linear}
\talsource{Corollary~1.10.3, Eqs.~(1.301)--(1.302)}
Under the same deterministic-field, fixed-point and high-temperature assumptions, let $f^-$
be the above product of $k$ finite linear combinations with fixed coefficients.
For four distinct replica labels $1,2,3,4$ there is $C>0$, independent of $N$ and the
labels in $f^-$, such that
\[
 \left|\nu((\varepsilon_1-\varepsilon_2)(\varepsilon_3-\varepsilon_4)f^-)
 -\beta^2(1-2q+\widehat q)
   \nu((D^-_{13}-D^-_{14}-D^-_{23}+D^-_{24})f^-)\right|
 \le C m^{-(k+2)/2}.
\]
For three distinct labels $1,2,3$, assume in addition that $f^-$ is unchanged when the
third replica is replaced by any configuration. There is $C'>0$, chosen before $N$,
such that the absolute difference between $\nu((\varepsilon_1-\varepsilon_2)\varepsilon_3f^-)$
and
\[
 \beta^2(1-4q+3\widehat q)\nu((D^-_{13}-D^-_{23})f^-)
 +\beta^2(q-\widehat q)\sum_{\ell\notin\{1,2,3\}}
   \nu((D^-_{1\ell}-D^-_{2\ell}-D^-_{1,n+1}+D^-_{2,n+1})f^-)
\]
is at most $C'm^{-(k+2)/2}$. Here the finite sum is over the other original replicas.
All averages are full $m$-spin Gibbs averages; replicas are added when needed.
The rectangular conclusion is stated on the original replica space: the two auxiliary
replicas are removed by the existing Gibbs marginal theorem. The third-replica formula
still uses the local kernel $J_{ab}=\nu(f^-D^-_{ab})$ on the enlarged replica space.
Linearity gives the displayed formulas.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Subtract four applications of the pair expansion for the rectangle, and two for the
third-replica difference. The exact coincidence algebra and signed replica-family sum
from Section~1.8 apply to the symmetric full-Gibbs kernel $J$.
The new rectangular identity works for every symmetric kernel and every real coefficient
function $b$, with no probabilistic assumptions. It follows by subtracting two instances
of the existing third-replica regrouping and splitting the two filtered sums.
For the second formula, Gibbs replica replacement and the stated independence hypothesis
give $J_{i3}=J_{i,n+1}$ for $i=1,2$. The existing coefficient split then yields
$b(2)-4b(1)+3b(0)=\beta^2(1-4q+3\widehat q)$ and
$b(1)-b(0)=\beta^2(q-\widehat q)$. The sums of the four or two positive error constants
bound the respective remainders.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Replacing Gibbs means in arbitrary mixed products}

\begin{lemma}[Independent fresh pairs, factor by factor]
\label{lem:sg-tal:fresh-pair-product}
\lean{MeasureTheory.integral_pi_sum_type, MeasureTheory.integral_pi_prod_fresh_pairs}
\leanok
\talsource{Independent-replica integration underlying Eq.~(1.305)}
Let $X$ be a finite measurable space with measurable singletons and finite measure $\mu$,
and let $R,J$ be finite types. For any kernels $K_j:X^R\times X\times X\to\mathbb R$,
\[
 \int_{X^{R\sqcup(J\sqcup J)}}
   \prod_{j\in J} K_j(\sigma|_R,\sigma_{j,0},\sigma_{j,1})\,d\mu^{\otimes}
 =\int_{X^R}\prod_{j\in J}
   \left(\int_X\int_X K_j(\rho,x,y)\,d\mu(y)d\mu(x)\right)d\mu^{\otimes R}(\rho).
\]
The two copies of $J$ give disjoint fresh labels; factors may share original labels in $R$.
The block-splitting identity also holds on an arbitrary sigma-finite measurable space,
assuming the joint block observable is integrable; only the finite-space applications
use automatic integrability.
The split into two independent blocks is also exposed for an arbitrary integrand.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use the existing measure-preserving equivalence between functions on a disjoint sum
and a pair of function spaces, followed by Fubini. Split the two fresh blocks and apply
Mathlib's finite-product integral factorization twice. Finiteness supplies integrability.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Mixed-product Gibbs-mean substitution, enumerated form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:T-product-fresh}
\lean{MeasureTheory.integral_replica_centered_products, SpinGlasses.sk_mixed_T_moment_eq_fresh}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:fresh-pair-product}
\talsource{Eq.~(1.305), the three definitions of $U(v)$ preceding it}
Let $\mu$ be a probability measure on a finite measurable space $X$ with measurable
singletons, $I$ a finite coordinate type, $v:X\to\mathbb R^I$, and
$b_i=\int_Xv_i\,d\mu$. Fix $c,q\in\mathbb R$, finite original replica type $R$,
finite factor types $P,S,G$, and labels $a_p,a'_p,d_s\in R$.
For $\rho\in X^R$ put
\[
 T_p=c\sum_i(v_i(\rho_{a_p})-b_i)(v_i(\rho_{a'_p})-b_i),\quad
 T_s=c\sum_i(v_i(\rho_{d_s})-b_i)b_i,\quad T=c\sum_i b_i^2-q.
\]
Set $J=P\sqcup(S\sqcup G)$ and give each $j\in J$ a fresh pair $x_j,y_j$.
Define
\[
 U_p=c\sum_i(v_i(\rho_{a_p})-v_i(x_p))
                    (v_i(\rho_{a'_p})-v_i(y_p)),\quad
 U_s=c\sum_i(v_i(\rho_{d_s})-v_i(x_s))v_i(y_s),\quad
 U_g=c\sum_i v_i(x_g)v_i(y_g)-q.
\]
Then the exact identity
\[
 \int_{X^R}\prod_{p\in P}T_p\prod_{s\in S}T_s\,T^{|G|}\,d\mu^{\otimes R}
 =\int_{X^{R\sqcup(J\sqcup J)}}
      \prod_{p\in P}U_p\prod_{s\in S}U_s\prod_{g\in G}U_g\,d\mu^{\otimes}
\]
holds. In the SK application take $X=\{-1,1\}^{N+1}$, $v_i(x)=x_i$,
$c=(N+1)^{-1}$ and $\mu$ the Gibbs measure at the fixed disorder.
Repeated original labels and empty factor types are permitted. The SK theorem also
exports this equality after the disorder integral, with the actual disorder-dependent
Gibbs means on the left and a deterministic fresh-replica integrand on the right.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply the preceding lemma with the three displayed kernels. Each fresh-pair average
is the corresponding $T_p,T_s$, or $T$, by finite-sum linearity and the definition of $b$.
The identity holds at each disorder realization and therefore also after disorder averaging.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fresh-mean substitution on selected factor subsets]
\label{lem:sg-tal:flagged-fresh-subsets}
\lean{MeasureTheory.integral_flagged_overlap_eq,
MeasureTheory.integral_finset_prod_flagged_fresh_eq,
SpinGlasses.sk_flagged_T_moment_eq_fresh}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:fresh-pair-product}
\talsource{Eq.~(1.305), also for the products remaining after factor deletions}
Let $(X,\mu)$ be a finite probability space with measurable singletons, let $I,R,J$
be finite types, and let $v:X\to\mathbb R^I$ with mean $b_i=\int v_i\,d\mu$.
Fix $c,q\in\mathbb R$, labels $a_j,a'_j\in R$ and Boolean flags $L_j,S_j$.
For $\rho\in X^R$ define
\[
 K_j(\rho,x,y)=c\sum_i
  \begin{cases}v_i(\rho_{a_j})-v_i(x)&L_j=1,\\v_i(x)&L_j=0\end{cases}
  \begin{cases}v_i(\rho_{a'_j})-v_i(y)&S_j=1,\\v_i(y)&S_j=0\end{cases}
  -\mathbf1_{L_j=S_j=0}q.
\]
Let $T_j(\rho)$ be the same expression with $v_i(x),v_i(y)$ replaced by $b_i$.
For every $s\subseteq J$, assigning a fresh pair $x_j,y_j$ to every $j\in J$ gives
\[
 \int_{X^{R\sqcup(J\sqcup J)}}\prod_{j\in s}K_j(\rho,x_j,y_j)\,d\mu^{\otimes}
 =\int_{X^R}\prod_{j\in s}T_j(\rho)\,d\mu^{\otimes R}.
\]
The fresh family stays fixed when $s$ is replaced by an erased subset.
For the SK Gibbs measure at each disorder realization, the same identity holds after
averaging over the disorder. It needs no fixed point or high-temperature assumption.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The existing selected-family factorization reduces the claim to a double average of one
kernel. Finite-sum linearity replaces its two independent observations by their means;
the four flag cases share this one calculation. The same calculation now also supplies
the pair, single and global cases in the full-family identity, without repeating three
integral manipulations. Unused fresh replicas integrate to one.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Deviations from Talagrand in the higher-order cavity results]
\label{rem:sg-tal:dev-110-cavity}
\talsource{Section~1.10, Eqs.~(1.296)--(1.302); random-field convention after Eq.~(1.61)}
The preceding statements have the following precise relationship to the source.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Field and temperature hypotheses.}
The Lean results fix a deterministic external field $h$. Talagrand's standing convention
after (1.61) also permits i.i.d. random external fields; that extension is not supplied here.
We assume $4\beta^2<1$, which agrees with the book's high-temperature range for
nonnegative $\beta$ and also permits signed $\beta$ with $|\beta|<1/2$.
The fixed-point equation is an explicit hypothesis, not a newly defined choice of $q$.
\item \emph{Size and normalization.}
The source's number of sites is $m=N+1$ in Lean. Both $R$ and $R^-$ use denominator $m$;
the latter sums only over the first $N$ sites. The old-spin Hamiltonian retains coupling
$\beta/\sqrt m$, as in (1.144); it is not silently replaced by the $N$-site model at
the same temperature. The statements also cover $N=0$.
\item \emph{Errors and uniformity.}
The source's $O(k+1)$ and $O(k+2)$ become explicit absolute bounds
$C m^{-(k+1)/2}$ and $C m^{-(k+2)/2}$. Positive constants are chosen before $N$,
the replica labels in the product, and the bounded factor when present.
For linear combinations, the finite coefficient families are fixed before the constants;
there is no claim uniform in unbounded coefficients or coefficients depending on $N$.
\item \emph{Indices and observables.}
The pair and corollary results permit arbitrary distinguished labels rather than only
the displayed labels $1,2,3,4$. Summand index types may depend on the factor;
signed coefficients, repeated pairs, and empty sums/products are allowed.
The rectangle identity even permits its two column labels to coincide, when both sides vanish.
For (1.302), independence of the distinguished third replica is stated as invariance of
$f^-$ under replacing that replica by any configuration. This retains the source hypothesis
and also admits observables whose apparent dependence cancels algebraically.
\item \emph{Proof and representation.}
The common source notation $\nu$ is implemented using the original replica type and
an explicit type with two extra replicas. The local kernel $J_{ab}=\nu(f^-D^-_{ab})$
is an actual Gibbs integral, not an assumed covariance model.
We distribute finite linear combinations once in a generic linear-functional lemma.
The rectangular coefficient calculation subtracts the existing third-replica regroupings
instead of repeating the coincidence case enumeration of Lemma~1.8.4.
These are changes of proof organization, with the source coefficients unchanged.
\item \emph{Remaining scope.}
The moment and Taylor estimates, pair expansion and both corollary formulas now expose
the source's full class of finite products of linear combinations. The bounded factor
in the moment and Taylor estimates may have any fixed bound $M$, rather than only $4$. Equation~(1.305) is proved in the enumerated-factor form above, for a general finite
probability space and vector observable. Fresh replicas are indexed by disjoint sum types
rather than by ordered integers larger than $n$. Grouping the enumerated factors into
multi-index powers and reindexing to a single $\operatorname{Fin}(n')$ are not additional
probabilistic assumptions; their downstream application is still to be implemented.
No proof of Propositions~1.10.4--1.10.5 or Theorem~1.10.1 is asserted. The status below records these open obligations.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\subsection{The induction for the mixed $T$-moments: the last-site step}

The proof of Propositions~1.10.4--1.10.5 and Theorem~1.10.1 repeats one step: write a product
of overlap factors as a function on $n'$ replicas (Eq.~(1.305), proved above), use symmetry
between sites to move one factor to the last site (Eq.~(1.309)), and expand the remaining
factors in their last-site parts (Eqs.~(1.310)--(1.311)). The following results prove this
step once, for an arbitrary finite family of factors, each a finite linear combination
$U_j=\sum_x c_{jx}(R_{a_{jx}b_{jx}}-q)$ of centered overlaps with $a_{jx}\neq b_{jx}$.
Write $m=N+1$, $U_j^-=\sum_x c_{jx}(R^-_{a_{jx}b_{jx}}-q)$ with $R^-$ the overlap over the
first $N$ sites normalized by $m$, and $e_j=\sum_x c_{jx}\varepsilon_{a_{jx}}\varepsilon_{b_{jx}}$
with the last spins $\varepsilon$. Then $U_j=U_j^-+e_j/m$.

\begin{lemma}[Symmetry between sites, general one-site observable]
\label{lem:sg-tal:site-mean}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_replica_avg_site_mean, SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_mul_eq_last_site_of_perm}
\leanok
\talsource{Eq.~(1.309); Eq.~(1.240)}
Let $\nu$ be the $n$-replica SK average $\nu(\Phi)=\mathbb E\langle\Phi\rangle$ of the
$(N+1)$-spin system, $\xi$ a function of the $n$ spins at one site, and $H$ a function of the
replica configuration that is invariant under every permutation of the sites. Then
\[
 \nu\Big(\Big(\frac1m\sum_{s\le N}\xi(\sigma_{\cdot,s})\Big)H\Big)
 =\nu\big(\xi(\sigma_{\cdot,\mathrm{last}})\,H\big).
\]
The earlier statement for $\xi=\varepsilon_x\varepsilon_y-q$ is now a corollary.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
For each site $s$ the transposition of $s$ and the last site carries the term of site $s$
to the last site and leaves $H$ unchanged. The disorder average is invariant under
relabelling the sites (\texttt{sk\_replica\_site\_perm}). Sum over $s$ by linearity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Second moment of a product over a subset of factors]
\label{lem:sg-tal:lincomb-sq-finset}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_lincomb_sq_le_finset}
\leanok
\talsource{Eq.~(1.297)}
Let $\beta_0$ satisfy $4\beta_0^2<1$, $\kappa$ an index type, $s\subseteq\kappa$ finite, $M\in\mathbb R$,
and fix coefficient families $c_{jx}$. There is $K>0$ such that, for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$,
$q$ the fixed point, all $N$, all labels with $a_{jx}\neq b_{jx}$ for $j\in s$,
and every $W$ with $|W|\le M$,
\[
 \nu\Big(\big(W\prod_{j\in s}U^-_j\big)^2\Big)\le K/(N+1)^{|s|}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Reindex $s$ by $\{1,\dots,|s|\}$ and apply the case already proved
(Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:cavity-higher-moments} and its linear-combination form).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Absolute moments of last-site corrections]
\label{lem:sg-tal:correction-absolute-moments}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_correction_sum_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:lincomb-sq-finset, lem:sg-tal:positive-functional-square}
\talsource{Eq.~(1.310), bound on the remainder monomials; replacement before Eq.~(1.313)}
Fix $4\beta_0^2<1$, coefficient families $c_{jx}$, a bound $M$, and $d\in\mathbb N$.
There is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, the replica labels and $W$, such that
for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, deterministic $h$, fixed point $q$, system size $m=N+1$,
distinct labels within each overlap, and observable $W$ with $|W|\le M$,
\[
 \sum_{\substack{t\subseteq\kappa\\|t|\ge d}}m^{-|t|}
 \nu\left(\left|W\prod_{i\in t}e_i\prod_{j\notin t}U_j^-\right|\right)
 \le C m^{-(|\kappa|+d)/2}.
\]
The bounded factor may depend on every spin. Empty families and thresholds larger than
$|\kappa|$ are included.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Absorb $W\prod_{i\in t}e_i$ into the bounded factor in the square-moment estimate for
the remaining $|\kappa|-|t|$ overlaps. Apply Jensen to the absolute value to bound its
first moment. Each term has scale $m^{-|t|}m^{-(|\kappa|-|t|)/2}$, which is at most
$m^{-(|\kappa|+d)/2}$. Sum finitely many constants before choosing $N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Last-site expansion of a product of overlap factors]
\label{thm:sg-tal:last-site-step}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_step_identity, SpinGlasses.sk_nu_sq_le,
      SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_step_remainder_le, SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_step_expansion}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:site-mean, lem:sg-tal:correction-absolute-moments}
\talsource{Eqs.~(1.309)--(1.311) and the bound $\nu(|W|)=O(k+1)$ after them}
With the notation above, let $4\beta_0^2<1$, fix finite families of coefficients $c_{jx}$ for
$j\in\kappa$ ($|\kappa|=k'$) and a bound $M_\xi$. There is $C>0$ such that for
$\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $q$ the fixed point, all $N$, all labels with $a_{jx}\neq b_{jx}$ and
every one-site observable $\xi$ with $|\xi|\le M_\xi$:
\[
\Big|\nu\Big(\Big(\frac1m\sum_{s\le N}\xi_s\Big)\prod_{j\in\kappa}U_j\Big)
 -\nu\Big(\xi_{\mathrm{last}}\prod_jU_j^-\Big)
 -\frac1m\sum_{u\in\kappa}\nu\Big(\xi_{\mathrm{last}}\,e_u\prod_{j\ne u}U_j^-\Big)\Big|
 \le \frac{C}{m^{(k'+2)/2}} .
\]
The proof uses an exact identity: the left side equals the first two terms plus the sum over
subsets $t\subseteq\kappa$ with $|t|\ge2$ of
$m^{-|t|}\nu(\xi_{\mathrm{last}}\prod_{i\in t}e_i\prod_{j\notin t}U_j^-)$
(\texttt{sk\_overlap\_step\_identity}, from the symmetry lemma and the exact product expansion
\texttt{Finset.prod\_add\_eq\_prod\_add\_single\_add\_rest}). Each such term is bounded by
Cauchy-Schwarz (\texttt{sk\_nu\_sq\_le}) and the previous lemma, because
$\xi_{\mathrm{last}}\prod_{i\in t}e_i$ is a bounded factor, and $m^{-|t|}m^{-(k'-|t|)/2}\le m^{-(k'+2)/2}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
As described. In the source, with $k=k'+1$ factors in total, this is the error $O(k+1)$ of
Eq.~(1.310); the leading factor of the source product has been separated as $\xi$. The
choice $\xi_s=e_{0,s}-q\sum_x c_{0x}$ identifies the leading factor
$U_0$ with $m^{-1}\sum_s\xi_s$. For the pair and single factor classes the coefficient
sum is zero, so $\xi_s=e_{0,s}$; for a global factor, $\xi_s=e_{0,s}-q$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Replacing truncated products by full products]
\label{lem:sg-tal:product-truncation-comparison}
\lean{Finset.prod_add_sub_prod_eq_sum_nonempty,
SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_prod_sub_truncated_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:correction-absolute-moments}
\talsource{Replacement of $U^-(v)$ by $U(v)$ immediately before Eq.~(1.313)}
Under the same high-temperature and fixed-point assumptions, fix the finite coefficient
families and a bound $M$. There is $C>0$, chosen before $N$, the replica labels and $W$,
such that for $k$ factors and $|W|\le M$,
\[
 \nu\left(\left|W\left(\prod_j U_j-\prod_j U_j^-\right)\right|\right)
 \le C (N+1)^{-(k+1)/2}.
\]
This holds uniformly for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $4\beta_0^2<1$, all deterministic
$h$ and fixed points $q$. The factors are finite linear combinations of centered
overlaps with distinct labels within each overlap, and $W$ may depend on every spin.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Over a commutative ring the difference $\prod_j(u_j+v_j)-\prod_j u_j$ is exactly the
sum over nonempty subsets $t$ of $\prod_{i\in t}v_i\prod_{j\notin t}u_j$.
Apply this with $u_j=U_j^-$ and $v_j=e_j/(N+1)$, use the triangle inequality and
positivity of $\nu$, and apply the preceding estimate with $d=1$. The higher-order
remainder in the last-site step is the case $d=2$ of the same estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Decoupled rectangular contraction against a finite pair combination]
\label{lem:sg-tal:rectangular-linear-contraction}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_rect_lincomb}
\leanok
\talsource{Decoupled-factor computation in the proof of Eq.~(1.312)}
Let $R,I$ be finite types, $c_i\in\mathbb R$, and $a_i\ne b_i$ labels in $R$.
Let $x_1,x_2$ each differ from $y_1,y_2$, let $G$ be any observable of the old spins,
and assume $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ for deterministic $\beta,h$.
Write $a=1-2q+\widehat q$ and
\[
 D_x(u,v)=\mathbf1_{x_1=u}+\mathbf1_{x_1=v}
             -\mathbf1_{x_2=u}-\mathbf1_{x_2=v},
\]
and define $D_y$ analogously. Then
\[
 \nu_0\left(G(\varepsilon_{x_1}-\varepsilon_{x_2})
                 (\varepsilon_{y_1}-\varepsilon_{y_2})
                 \sum_{i\in I}c_i\varepsilon_{a_i}\varepsilon_{b_i}\right)
 = a\left(\sum_{i\in I}c_i D_x(a_i,b_i)D_y(a_i,b_i)\right)\nu_{\rm old}(G).
\]
Here $\widehat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ and $\nu_{\rm old}$ retains
the coupling $\beta/\sqrt{N+1}$. No high-temperature assumption is needed for this
exact identity. The fresh-factor specialization and its multiplicity form are given below.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Distribute the finite linear combination by the existing nested-Gibbs linearity theorem,
apply the rectangular monomial identity to each summand, and factor the common old-spin
average and $1-2q+\widehat q$. No quartic-moment enumeration is repeated.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fresh-factor specialization of the decoupled contraction]
\label{lem:sg-tal:fresh-factor-contraction}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_fresh_factor}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:rectangular-linear-contraction}
\talsource{The coefficient and zero computations preceding Eq.~(1.312)}
Let $R,J$ be finite types and let the replica labels be $R\sqcup(J\sqcup J)$.
Write $j^0,j^1$ for the two fresh labels of $j\in J$. Fix $x\ne y$ in $R$ and
$p\ne u$ in $J$. Put $e_0=(\varepsilon_x-\varepsilon_{p^0})
(\varepsilon_y-\varepsilon_{p^1})$ and let
\[
 e_u=(L?(\varepsilon_a-\varepsilon_{u^0}):\varepsilon_{u^0})
     (S?(\varepsilon_b-\varepsilon_{u^1}):\varepsilon_{u^1}),
\]
where $L,S$ are Boolean flags and $a\ne b$ is required only if both flags are true.
Assume $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$ and put
$\alpha=1-2q+\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$. For every old-spin observable $G$,
\[
 \nu_0(G e_0 e_u)=\alpha\,\delta_u\,\nu_{\rm old}(G),\qquad
 \delta_u=\begin{cases}
 \mathbf1_{x=a,\,y=b}+\mathbf1_{x=b,\,y=a},&L=S=\mathrm{true},\\
 0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}
\]
There is no high-temperature restriction for this exact identity. The cases
$(L,S)=(\mathrm{true},\mathrm{true})$, $(\mathrm{true},\mathrm{false})$ and
$(\mathrm{false},\mathrm{false})$ are the source pair, single and global factors.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Expand $e_u$ into at most four spin pairs, apply the preceding linear-combination
contraction, and simplify only its incidence coefficients using the disjoint sum labels.
The sixteen-term spin-moment expansion is not repeated.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative contractions and the finite pairing sum for term I]
\label{thm:sg-tal:term-I-pairing-sum}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_factor_product_compare,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_term_sum_compare}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:cavity-uniform-finite, lem:sg-tal:fresh-factor-contraction}
\talsource{First two lines of the evaluation of I preceding Eq.~(1.312)}
Fix $4\beta_0^2<1$, finite replica types, coefficient families and a finite collection
of products $G_i$ of $k$ truncated centered overlap factors. Each factor may be a finite
linear combination, with distinct labels within each constituent overlap. Fix a leading
pair $e_0$ and other factors $e_i$ with different fresh labels and coefficients $\delta_i$
as above. There is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$ and the replica labels, such that
for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$ and $q$ the fixed point, with $m=N+1$,
\[
 |\nu(G_i e_0 e_i)-\alpha\delta_i\nu(G_i)|\le C_i m^{-(k+1)/2},
\]
and, for the finite sum over $i$,
\[
 \left|\frac1m\sum_i\nu(G_i e_0 e_i)
       -\frac{\alpha}{m}\sum_i\delta_i\nu(G_i)\right|
 \le C m^{-(k+3)/2}.
\]
The products $G_i$ may use different finite subsets of the same coefficient family,
provided their factor counts are all $k$. Here $k$ counts factors after deleting the
leading and contracted factors; for a nonempty source sum the total is $k+2$. The second
estimate therefore has precisely the source error $O(k_{\rm source}+1)$. Empty sums are
included directly and do not require a subtraction of two from a smaller factor count.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The spin weight $|e_0e_i|$ is at most $16$. Use the uniform first-order comparison for
that weight and for weight $1$. The exact decoupled contraction evaluates the weighted
endpoint; the unweighted endpoint is the old-spin average. Since $|\alpha|\le1$ and
$|\delta_i|\le2$, their errors are bounded by $C_{16}+2C_1$.
Sum these errors and multiply by $1/m$, using
$m^{(k+3)/2}=m\,m^{(k+1)/2}$. No asymptotic expansion is assumed as an input.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Deleted fresh products with equal conditional factors]
\label{lem:sg-tal:fresh-deletion-equality}
\lean{Finset.prod_erase_eq_of_eq, MeasureTheory.integral_pi_finset_prod_fresh_pairs,
MeasureTheory.integral_pi_prod_fresh_pairs_erase_eq,
MeasureTheory.integral_fresh_overlap_prod_erase_eq}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:fresh-pair-product}
\talsource{Equality of the surviving expectations and counting, immediately before Eq.~(1.312)}
On a finite probability space $(X,\mu)$, let $R,J$ be finite, $s\subseteq J$, and
$K_j:X^R\times X\times X\to\mathbb R$. Fresh-pair factorization also holds for
$\prod_{j\in s}K_j$ in the full fresh replica space indexed by $J$: unused replicas
integrate to one. If $u,w\in s$ and
\[
 \int\!\int K_u(\rho,x,y)\,d\mu(x)d\mu(y)
 =\int\!\int K_w(\rho,x,y)\,d\mu(x)d\mu(y)\quad\text{for all }\rho,
\]
the expectations of the products with $u$ and $w$ respectively deleted are equal.
For the overlap kernels with Boolean flags $L_j,S_j$, it suffices that the flags agree
at $u,w$ and their original labels agree wherever the corresponding flag is true.
The vector observable, scaling and centering parameter are arbitrary.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply fresh-pair factorization to the family whose unused factors are $1$. Products
with two equal factors give the same result when either is deleted: expand both using
the product with both factors deleted. This works in any commutative monoid, including
when factors vanish, and uses no division. Apply it pointwise to the conditional averages.
The overlap-kernel specialization follows by equality of those kernels.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The multiplicity form of term I]
\label{thm:sg-tal:term-I-multiplicity}
\lean{Finset.sum_centered_pairs_eq_fresh_overlap, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_term_I_multiplicity}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:term-I-pairing-sum, lem:sg-tal:fresh-deletion-equality}
\talsource{Eq.~(1.312)}
Let $R$ be a finite linearly ordered replica type and $J$ a finite factor type with
distinguished $p\in J$. Fix Boolean flags $L_j,S_j$ with $L_p=S_p=1$.
For each $j$ with $L_j=S_j=1$, assume $a_j<b_j$ in $R$.
Fix $4\beta_0^2<1$. There is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N,a,b$, such that the
following holds for deterministic $h$, $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, and
$q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$. Put $m=N+1$ and
$\alpha=1-2q+\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$.
Use the full $m$-spin SK average $\nu$ on the replica type $R\sqcup(J\sqcup J)$;
write $j_L,j_R$ for the two fresh labels of factor $j$. Define
\[
 d_j^L(v)=\begin{cases}\sigma_v^{a_j}-\sigma_v^{j_L}&L_j=1,\\
                          \sigma_v^{j_L}&L_j=0,\end{cases}
 \qquad
 d_j^S(v)=\begin{cases}\sigma_v^{b_j}-\sigma_v^{j_R}&S_j=1,\\
                          \sigma_v^{j_R}&S_j=0,\end{cases}
\]
\[
 U_j^-=m^{-1}\sum_{v<N}d_j^L(v)d_j^S(v)
            -\mathbf1_{L_j=S_j=0}q,\qquad
 e_j=d_j^L(N)d_j^S(N),\qquad
 G_u=\prod_{j\in J\setminus\{p,u\}}U_j^-.
\]
Let $M=\{u\in J:L_u=S_u=1,\ a_u=a_p,\ b_u=b_p\}$ and choose $u_0\in J$,
requiring $u_0\in M\setminus\{p\}$ whenever that set is nonempty. Then
\[
 \left|m^{-1}\sum_{u\ne p}\nu(G_u e_p e_u)
       -\frac{\alpha}{m}(|M|-1)\nu(G_{u_0})\right|
 \le \frac{C}{m^{(|J|+1)/2}}.
\]
The leading factor belongs to $M$. Thus $|M|-1$ is exactly the number of other
matching pairs, including zero; no second matching factor is assumed to exist.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Expand each flagged overlap factor once into the four centered pair terms indexed by
$\{0,1\}^2$. The coefficient in each coordinate is $2i-1$ when its flag is true
and $i$ otherwise. Their product gives the four coefficients. The centering terms
cancel unless both flags are false. This identity is valid over any commutative ring,
for any finite coordinate type and observable, and is formalized separately from SK.
Apply the finite pairing-sum theorem to the $|J|-2$ remaining factors for each $u\ne p$.
The ordered-pair hypotheses exclude the reversed match, leaving precisely the
indicator of $u\in M\setminus\{p\}$. The deleted-product identity makes every surviving
expectation equal to $\nu(G_{u_0})$, even when a conditional factor vanishes.
Use a filtered sum and the sum of a constant to count the class. If it is empty,
the filtered sum and multiplicity both vanish. The case $|J|=1$ also has an empty
pairing sum; weakening the denominator exponent gives the stated uniform bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The pair-factor feedback and recurrence]
\label{thm:sg-tal:pair-factor-recurrence}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_pair_feedback, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_pair_recurrence,
SpinGlasses.flagged_pair_ne, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_pair_feedback_uniform,
SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_pair_recurrence_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_pair_recurrence_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{lem:sg-tal:flagged-fresh-subsets, thm:sg-tal:term-I-multiplicity, thm:sg-tal:last-site-step,
cor:sg-tal:cavity-higher-rect-third, lem:sg-tal:product-truncation-comparison}
\talsource{Application of Eq.~(1.301) immediately after Eq.~(1.312); Eq.~(1.313)}
Fix $4\beta_0^2<1$, finite $R,J$, and all the flags, ordered original pairs and
distinguished factor $p$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:term-I-multiplicity}.
The following constants work for all deterministic $\beta,h,q$ with
$\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$ and $q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$.
Let $m=N+1$ and use the notation $U_j^-,e_j,M,\alpha$ there. Put
\[
 U_j=m^{-1}\sum_{v\le N}d_j^L(v)d_j^S(v)-\mathbf1_{L_j=S_j=0}q,
 \quad V=\nu\left(\prod_{j\in J}U_j\right),\quad
 H=\nu\left(\prod_{j\ne p}U_j^-\,e_p\right).
\]
There is $C_1>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that
\[
 |H-\beta^2\alpha\,\nu(\prod_{j\in J}U_j^-)|
 \le C_1m^{-(|J|+1)/2}.
\]
Choose $u_0\in J$ in $M\setminus\{p\}$ whenever that set is nonempty.
There is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that
\[
 \left|(1-\beta^2\alpha)V-
   \frac{\alpha}{m}(|M|-1)\nu\left(\prod_{j\in J\setminus\{p,u_0\}}U_j\right)\right|
 \le C m^{-(|J|+1)/2}.
\]
The recurrence uses full factors on both sides, including when there is no other match.
The constants are uniform over the stated temperature range. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:sg-tal:flagged-fresh-subsets}, replacing both displayed fresh-product
moments by their actual Gibbs-mean T-moments gives the same recurrence. This
T-moment version is exported separately and does not assume the Gibbs means are
independent of the disorder.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Transport $R\sqcup(J\sqcup J)$ to $\mathrm{Fin}$ once by the existing Gibbs reindexing
theorem and enumerate $J\setminus\{p\}$ by its existing finite equivalence. The generic
four-term flagged-factor identity supplies the centered-overlap representation.
Apply the rectangular corollary, then transport back. Its overlap rectangle is exactly
$U_p^-$ and the remaining product has $|J|-1$ factors, proving the feedback estimate.
Combine it with the last-site expansion and the multiplicity theorem. Replace the
degree-$|J|$ truncated product by its full product, with error $Cm^{-(|J|+1)/2}$.
The deleted product has $|J|-2$ factors and hence comparison error
$Cm^{-(|J|-1)/2}$; its additional factor $m^{-1}$ restores the required error order.
When $|M|-1=0$, that entire contribution is zero and no second factor is needed.
The five errors are added explicitly; the recurrence is not an assumed input.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform induction for a two-factor moment recurrence]
\label{thm:sg-tal:finite-moment-induction}
\lean{ProbabilityTheory.exists_finset_moment_error_of_two_factor_recurrence}
\leanok
\talsource{The induction scheme of Propositions~1.10.4--1.10.5 and Theorem~1.10.1}
Let $P$ be a property on a type $J$, and let $F_\theta(N,s),G_\theta(N,s)$
be real families for finite $s\subseteq J$ and parameters $\theta$ in a prescribed domain.
Assume $|c_\theta|\le B$. For every $p\in s$ with $P(p)$ fix $w(s,p)\in\mathbb R$
and a finite $D(s,p)$ such that either $w(s,p)=0$ or $|D(s,p)|+2=|s|$.
Suppose $F=G$ on sets containing no selected factor, and
\[
 G_\theta(N,s)=\frac{w(s,p)c_\theta}{N+1}G_\theta(N,D(s,p)).
\]
Assume for each such $s,p$ there is $K_{s,p}\ge0$, chosen before $\theta,N$, with
\[
 \left|F_\theta(N,s)-\frac{w(s,p)c_\theta}{N+1}F_\theta(N,D(s,p))\right|
 \le\frac{K_{s,p}}{(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}}.
\]
Then for every finite $s$ there is $K_s>0$, chosen before $\theta,N$, with
\[
 |F_\theta(N,s)-G_\theta(N,s)|\le\frac{K_s}{(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}}.
\]
This is an abstract induction theorem. The Gaussian reference identity and the SK
specializations required for Propositions~1.10.4--1.10.5 are not asserted here.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Induct strongly on $|s|$. If no factor is selected the difference is zero.
A zero weight reduces the claim to the supplied recurrence remainder, without any
hypothesis on the deleted set. Otherwise apply the induction hypothesis to $D(s,p)$,
multiply by the bounded coefficient and $(N+1)^{-1}$, and add the remainder.
The identity $(|s|+1)/2=1+(|D(s,p)|+1)/2$ gives exactly the required error scale.
One may choose the new constant as
$K_{s,p}+|w(s,p)|\,|B|K_{D(s,p)}+1$, before $\theta,N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Gaussian class-product reference for a two-factor recurrence]
\label{thm:sg-tal:gaussian-class-reference}
\lean{ProbabilityTheory.exists_gaussian_class_moment_error}
\leanok
\talsource{The reference identity in the induction of Propositions~1.10.4--1.10.5}
Let $P$ select factors of a type $J$, let $\mathrm{label}:J\to\iota$ be a class label with $\iota$ finite,
and let $F_\theta(N,s)$ be real for finite $s\subseteq J$ and parameters $\theta$ in a prescribed
domain, with $0\le c_\theta\le B$. For every $p\in s$ with $P(p)$ and every $u\in s$ that is,
when the class of $p$ in $s$ has another member, one of those members, assume a constant
$K\ge0$, chosen before $\theta,N$, with
\[
 \Big|F_\theta(N,s)-\frac{(m-1)\,c_\theta}{N+1}F_\theta(N,(s\setminus\{p\})\setminus\{u\})\Big|
 \le\frac{K}{(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}},
\]
where $m$ is the number of selected factors of $s$ with the label of $p$. Then for every finite $s$
there is $K_s>0$, chosen before $\theta,N$, with
\[
 \Big|F_\theta(N,s)-\prod_{k\in\iota}\mathbb E\big(\sqrt{c_\theta/(N+1)}\,g\big)^{m_k(s)}
 \cdot F_\theta\big(N,\{j\in s:\neg P(j)\}\big)\Big|\le\frac{K_s}{(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}},
\]
where $g$ is a standard Gaussian and $m_k(s)$ the number of selected factors of label $k$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The Gaussian moments $f(n)=\mathbb E(sg)^n$ satisfy $f(0)=1$, $f(1)=0$ and
$f(n+2)=(n+1)s^2f(n)$ by Gaussian integration by parts. A product over classes of such moments
then satisfies the same two-factor recurrence with the multiplicity $m-1$, because deleting two
members of one class lowers only that class size by two and singleton classes vanish. The
reference $G=\prod_kf(m_k)\,F(\{\neg P\})$ agrees with $F$ on sets without selected factors and
satisfies the recurrence exactly. Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:finite-moment-induction}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition 1.10.4, enumerated form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-1104}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_T_pair_gaussian_moment_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_pair_odd_class_moment_uniform, ProbabilityTheory.integral_pow_scaled_gaussianReal_of_odd}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:gaussian-class-reference, thm:sg-tal:pair-factor-recurrence}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.4, Eq.~(1.303)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, $R$ a finite linearly ordered set of replica labels and $J$ a type of factors
$j$ carrying flags $L_j,S_j\in\{0,1\}$ and labels $a_j,b_j\in R$, with $a_j<b_j$ when both flags
are set (pair factor). Let $T_j=\frac1{N+1}\sum_iX_iY_i-q\,[L_j=S_j=0]$ be the $T$-basis variable, where
$X_i=\sigma^{a_j}_i-b_i$ if $L_j=1$ and $X_i=b_i$ otherwise, $Y_i$ likewise with $S_j$ and $b_j$, and
$b_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle$ is the actual Gibbs mean at the sampled disorder. For a finite set $s$ of
factors there is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $q$ the
fixed point of (1.74), every $N$, and $A^2=(1-2q+\hat q)/(1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q))$,
\[
 \Big|\nu\Big(\prod_{j\in s}T_j\Big)-\prod_{(x,y)\in R^2}\mathbb E\Big(\tfrac{A}{\sqrt{N+1}}g\Big)^{m_{xy}(s)}
 \cdot\nu\Big(\prod_{j\in s,\ \text{not a pair factor}}T_j\Big)\Big|\le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}},
\]
where $m_{xy}(s)$ is the number of pair factors of $s$ with labels $(x,y)$. The Gaussian moment
equals $a(m)A^m(N+1)^{-m/2}$, vanishes for odd $m$ and satisfies $a(m)=(m-1)a(m-2)$.
In particular, if some ordered pair label occurs an odd number of times among the pair factors of
$s$ (\texttt{sk\_T\_pair\_odd\_class\_moment\_uniform}, with the odd scaled Gaussian moments
\texttt{integral\_pow\_scaled\_gaussianReal\_of\_odd}), then $|\nu(\prod_{j\in s}T_j)|\le C(N+1)^{-(|s|+1)/2}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The pair recurrence (1.313) on finite factor sets is divided by
$1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)\ge3/4$ (here $0\le1-2q+\hat q\le1$ and $\beta^2<1/4$), giving the coefficient
$A^2/(N+1)$ bounded by $4/3$. Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:gaussian-class-reference} with
$P$ the pair flag and label $(a_j,b_j)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Decoupled contraction of a leading single factor]
\label{thm:sg-tal:single-contraction}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_single_monomial, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_single_lincomb, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_decoupled_fresh_single}
\leanok
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, the evaluation of $\nu_0(\varepsilon(1)\varepsilon(v))=q-\hat q$ behind (1.316)}
Let $x_1,x_2,y$ be replicas with $x_1\ne y\ne x_2$, and $(a_i,b_i)_i$ finitely many pairs with
$a_i\ne b_i$ and $y\notin\{a_i,b_i\}$. For an arbitrary observable $G$ of the old spins, the
decoupled endpoint average of
$G\,(\varepsilon_{x_1}-\varepsilon_{x_2})\,\varepsilon_y\sum_ic_i\varepsilon_{a_i}\varepsilon_{b_i}$
equals
\[
 (q-\hat q)\sum_ic_i\big(\#(\{x_1\}\cap\{a_i,b_i\})-\#(\{x_2\}\cap\{a_i,b_i\})\big)
 \cdot\nu_0(G),\qquad \hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h).
\]
Here $q$ is the fixed point of (1.74). This is the single-type analogue of the coefficient
$1-2q+\hat q$ of the rectangular factor.
For fresh flagged partner factors (the form used in the replica substitution (1.305)) the
leading factor $(\varepsilon_x-\varepsilon_{p})\varepsilon_{p'}$ against a flagged partner with
flags $(L,S)$ and labels $(a,b)$ has coefficient
$(q-\hat q)\big([L\wedge\neg S][x=a]+[S\wedge\neg L][x=b]\big)$: only a single partner
with the same label survives.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Expand $(\varepsilon_{x_1}-\varepsilon_{x_2})$; each term is a decoupled average of four last
spins, equal to $\mathbb E\tanh^{4-2c}$ with $c$ the number of coincidences. Since $y\notin\{a,b\}$ the
exponent is $4$ or $2$, and the difference of the two terms is $(q-\hat q)$ times the signed
membership. The linear combination follows from linearity of the nested Gibbs average.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative contractions and multiplicity for a leading single factor]
\label{thm:sg-tal:single-term-I}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_prod_contraction_compare, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_single_product_compare, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_single_term_sum_compare, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_single_term_I_multiplicity}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:single-contraction, thm:sg-tal:term-I-multiplicity}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, Eq.~(1.316)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$. Let $J$ be a finite set of factors with flags $(L_j,S_j)\in
\{(1,1),(1,0),(0,0)\}$ (pair, single, global), labels $a_j,b_j$ with $a_j\ne b_j$ for pairs, and let
$p$ be a single factor. With $d=q-\hat q$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, and
$\mathrm{cls}$ the set of single factors with the label of $p$, there is $C>0$, chosen before
$\beta,h,q,N$ and all labels, such that for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $q$ the fixed point of (1.74), and
$u_0\in\mathrm{cls}\setminus\{p\}$ when this set is nonempty,
\[
 \Big|\tfrac1{N+1}\sum_{u\ne p}\nu\big(U_p^{\mathrm{last}}U_u^{\mathrm{last}}\!\!\prod_{j\ne p,u}U_j^-\big)
 -\tfrac{d\,(|\mathrm{cls}|-1)}{N+1}\,\nu\Big(\prod_{j\ne p,u_0}U_j^-\Big)\Big|
 \le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(|J|+1)/2}}.
\]
Here $U_j^-$ are the truncated centered overlaps of the fresh factors and $U^{\mathrm{last}}$ the
last-site factors, as in Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:term-I-multiplicity}. The generic comparison
\texttt{sk\_cavity\_prod\_contraction\_compare} (a bounded last-site factor with an exact
decoupled contraction) is shared with the pair case.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Compare the full average with the decoupled one by Eq.~(1.298) for the product with and without
the last-site factor; the exact contraction is Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-contraction}. Sum over
partners $u$, using that errors of order $(N+1)^{-(k+1)/2}$ averaged with $(N+1)^{-1}$ have order
$(N+1)^{-(k+3)/2}$. Only singles with the label of $p$ contribute, with coefficient $d$; the matching
deleted products have equal expectation by the replica symmetry of the fresh factors.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Single-factor feedback, Eqs.~(1.317)--(1.318)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:single-feedback}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_single_feedback_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:pair-factor-recurrence, thm:sg-tal:single-term-I}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, Eqs.~(1.317), (1.318); Corollary~1.10.3, Eq.~(1.302)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, and let $J$ be a finite set of fresh factors with flags $(L_j,S_j)$ and labels
$a_j,b_j$, with $a_j\ne b_j$ for pairs, and $p$ a single factor ($L_p=1$, $S_p=0$). Let
$Q=R\sqcup(J\sqcup J)$ be the replica space, $V_j$ the truncated fresh-overlap kernels, and
$F=\prod_{j\ne p}V_j$, which does not depend on the last fresh replica $p'$ of $p$.
There is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$ and $q$ the
fixed point of (1.74),
\[
 \Big|\nu\big(F\,(\varepsilon_{a_p}-\varepsilon_{p})\varepsilon_{p'}\big)
 -\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)\,(\mathrm J_{a_p,p'}-\mathrm J_{p,p'})
 -\beta^2(q-\hat q)\sum_\ell\big(\mathrm J_{a_p,\ell}-\mathrm J_{p,\ell}-\mathrm J_{a_p,*}+\mathrm J_{p,*}\big)\Big|
 \le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(|J|+1)/2}},
\]
where $\mathrm J_{x,y}=\nu_2\big(F\,(R^-_{x,y}-q)\big)$ is computed with two extra replicas
$*$ and one more, and $\ell$ ranges over $Q\setminus\{a_p,p,p'\}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Transport $Q$ and the two extra replicas to $\mathrm{Fin}$ by the Gibbs reindexing theorem and
apply Corollary~1.10.3 (1.302) in its uniform form. The independence hypothesis holds because the
labels of the other factors are distinct from $p'$ for $j\ne p$. The sum over $\ell$ is transported
back by the equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Single-factor recurrence with the term II, Eq.~(1.320) before (1.319)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:single-recurrence}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_single_recurrence_uniform, SpinGlasses.abs_sub_integral_tanh_fourth_le_one_of_fixedPoint, SpinGlasses.abs_one_sub_four_mul_add_three_mul_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:last-site-step, thm:sg-tal:single-term-I, thm:sg-tal:single-feedback}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, Eqs.~(1.315)--(1.320)}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-term-I}, with $p$ a single factor, $\mathrm{cls}$ the
singles with the label of $p$, $u_0\in\mathrm{cls}\setminus\{p\}$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4$ and
$\mathrm{II}$ the sum over replicas of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-feedback}, there is $C>0$,
chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that
\[
 \Big|\big(1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)\big)\,\nu\Big(\prod_{j\in J}U_j\Big)
 -\frac{(q-\hat q)(|\mathrm{cls}|-1)}{N+1}\,\nu\Big(\prod_{j\ne p,u_0}U_j\Big)
 -\beta^2(q-\hat q)\,\mathrm{II}\Big|\le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(|J|+1)/2}}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Add the errors of the last-site expansion (1.310), the feedback (1.317), the multiplicity count
(1.316) and the truncated-to-full comparisons. The coefficients $|1-4q+3\hat q|\le3$ and
$|q-\hat q|\le1$ follow from $0\le\hat q\le q\le1$. The difference of the two increments
$\mathrm J_{a_p,p'}-\mathrm J_{p,p'}$ equals $\nu(\prod_jV_j)$ by marginalizing the two extra replicas.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Expansion of products of T-sums with live replicas]
\label{thm:sg-tal:live-T-expansion}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_live_T_expansion_exact, SpinGlasses.sk_live_T_expansion_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_live_T_drop_pair_terms_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_live_T_square_factor_uniform, SpinGlasses.integrable_integral_pi_count_tilted_of_bound, SpinGlasses.integral_integral_pi_count_tilted_finset_sum}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:prop-1104}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, Eqs.~(1.324) and the discussion after it}
Let $K$ be a finite set of factors, $M$ a finite set of term indices, and $T(l,s;x,y)$ the
$T$-basis variable with flags $l,s\in\{0,1\}$ and live replica labels $x,y$ (pair if both flags
are set, single if one, global if none). For coefficients $\gamma_{k,m}$ the exact identity
$\nu(\prod_k\sum_m\gamma_{k,m}T_{k,m})=\sum_{f:K\to M}\prod_k\gamma_{k,f(k)}\,\nu(\prod_kT_{k,f(k)})$
holds, and each monomial is within $C(N+1)^{-(|K|+1)/2}$ of its Gaussian reference
(Proposition~1.10.4), with $C$ chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$. Consequently:
(i) if the pair labels of different factors are pairwise distinct, all pair terms may be
dropped at the cost of $O(k+1)$; (ii) if moreover a square $T(1,1;u,v)^2$ multiplies the product
and no other pair label equals $\{u,v\}$, then the product equals $\frac{A^2}{N+1}$ times
the product with the pair terms dropped, up to $O(k+1)$, where $A^2=\alpha/(1-\beta^2\alpha)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Expand the product by distributivity; the interchange of the disorder integral with the finite
sum uses the boundedness and measurability in the disorder of the $T$-factors. Apply
Proposition~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-1104} to each monomial. A monomial with a pair factor whose label is
unique has a class of size one, whose Gaussian moment vanishes; a monomial whose only repeated
label is $\{u,v\}$, occurring twice, has the second Gaussian moment $A^2/(N+1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The increment against products of overlap polynomials]
\label{thm:sg-tal:increment-products}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_live_inc_unmatched_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_live_inc_matched_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_live_sp_two_special_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_live_sp_pair_only_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_live_sp_drop_pair_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_live_sp_square_uniform, SpinGlasses.sum_flag_T_factor_eq_overlap, SpinGlasses.sum_sign_overlap_eq_pair_T}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:live-T-expansion}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, the proof of (1.319): which terms of the expansion of $\prod U(v)$ can be matched}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and let $\mathrm{Inc}=\sum_{i_1,i_2}s_{i_1}s_{i_2}(R_{X_{i_1},Y_{i_2}}-q)$ be an increment
(signs $s_{\pm}=\pm1$) and $U_b=\sum_ic_{b,i}(R_{A_{b,i},B_{b,i}}-q)$ finitely many overlap polynomials
on live replicas, with $A_{b,i}\ne B_{b,i}$ and $X_{i_1}\ne Y_{i_2}$. (i) If the unordered pair labels of
the increment, and those of the factors with nonzero coefficient, are pairwise different across
different factors, then $|\nu(\mathrm{Inc}\prod_bU_b)|\le C(N+1)^{-(|B|+2)/2}$. (ii) If a further
factor $U_u=\sum_ic^u_i(R_{A^u_i,B^u_i}-q)$ shares exactly one pair label with $\mathrm{Inc}$ (that of
the term $i=(1,1)$, where $c^u_{(1,1)}=1$ and the labels agree) and all other pair labels are distinct,
then
\[
 \nu\Big(\mathrm{Inc}\,U_u\prod_bU_b\Big)=\frac{A^2}{N+1}\,\nu\Big(\prod_bU_b\Big)+O\big((N+1)^{-(|B|+3)/2}\big),
 \quad A^2=\frac{1-2q+\hat q}{1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)},
\]
with $C$ chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$ (here $\nu$ is the average over live replicas, with the
Gibbs mean $b$ of the sampled disorder).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
By (1.246), $R_{xy}-q=\sum_{l,s}T(l,s;x,y)$ pointwise, and the increment equals its pair form (all single and global
terms cancel because the signs sum to zero). Expand by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:live-T-expansion}. In (i)
the increment is a pair-only factor with a unique label, hence each monomial has an odd class. In (ii)
write $\mathrm{Inc}=T_1+\mathrm{Inc}'$, $U_u=T_1+Q_u$ with $T_1=T(1,1;X_{(1,1)},Y_{(1,1)})$;
then $\mathrm{Inc}\,U_u=T_1^2+\mathrm{Inc}'U_u+T_1Q_u$, the last two are $O(k+1)$ by (i)-type counting
and $T_1^2$ is handled by the squared-factor part of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:live-T-expansion}, followed by
dropping the pair terms of the remaining factors.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The hard step (1.319): evaluation of the term II]
\label{thm:sg-tal:single-II}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_single_II_eval_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_single_Y_conv_all_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_single_Xfull_unmatched_all_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_single_Xfull_matched_all_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_inc_prod_full_trunc_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_fresh_prod_full_trunc_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_fresh_prod_erase_symm}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:increment-products, thm:sg-tal:single-feedback, thm:sg-tal:single-recurrence}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, Eq.~(1.319)}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-recurrence}, all factors being single or global
($S_j=0$), let $\mathrm{II}=\sum_\ell\big(\mathrm J_{a_p,\ell}-\mathrm J_{p,\ell}-\mathrm J_{a_p,*}+\mathrm J_{p,*}\big)$
be the sum of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-feedback} and $u_0\in\mathrm{cls}\setminus\{p\}$. There is $C>0$,
chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, with
\[
 \Big|\mathrm{II}-(|\mathrm{cls}|-1)\,\frac{A^2}{N+1}\,\nu\Big(\prod_{j\ne p,u_0}V_j\Big)\Big|
 \le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(|J|+1)/2}},\qquad A^2=\frac{1-2q+\hat q}{1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Each summand is converted from the truncated increment and product to the full ones, up to $O(k+1)$,
by adding the increment as one more factor of the truncated-to-full comparison. A replica $\ell$ that is
not the last fresh replica of a single factor with the label of $p$ contributes $O(k+1)$, and each
$\ell=$ last fresh replica of such a factor $u$ contributes $\frac{A^2}{N+1}\nu(\prod_{j\ne p,u}U_j)$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:increment-products}). The $|\mathrm{cls}|-1$ matched products are equal
by replica symmetry, and are compared with the truncated product once more.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proposition 1.10.5, enumerated form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:prop-1105}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_T_single_gaussian_moment_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_single_recurrence_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_single_recurrence_finset_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_cavity_fresh_single_recurrence_final_uniform, SpinGlasses.single_coeff_facts}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:single-II, thm:sg-tal:single-recurrence, thm:sg-tal:gaussian-class-reference}
\talsource{Proposition~1.10.5, Eqs.~(1.314), (1.320)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, and let $J$ be a type of factors with flags $(L_j,S_j)$, $S_j=0$ (single if $L_j=1$,
global otherwise), and replica labels $a_j\in R$. For every finite set $s$ of factors there is $C>0$,
chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $q$ the fixed point of (1.74) and all $N$,
\[
 \Big|\nu\Big(\prod_{j\in s}T_j\Big)-\prod_{k\in R}\mathbb E\Big(\tfrac{B}{\sqrt{N+1}}\,g\Big)^{m_k(s)}
 \nu\Big(\prod_{j\in s,\ \text{global}}T_j\Big)\Big|\le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}},
\]
where $m_k(s)$ is the number of single factors of $s$ with label $k$ and
$B^2=\dfrac{(q-\hat q)(1+\beta^2A^2)}{1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)}$, $A^2=\dfrac{1-2q+\hat q}{1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The recurrence with the term II (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-recurrence}) and the evaluation (1.319)
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-II}) give (1.320) for the fresh-factor products. Eq.~(1.305)
converts it to $T$-moments, and the subtype transport to an arbitrary finite factor set. Division by
$1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)\ge3/4$ gives the recurrence with coefficient $B^2/(N+1)$, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:gaussian-class-reference} gives the Gaussian reference.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact integration of replicas not shared with the other factors]
\label{thm:sg-tal:replica-integrate-out}
\lean{MeasureTheory.integral_pi_integrate_out_replica, MeasureTheory.integral_pi_overlap_pendant, MeasureTheory.integral_pi_overlap_block, MeasureTheory.integral_pi_prod_overlap_blocks, MeasureTheory.integral_pi_centered_replica}
\leanok
\talsource{Section~1.10, the computation after Eq.~(1.328)}
Let $\mu$ be a probability measure on a finite set, $\rho=(\rho_r)_{r\in R}$ an i.i.d.\ family with law
$\mu$, and let $v:\alpha\to\mathbb R^I$ have mean $b$. If $Z(\rho)$ does not depend on the
coordinate $\rho_d$, then $\mathbb E[Z\,\Phi(\rho,\rho_d)]=\mathbb E[Z\,\mathbb E_t\Phi(\rho,t)]$ when
$\Phi$ does not depend on $\rho_d$ either. Consequently, for $x\ne d$ and $Z$ independent of $\rho_d$,
$\mathbb E[Z(R_{xd}-q)]=\mathbb E[Z(\tfrac1N\sum_i v(\rho_x)_ib_i-q)]$; if $Z$ is independent of
both $\rho_x$ and $\rho_d$ then $\mathbb E[Z(R_{xd}-q)]=\mathbb E[Z]\,T$ with
$T=\tfrac1N\sum_ib_i^2-q$; a factor that is centered in an absent replica has mean zero; and
for pairwise disjoint blocks $\{c_u,d_u\}$, $u\in W$, absent from $Z$, one has
$\mathbb E[Z\prod_{u\in W}(R_{c_ud_u}-q)]=\mathbb E[Z]\,T^{|W|}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Sum over the configurations and split the coordinate $d$ with the equivalence
\texttt{Equiv.piSplitAt}; the weights of the remaining coordinates factor out and the weights of
the split coordinate add up to one. The block formula is a finite induction over $W$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The moments $\mathbb E\,T_x^2T^j$, $\mathbb E\,T_{xy}^2T^j$ and $\mathbb E\,T_{xy}T_xT_yT^j$]
\label{thm:sg-tal:global-moments}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_T_single_square_moment_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_pair_square_moment_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_pair_two_singles_moment_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:prop-1105, thm:sg-tal:prop-1104}
\talsource{Propositions~1.10.4 and 1.10.5, applied in the proof of Theorem~1.10.1}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, $j\in\mathbb N$, $x\ne y$ replicas. There is $C>0$, chosen before
$\beta,h,q,N$, such that for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$ and the fixed point $q$,
$|\nu(T_x^2T^j)-\tfrac{B^2}{N+1}\nu(T^j)|\le C(N+1)^{-(j+3)/2}$,
$|\nu(T_{xy}^2T^j)-\tfrac{A^2}{N+1}\nu(T^j)|\le C(N+1)^{-(j+3)/2}$ and
$|\nu(T_{xy}T_xT_yT^j)|\le C(N+1)^{-(j+4)/2}$, where $\nu(T^j)=\mathbb E\,T^j$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply Proposition~1.10.5 to two single factors with the label $x$ and $j$ global factors, and
Proposition~1.10.4 to one squared pair, or to one pair with an odd class size.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The overlap against a product of blocks]
\label{thm:sg-tal:blocks-classification}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_blocks_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_blocks_untouched_exact, SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_blocks_one_touched_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_blocks_same_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_overlap_blocks_two_touched_uniform}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:replica-integrate-out, thm:sg-tal:global-moments}
\talsource{Section~1.10, the computation of $\nu((R_{\ell\ell'}-q)f)$ after Eq.~(1.328)}
Let $\{c_u,d_u\}_{u\in U}$ be pairwise disjoint blocks of replicas, $m=|U|$, and $x\ne y$ replicas.
Let $t$ be the number of members of $\{x,y\}$ lying in a block and $e=1$ if $\{x,y\}$ is a block, $0$
otherwise. Then there is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, with
\[
 \Big|\nu\Big((R_{xy}-q)\prod_{u\in U}(R_{c_ud_u}-q)\Big)-\nu(T^{m+1})-\frac{tB^2+eA^2}{N+1}\,\nu(T^{m-1})\Big|\le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(m+2)/2}}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Integrate out the replicas that occur in no other factor (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:replica-integrate-out}):
blocks not meeting $\{x,y\}$ become $T$, a partner of $x$ becomes the mean, so the average is a polynomial in $T$,
$T_x$, $T_y$, $T_{xy}$ in which all mixed terms with a replica entering once vanish exactly.
The surviving monomials are $T^{m+1}$, $T_x^2T^{m-1}$, $T_y^2T^{m-1}$, $T_{xy}^2T^{m-1}$ and
