$T_{xy}T_xT_yT^{m-2}$, evaluated by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:global-moments}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The coefficients of Eq.~(1.328)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:global-counting}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_count_plain, SpinGlasses.sk_count_touched, SpinGlasses.sk_count_blocks}
\leanok
\talsource{Section~1.10, the end of the proof of Theorem~1.10.1}
Let $x\ne y$ be replicas among $n$, let $\mathrm{pt}$ be a fixed-point free involution of the replicas
outside $\{x,y\}$, and $b:\mathbb N\to\mathbb R$. Let $W_j$ be the weights $1,-n,n(n+1)/2$ of the three
families of pairs in Eq.~(1.152) and $c_j$ the number of coincidences of the pair $j$ with $\{x,y\}$.
Then $\sum_jW_jb(c_j)=b(2)-4b(1)+3b(0)$; the same sum weighted by the number of members of the pair
outside $\{x,y\}$ equals $(n-2)(2b(1)-3b(0))$; and weighted by the indicator of $\{r,\mathrm{pt}\,r\}$
equals $\tfrac{n-2}2\,b(0)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Write $b(c)=b_0+(b_1-b_0)(u_r+u_s)+(b_2-2b_1+b_0)u_ru_s$ with $u$ the indicator of $\{x,y\}$ for a
pair of distinct replicas, sum the off-diagonal double sum, and use $\sum u=2$, $u^2=u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The recurrence for $\mathbb E\,T^k$, Eqs.~(1.325)--(1.328)]
\label{thm:sg-tal:global-recurrence}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_T_global_recurrence_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_global_recurrence_clean, SpinGlasses.sk_global_step_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_global_feedback_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_global_trunc_to_full_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_global_feedback_eval_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_global_termI_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_global_termI_term_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_global_blocks_moment}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:last-site-step, thm:sg-tal:blocks-classification, thm:sg-tal:global-counting}
\talsource{Theorem~1.10.1, Eqs.~(1.325)--(1.328)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $k\ge1$. There is $C>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that for
$\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $q$ the fixed point of (1.74) and all $N$, with $\nu_j=\mathbb E\,T^j$,
\[
 \Big|\big(1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)\big)\nu_k-\frac{k-1}{N+1}\,E_0\,\nu_{k-2}\Big|\le\frac{C}{(N+1)^{(k+1)/2}},
\]
where $E_0=(\hat q-q^2)(1+\beta^2A^2)+2\beta^2(2q+q^2-3\hat q)B^2$, with $A^2,B^2$ as in
Propositions~1.10.4 and 1.10.5.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Take $2k$ replicas in $k$ blocks and a leading block $p$. The last-site expansion with the one-site
observable $\varepsilon^x\varepsilon^y-q$ gives (1.325). The term I is $(k-1)(\hat q-q^2)\nu_{k-2}/(N+1)$
(1.326): each summand contracts $(\varepsilon^x\varepsilon^y-q)\varepsilon^a\varepsilon^b$ over four distinct
replicas to $\hat q-q^2$. The term II is the sum of Lemma~1.10.2 over the signed pair families; each
average is converted to full overlaps and classified by Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:blocks-classification},
and Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:global-counting} sums the three coefficients of (1.328).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The Gaussian law of $T$]
\label{thm:sg-tal:global-gaussian}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_T_global_gaussian_moment_uniform, SpinGlasses.global_coefficient_facts}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:global-recurrence, thm:sg-tal:gaussian-class-reference}
\talsource{Theorem~1.10.1 for the factors $T$, Eq.~(1.262)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$ and $k\ge0$. There is $K>0$, chosen before $\beta,h,q,N$, such that for
$\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$ and the fixed point $q$,
$|\mathbb E\,T^k-\mathbb E(\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{N+1}}g)^k|\le K(N+1)^{-(k+1)/2}$, where
$C^2=E_0/(1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q))$ is the solution of the relation between $C^2$, $A^2$ and $B^2$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The recurrence of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:global-recurrence} divided by $1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)\ge3/4$
is the two-factor recurrence of the Gaussian class engine with one class. The variance coefficient
is bounded by $12$ and is nonnegative: otherwise the recurrence for $k=2$ and $\mathbb E\,T^2\ge0$
would give $E_0\ge -K/\sqrt{N+1}$ for all $N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 1.10.1, enumerated form]
\label{thm:sg-tal:thm-1101}
\lean{SpinGlasses.sk_T_mixed_gaussian_moment_uniform, SpinGlasses.sk_T_single_gaussian_moment_on}
\leanok
\uses{thm:sg-tal:prop-1104, thm:sg-tal:prop-1105, thm:sg-tal:global-gaussian}
\talsource{Theorem~1.10.1, Eq.~(1.290)}
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, and let $s$ be a finite set of factors of pair, single and global type, with
replica labels, the single factors normalized as $(L,S)=(1,0)$. There is $C>0$, chosen before
$\beta,h,q,N$, such that for $\beta^2\le\beta_0^2$, $q$ the fixed point of (1.74) and all $N$,
\[
 \Big|\nu\Big(\prod_{j\in s}T_j\Big)-\prod_{\kappa}\mathbb E\Big(\tfrac{A}{\sqrt{N+1}}g\Big)^{m_\kappa}
 \prod_{k}\mathbb E\Big(\tfrac{B}{\sqrt{N+1}}g\Big)^{m_k}\;
 \mathbb E\Big(\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{N+1}}g\Big)^{m_*}\Big|\le\frac{C'}{(N+1)^{(|s|+1)/2}},
\]
where $\kappa$ runs over the ordered pairs of replica labels, $k$ over the replica labels,
and $m_\kappa,m_k,m_*$ are the numbers of pair, single and global factors in the corresponding class.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Peel the pair factors with Proposition~1.10.4, the single factors with Proposition~1.10.5 applied to the
remaining factors, and use Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:global-gaussian} for the global ones. The three
errors are combined with $|\prod_k\mathbb E(\sigma g)^{n_k}|\le\prod_k n_k!\;\sigma^{\sum n_k}$ and
$\sigma^n\le(\sqrt{12})^n(N+1)^{-n/2}$ for $\sigma^2=c_0/(N+1)$, $0\le c_0\le12$; the exponents add up
to $(|s|+1)/2$ because $|s|=m_{\rm pair}+m_{\rm single}+m_*$. The identification of $A^2,B^2,C^2$
with the covariance constants of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812} is not formalized here.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Statements of Propositions~1.10.4, 1.10.5 and Theorem~1.10.1]
\label{rem:sg-tal:overlap-clt-statements}
\talsource{Propositions~1.10.4, 1.10.5, Theorem~1.10.1, Eqs.~(1.290), (1.303), (1.314)}
These are the statements of Section~1.10; Propositions~1.10.4, 1.10.5 and Theorem~1.10.1 are proved in
enumerated form (Theorems~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-1104}, \ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-1105}, \ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1101}).
Fix $n\ge2$ and let $D_n=\{(\ell,\ell'):\ell<\ell'\le n\}\sqcup\{1,\dots,n\}\sqcup\{*\}$ index the
three kinds of variables $T_{\ell,\ell'}$, $T_\ell$, $T$. Let $V$ be a finite set of factors
with kind map $d:V\to D_n$ and put $k=|V|$ and $k_x=|d^{-1}(x)|$. This enumerated form
is equivalent to the multi-index form of (1.290): take $V$ to be the disjoint union of
$\{1,\dots,k(x)\}$ over $x\in D_n$.
Let $4\beta_0^2<1$, $h\in\mathbb R$; for $|\beta|\le\beta_0$, $q$ the fixed point
$q=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$, $\hat q=\mathbb E\tanh^4(\beta\sqrt q\,z+h)$,
and $b_i=\langle\sigma_i\rangle$ the Gibbs mean, set
\[
T_{\ell,\ell'}=\tfrac1m\sum_i(\sigma^\ell_i-b_i)(\sigma^{\ell'}_i-b_i),\quad
T_\ell=\tfrac1m\sum_i(\sigma^\ell_i-b_i)b_i,\quad T=\tfrac1m\sum_ib_i^2-q,
\]
and let $A^2,B^2,C^2$ be the quantities of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812}.
With $a(j)=\mathbb E g^j$ ($g$ standard Gaussian), $s_x=A,B,C$ for $x$ of kind pair, single, global:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Proposition 1.10.4 (1.303).} Splitting $V=V_1\sqcup V_2$ into the pair-type factors and the rest,
 there is $K$ independent of $N$ with
 $\big|\nu(\prod_{v\in V}T_{d(v)})-\prod_{x\ \mathrm{pair}}a(k_x)A^{k_x}\,\nu(\prod_{v\in V_2}T_{d(v)})\big|\le K\,m^{-(k+1)/2}$.
\item \emph{Proposition 1.10.5 (1.314).} If no factor is of pair type, the same with
 $\prod_{x\ \mathrm{single}}a(k_x)B^{k_x}\,\nu(T^{k_*})$.
\item \emph{Theorem 1.10.1 (1.290).} $\big|\nu(\prod_{v\in V}T_{d(v)})-\prod_{x\in D_n}a(k_x)s_x^{k_x}\big|\le K\,m^{-(k+1)/2}$.
\end{itemize}
Here $K$ depends on $n$, $V$, $d$, $\beta_0$, $h$ and not on $N$ or $\beta$. The nonzero
denominators $1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q)$ and $1-\beta^2(1-4q+3\hat q)$ are bounded below by $3/4$.
For the first use $1-2q+\hat q\le1$ and $\beta^2<1/4$; the second is the direct conclusion of
\texttt{three\_quarters\_le\_one\_sub\_beta\_sq\_mul}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Status of Section~1.10]\label{rem:sg-tal:overlap-clt-status}
\talsource{Section~1.10, Lemma~1.10.2, Corollary~1.10.3, Eqs.~(1.298)--(1.312), Theorem~1.10.1}
\emph{Proved:} the product estimates and expansions (1.298)--(1.299) for products of finite
linear combinations of truncated centered overlaps with an arbitrary bounded factor;
Lemma~1.10.2; both formulas of Corollary~1.10.3; the exact fresh-replica substitution (1.305)
in enumerated-factor form; symmetry between sites for a general one-site observable (1.309);
and the last-site expansion (1.310)--(1.311) with its explicit $O(k+1)$ remainder
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:last-site-step}). The absolute correction-moment estimate and
the truncated-to-full product comparison before (1.313) are also proved, as is the
linear-combination form of the exact rectangular contraction used in (1.312). Its fresh
factor coefficients, the quantitative individual contractions and finite pairing sum,
and the identity of matching deleted fresh-product expectations are proved above.
The explicit flagged-factor representation and counting now give the common-moment
multiplicity form of (1.312), Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:term-I-multiplicity}.
The disorder-averaged identity (1.305), the application of (1.301) to the explicit
fresh factors, and (1.313) uniformly in the high-temperature range are now proved in
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:pair-factor-recurrence}, including the actual T-moment form.
Selected-subset fresh identities and the generic finite moment induction are also proved.
Proposition~1.10.4 is proved in enumerated form
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-1104}) from the Gaussian class-product reference
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:gaussian-class-reference}). Its leading term is stated with the
variance $A^2=(1-2q+\hat q)/(1-\beta^2(1-2q+\hat q))$; the identification of this $A^2$ with the
limit constant of Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812} is not formalized, and the unselected factors
keep their actual moment, as in the book.
\emph{Proved toward Proposition~1.10.5:} the decoupled contraction of a leading single factor
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-contraction}).
The single-type version of the term I, Eq.~(1.316), including its multiplicity count, is proved
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-term-I}).
The single-type feedback (1.317)--(1.318) is proved
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-feedback}).
The recurrence (1.320) with the term II still explicit is proved
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-recurrence}).
The hard step (1.319) is proved (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:single-II}).
Proposition~1.10.5 is proved in enumerated form (Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:prop-1105}).
The global stage is proved: the recurrence (1.325)--(1.328) for $\mathbb E\,T^k$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:global-recurrence}), the Gaussian law of $T$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:global-gaussian}), and Theorem~1.10.1 in enumerated form
(Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1101}).
\emph{Not proved:} the identification of the constants $A^2,B^2,C^2$ with those of
Theorem~\ref{thm:sg-tal:thm-1812}; the multi-index restatement is the enumerated form with
$V$ the disjoint union of the index sets; the nearly-Gaussian Gibbs corollaries (1.291)--(1.295)
are also open.
\end{remark}
